# Reference research

## Whole-game subsystem search (2026-09-24)

Source-inspected and pinned references, fork gaps, and first read-only experiments are in [WHOLE_GAME_SYSTEM_ATLAS](reverse-engineering/WHOLE_GAME_SYSTEM_ATLAS.md). Adopt [Precision](https://github.com/ersh1/Precision/tree/9ef45e0d8d9e) for pre-physics/contact/hit and ragdoll-pose seam design, not as a multiplayer physics solution; [SSE Display Tweaks](https://github.com/SlavicPotato/SSEDisplayTweaks/tree/41668a749787) for Havok-time and menu-event observation, not for authority; [QuickLootIE](https://github.com/Ensrick/QuickLootIE/tree/b4967aa100a8) for pre-take/drop-reference transaction design, not anti-duplication; and [SkyMP](https://github.com/skyrim-multiplayer/skymp/tree/f926944b18e3) for server-owned inventory/change-form and explicit take/put patterns, not a drop-in replacement for this fork's protocol.

For presentation, adopt [moreHUD Inventory](https://github.com/ahzaab/moreHUDInventory/tree/68718eeb1698) for Scaleform item-card binding, [SKSE Menu Framework 3](https://github.com/QTR-Modding/SKSE-Menu-Framework-3/tree/ff6b9d0cc313) for blocking versus nonblocking overlay-input separation, and [Skyrim Souls RE](https://github.com/Vermunds/SkyrimSoulsRE/tree/280fe1afb6aa) for careful menu pause-count/background handling and active Sleep/Wait protection. Adopt [AudioUtil](https://github.com/crajjjj/AudioUtil/tree/d065e15cbe2d) for sound-handle playback, 3D attachment and lip-sync architecture; this does not synchronize music/voice cue decisions or output devices. Adopt [Integrated Magics](https://github.com/SkyDwemerForge/Integrated-Magics/tree/a5b9b90493ec) for distinct cast-start, interrupt, charge, animation-event and shout-equipment seams; reject any direct hook-offset reuse until the installed 1.7.104 ABI is validated. These projects have different runtime targets/licenses; none was built or installed in either game.

Reviewer A and Reviewer B independently reviewed physics, drops, ragdolls, inventory and UI read-only. Both identified disabled active-body correction, incomplete world-drop identity/pickup, bool-only death relay, and menu state gaps. Reviewer A additionally flagged negative unmatched inventory deltas, base-ID-only equipment matching, absent `IsAmmo` in equipment notify, and a subtitle `TopicFormId` relay omission; direct source checks confirmed those *code paths*, not an in-game failure. Reviewer B cautioned that current physics-body hooks and offsets remain probes. No production/runtime behavior was changed in this research pass.

The second Reviewer A/Reviewer B read-only reviews completed. Both found graph/pose transport without production pose apply, a missing rig/behavior compatibility gate, load-transition gaps, disabled melee target/hit handlers, concentration/projectile-biased spell sync, and no shout-fire/cooldown authority. Direct source review additionally confirmed Reviewer A's higher-priority finding: `RequestHealthChangeBroadcast` is relayed by the server without owner/epoch checks; the server subtracts `DeltaHealth` while local damage events are negative and receivers add the delta, so damage moves the server's cached health in the wrong direction. Server projectile and magic relays likewise do not check sender ownership. `MagicService::OnNotifySpellCast` dereferences a looked-up actor without a null guard. These are code findings, not paired-game observations. Reviewer B's statement that no native voice hook exists is **incorrect for this fork**: `Actor.cpp::HookSpeakSoundFunction` records/mutes native voice and `CharacterService` replays its filename; that still does not establish synchronized cue start, lipsync or music. No code was changed to fix these findings.

## Deeper native-tool review (2026-09-24)

- Adopt [alandtse/CommonLibSSE-NG](https://github.com/alandtse/CommonLibSSE-NG/tree/2dde70e8bdf9890bbd5e648966c7d2c24e83092f) as the preferred 1.7.104 type/address candidate source: its module code handles the 1.7 branch as AE and its ID database parses format 5. Reject the older bundled CommonLib pin as authoritative for 1.7 layouts. Our corrected read-only manifest/parser and PE unwind inspector are documented in [Engine Atlas](../Tools/EngineAtlas/README.md). Neither source labels nor unwind boundaries prove a safe call ABI.
- Adopt [Animation Motion Revolution](https://github.com/alexsylex/AnimationMotionRevolution/tree/1e296182e8bcd6e33a46b39f9018831d5f60ec33) for clip start, annotation, root-motion and character-controller observation patterns; [PapyrusExtenderSSE](https://github.com/powerof3/PapyrusExtenderSSE/tree/81e4c6b7b4912a3dea6e6be22c31c5e805f69503) for quest/object/furniture/kill event patterns; and [BehaviorDataInjector](https://github.com/max-su-2019/BehaviorDataInjector/tree/765950347c83a8b5c80582fb66d6f72f4a587e25) for behavior symbol-map injection design. Reject their hook offsets/DLLs for direct installation until the exact 1.7.104 ABI is proven.
- Adopt [skyrim-headless-mods](https://github.com/myaiexp/skyrim-headless-mods/tree/6c0842071a57d88eae30b53c3881957cfd7b853e) as an isolated-profile, named-pipe probe design reference only. Do **not** run its Linux-specific `skytest` against either live Windows `Data`/Saves tree: it mutates those locations and vendors a binary with no verified source in this review. [ItJustWorks](https://github.com/ryangubele/ItJustWorks/tree/7ce71809c6844c84c3837fb61fdb0a76bc6f1759) is a narrow player-scene watchdog reference, not a generic scene-authority solution.
- Local Reviewer A and Reviewer B independently reviewed the fork read-only. Reviewer A identified `QuestService::OnUpdate` calling `ScriptSetStage` under `ScopedQuestOverride`; source confirms that path allows the follower native stage setter, but whether stage fragments actually run and cause divergence remains a hypothesis. Reviewer B corroborated the existing scene/graph/loading probes but did not establish that causal claim. See [the manipulation map](reverse-engineering/NATIVE_MANIPULATION_SURFACES.md) for the measurement gate. No game install, live hook or gameplay fix was made in this pass.

## Overhaul tool search (2026-09-24)

- Source and license pins, actual implementation seams, rejected shortcuts and the measurement sequence are in [OVERHAUL_TOOL_WORKBENCH](reverse-engineering/OVERHAUL_TOOL_WORKBENCH.md). This pass cloned and inspected public source only; it did not install third-party binaries in Skyrim or claim a paired-game fix.
- Adopt [serde-hkx](https://github.com/SARDONYX-sard/serde-hkx/tree/3f53c0596dd916c964d7efe71fb514cc74430a51) and [HKX2Library](https://github.com/ret2end/HKX2Library/tree/5156f4f21fa04fad31ab39b07db83053941e09a5) as independent SE 64-bit HKX parser candidates. Their source contains SE header/packfile parsing; only local owned-asset cross-comparison can establish coverage of our actual graphs. [Pandora](https://github.com/Monitor221hz/Pandora-Behaviour-Engine-Plus/tree/90e412bff0a6f752e79a9560ff0aeb22d5f7c828) adds behavior-patch provenance, not native animation authority.
- Adopt [Open Animation Replacer](https://github.com/ersh1/OpenAnimationReplacer/tree/f4e7688b065175aff70aa523073857911e15aca3) as a clip-choice/condition-hook reference. Its `src/Hooks.cpp` follows clip activation, update, generation and synchronized clips, and `src/ActiveClip.cpp` evaluates replacement conditions. Our native read-only trace should attribute a selected actor's differing clip/variant at the same source tick before any broad bone writer. It does not make cart physics or dialogue/quest state authoritative.
- Adopt [Mutagen](https://github.com/Mutagen-Modding/Mutagen/tree/5f8f1adeac2c4aaba59d06779340c7aec3da24c5) as a second authored quest/scene/package parser, [nifly](https://github.com/ousnius/nifly/tree/cca0a770094bb962fb28ea1fec5ea903e68fda8e) for offline NIF attachment trees, and [FallrimTools/ReSaver](https://github.com/mdfairch/FallrimTools/tree/61bb57d895f023617cb55045a4907849fd1ff566) for read-only ESS change-form comparison. None reveals live native decision order by itself; compare against this repo's host-tick actor and scene probes.
- Use [skyrim-re-toolkit](https://github.com/ByteBard97/skyrim-re-toolkit/tree/b5039b37de51c78a04d0319fbdb4c436a339853a) for Ghidra type/symbol prior art only until exact 1.7.104 addresses and layouts are validated. Its documented live harness is AE 1.6.1170, not our runtime, and HavokStepLogger is explicitly non-working. [PapyrusProfiler](https://github.com/DennisSoemers/PapyrusProfiler/tree/a66e3800d14e00dc761b5afa414cf7a3af8d4df9) exposes a plausible bounded VM-call trace but also lacks 1.7.104 validation; do not drop its DLL into the live game.
- Rejected for direct SE graph analysis: [Skyrim Behavior Editor](https://github.com/BrannigansLaw/Skyrim-Behavior-Editor-/tree/7b7167cb18e28f435a64069acbf61eca49a07be3), whose README says its `hkxcmd` path cannot read SSE files. The newer [SageHavokEditor](https://github.com/lennart99v/SageHavokEditor/tree/e9508055afe411be50187ffe280bcdf0c88ce3a5) is SE-aware UI/debugger-client prior art, but its required game-side SKSE debugger plugin is not source-verified in that clone; no binary was installed.
- A concrete compatibility gap was found and bridged: the pinned toolkit's symbol miner rejects our installed Address Library **format 5**, whereas this fork's `Code/client/VersionDb.h::LoadV5` already documents its fixed ID-indexed RVA table. `Tools/EngineAtlas/Build-FunctionManifest.py` reuses the toolkit's CommonLib miner with a strict read-only v5 parser. The first SHA-pinned private manifest contains 15,220 candidate labels and flags 11 multi-name IDs; three synthetic parser tests pass. It is a function *candidate* map, not proof of ABI or hook safety. In particular ID `39484` resolves to RVA `0x6FF050`, close to the earlier crash at `+0x6FF05A`, but receiver state and call phase remain unverified. See the workbench for exact hashes, counts, and review disagreement.

## Wah Krah Jol source inspection (2026-09-24)

- Inspected [Wah Krah Jol at `2105bfd`](https://github.com/realfakenerd/wah-krah-jol/tree/2105bfd468d23bbddd54c74d898bfc11fd798f21) in a separate read-only research clone. Its [MIT/Apache dual license](https://github.com/realfakenerd/wah-krah-jol/blob/2105bfd468d23bbddd54c74d898bfc11fd798f21/README.md) is explicit; its legal notice requires players to supply their own game data. No game data was copied into this repository.
- Adopt as *reference*, not runtime replacement: [ESM-to-SQLite export](https://github.com/realfakenerd/wah-krah-jol/blob/2105bfd468d23bbddd54c74d898bfc11fd798f21/crates/converter/src/esm/exporter.rs) retains records/FormIDs/VMAD and [PEX conversion](https://github.com/realfakenerd/wah-krah-jol/blob/2105bfd468d23bbddd54c74d898bfc11fd798f21/crates/converter/src/script.rs) exposes a source-visible bytecode parser/CFG/IR. Its synthetic content and [Phase 2 acceptance gate](https://github.com/realfakenerd/wah-krah-jol/blob/2105bfd468d23bbddd54c74d898bfc11fd798f21/docs/roadmap/02-acceptance.md) are useful patterns for an asset-independent modsim and explicit visual sign-off.
- Reject it as an immediate 1:1 engine: [current Bevy app assembly](https://github.com/realfakenerd/wah-krah-jol/blob/2105bfd468d23bbddd54c74d898bfc11fd798f21/crates/engine/src/app.rs) centers on rendering, streaming and fixtures. [Gameplay/physics/quest/save](https://github.com/realfakenerd/wah-krah-jol/blob/2105bfd468d23bbddd54c74d898bfc11fd798f21/docs/roadmap/04-gameplay-and-physics.md) and [network replication](https://github.com/realfakenerd/wah-krah-jol/blob/2105bfd468d23bbddd54c74d898bfc11fd798f21/docs/roadmap/05-multiplatform-and-networking.md) remain roadmap items. Its Luau bridge is not evidence of Papyrus native-function or scheduling equivalence. Source inspection only; no compile, game-data conversion, or paired in-game parity test was run.
- Next narrow experiment: compare its parsed MQ101 FormIDs, aliases/VMAD fragments, and PEX control-flow against our existing xEdit/Champollion corpus, recording mismatches without importing converted Bethesda data or replacing the live Skyrim mod.

## Character-creator input trial (2026-09-23)

- [CommonLibSSE-NG RaceSexMenu](https://github.com/CharmedBaryon/CommonLibSSE-NG/blob/main/include/RE/R/RaceSexMenu.h) identifies Skyrim's native creator as a paused menu. [SKSE input structures](https://github.com/ianpatt/skse64/blob/master/skse64/GameInput.h) distinguish player controls from menu controls, so `bBlockPlayerInput` alone does not prove a menu-input failure. This remains a scoped causal trial, not a confirmed fix.
- In the paired native creator, the follower showed the finish confirmation but its rendered cursor and selection remained frozen despite a successful desktop child click. A requested native `game_snapshot` reported `controls.blocked=true`, the RaceSex menu open, a foreground 2560x1440 window, and a still-advancing world tick. The capture image is 2048x1152, so screenshot pixels are not client click coordinates; testing the scaled click still did not advance the dialog. The host had previously advanced its native confirmation with an external desktop click.
- The client now releases the follower's party-owned player-input block only while its native RaceSex menu is visible in an active, post-barrier shared-campaign epoch with the overlay inactive, then restores the block on close. Camera authority is based on party phase instead of that local input bit; CameraService already suppresses host-camera writes during RaceSex. This build still requires a fresh paired validation before calling the creator pass fixed or claiming a ready queue.
- [Microsoft's WM_NULL documentation](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/winmsg/wm-null) permits a `SendMessageTimeout` responsiveness round trip. The test-only key helper now forks a child so the game's pipe/WndProc handler can return, waits for that round trip, and rejects overlapping child actions. Reviewer A and Reviewer B independently warned that helper launch success is not game input completion, so every action must be followed by an observed native menu transition; the helper trace records whether it reached input injection.
- The fresh two-PC trial with client hash `F563497B...` reached both native creators. The follower's `controls.blocked` changed to `false` while RaceSex was open, but a correctly scaled synthetic click still left the confirmation modal open and its rendered cursor unchanged. Thus the scoped release is confirmed to change the flag, **not** to fix automation or creator progression. The current session is left at the modal for further study.
- Read-only native input-poll counters were then added. A fresh paired run with client hash `0AD8EBD3...` again reached the follower's RaceSex confirmation. At that modal, `overlayActive=false`, `directInputSuppressed=false`, and all 24,669 sampled native input-poll calls had been forwarded, with zero skipped for focus, overlay, or cooldown. After the helper queued a click, the modal remained open and all 26,221 input polls were still forwarded. This rules out the overlay and top-level native poll gate as the proximate cause. It does not establish whether the synthetic events reached DirectInput, raw input, Scaleform mouse state, or the MessageBox callback. The next low-risk diagnostic is to count DirectInput keyboard/mouse events and read native menu GFx cursor state before implementing any test-only injection. [Microsoft's DirectInput buffered-mouse documentation](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/previous-versions/windows/desktop/ee416239%28v%3Dvs.85%29) explains why client-coordinate clicks cannot simply be inserted into its relative mouse event stream.

## Cinematic actor spawn probe (2026-09-23)

- Live stage-75 capture before restarting (`authority-20260923-161250.json`) sampled both PCs one millisecond apart: 21/25 same-tick host-owned actor pose packets and 21/25 visual packets matched checksums, but 0/12 overlapping rendered skeletons/evaluated poses matched. A five-second follower-only `set_visual_pose_apply` trial (`-161600.json`) was turned off immediately; it applied **zero** bones and did not change the 83/134 sampled render-local matches. Only 1/31 tracked follower actor graph holders had a callback in the prior 200 ms, although global graph callbacks continued. Local `VisualPoseMailbox::InspectPostGraph` is invoked from the graph hook, so packet integrity does not imply visible application when that hook is absent for a remote actor. [CommonLibSSE-NG `NiAVObject::Update`](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/CharmedBaryon/CommonLibSSE-NG/main/src/RE/N/NiAVObject.cpp#L221-L226) provides a native world-transform propagation seam after local transforms are changed, but it is not yet proven safe on this runtime or callback thread. Next trial should invoke the existing lock/generation-guarded mailbox once per remote actor on the game update path *only when opt-in enabled*, then measure applied bones, callback cost, and render readback; do not enable by default until a paired visual and performance result supports it.

- Follow-up source check: [CommonLibSSE-NG `Actor::GetMount` and `IsOnMount`](https://github.com/CharmedBaryon/CommonLibSSE-NG/blob/main/src/RE/A/Actor.cpp) distinguishes a native rider-horse link from a queued package and an interaction extra. In this codebase, `Utils::GetByServerId` resolves a current entity/form, but mount queue state retains only server IDs and has no removal-time cleanup. The sustained-AI trial failed, so the next safe change is to expose *which* rider/mount server IDs are pending, their current form/3D generation, and native link; remove queue entries when either network actor is explicitly removed; and disable the ineffective remote-AI exception by default. This preserves diagnostic leverage while preventing a stale server relation from running remote AI indefinitely. A future authoritative mount presenter needs real source dismount events and a validated native seating path; this cleanup is not that presenter.
- Local server source inspection finds `OnCharacterRemoveEvent` destroys a character without clearing any other character's `MountedOnServerId` referencing it. Since `Serialize` sends that field in later spawns, a destroyed horse can be resurrected as a stale rider-to-horse relation on another client. Clear those references before destroying the horse; the existing `NotifyRemoveCharacter` tells clients to clear pending relations involving that server ID. Do not treat transient local 3D removal as a network removal: leveled-NPC reconciliation can disable/re-enable an actor without invalidating its server identity.
- [The Skyrim `Actor.psc` native API](https://github.com/Amelinyaa/SKSE/blob/master/Data/Scripts/Source/Actor.psc) documents `SetVehicle(ObjectReference)` as attach/detach for horses, carts and other vehicles; it is a potentially more direct general engine seam than `InitiateMountPackage`. This is only a Papyrus declaration, not a verified native binding or proof that it establishes a seated horse animation. Do not call it in the patch until its actual AE implementation/signature and paired effects have been inspected.
- Fresh paired build `0C163803...` showed at MQ101 stage 15 that two host riders had nonzero native `GetMount` links while the follower had **zero mount notifications** (`authority-20260923-160729.json`). The cinematic can establish mounted state without calling the hooked `InitiateMountPackage`, so event-only authority can never cover it. Following the [CommonLibSSE-NG native `GetMount` binding](https://github.com/CharmedBaryon/CommonLibSSE-NG/blob/main/src/RE/A/Actor.cpp), poll host-owned actors' actual mount relations at a bounded cadence, resolve rider/mount current server identity and ownership epochs, send changes plus periodic idempotent retries, and send an explicit zero relation after a short absence debounce. On the server, validate zero-relation requests against the rider's ownership epoch without requiring a mount entity, then publish `NotifyMount(0)`; do not reinterpret missing 3D as dismount. This is a general actor-state source path, not an intro script patch. It still does not solve follower native seating; measure that separately.
- Host-native polling build `FDAFC0E8...` passed 135 protocol assertions and ran paired. At `authority-20260923-161942.json`, host rider form `0x198BC` (server ID 32) reported native mount form 337905 (horse server ID 28). The follower already had the durable rider32→horse28 relation from its spawn snapshot, with both actors' 3D present, but native `GetMount` remained zero; 34 package rejections and zero confirmed seats. By `-162128.json`, it had 113 rejections and 17 package starts with no seat. This confirms the source relationship now reaches the server/late-spawn follower; it does **not** make the remote actor ride. Receiver lifecycle/seating remains a distinct unsolved engine problem. A zero-notification count is not contradictory because spawn snapshots carry the persistent relation.

- The user's floating heads and standing riders occur **while cinematic NPCs spawn**, not after the intro. [CommonLibSSE-NG `TESObjectREFR::Get3D`/`GetBiped`](https://github.com/alandtse/CommonLibSSE-NG/blob/ng/src/RE/T/TESObjectREFR.cpp) confirms scene 3D and biped state are distinct native surfaces; [its `Actor::GetWornArmor`/`GetMount` implementations](https://github.com/CharmedBaryon/CommonLibSSE-NG/blob/main/src/RE/A/Actor.cpp) expose equipped-state and actual mount-state checks. Local `WaitingFor3D` code currently assumes first root node means ready and then sets inventory, while mount code treats a successful package start as applied. A read-only paired spawn-window probe will compare root-node presence/children, worn-armor count, and native mount relation before changing the actor visual lifecycle. These are diagnostics, not a fix.
- Paired spawn capture `authority-20260923-154732.json` at MQ101 stage 15 found both riders mounted natively on the host and **neither** mounted natively on the follower, although the follower counted two successful mount-package initiations. Both riders had root nodes with seven children and three worn armor entries on each PC. A third NPC briefly had four host vs two follower worn armor entries; by `-154748.json` that count converged. The host/follower mount mismatch persisted. This rejects the assumption that successful `InitiateMountPackage` equals seated state and indicates a separate transient inventory/equip race. Local `HookActorProcess` returns immediately for all remote actors, preventing any remote mount package from advancing. The next narrowly scoped trial permits native actor processing only while a remote rider is awaiting a server-authoritative mount and retains the pending relation until native `GetMount` confirms seating. It may still let native AI run during that transition; paired visual/quest parity must be rechecked, and no full fix is claimed.
- The first mount-processing trial (`7045AE8D...`) reported `seated=2` without ever calling `InitiateMountPackage` on the follower, but `authority-20260923-155218.json` and `-155235.json` showed both follower `GetMount` results back at zero while the host riders remained mounted. Therefore a momentary seated state is not durable under the old remote-AI suppression. The next trial keeps native actor processing enabled for the server-reported mounted interval and retains the relation for validation. Dismount source updates are not yet implemented, so this remains an experiment rather than a general solution.
- Sustained-processing trial `D5DAFFD8...` did **not** resolve seating. At MQ101 stage 15, `authority-20260923-155600.json` showed two pending follower mount relations, two native package starts, zero confirmed seats, 109 missing-3D retries and 16 package rejections; `-155615.json` rose to 246 missing-3D retries and 46 rejections. Later source/follower snapshots both had 3D for rider server ID 6 and horse ID 8, yet only the source rider's native `GetMount` pointed to that horse. A separate stale pending relation (rider 11/mount 14) outlived its rider entity. This disproves a simple remote-AI-tick-only fix. Next investigation must correlate each pending server relation with actor generation/3D readiness, validate actual mounted state continuously, clear stale relations on removal/dismount, and inspect native package rejection reasons before shipping this path. The current deployed build is experimental and parity remains red.

## Native physics parity timing trial (2026-09-23)

- [CommonLibSSE-NG's animation graph manager holder implementation](https://github.com/CharmedBaryon/CommonLibSSE-NG/blob/main/src/RE/I/IAnimationGraphManagerHolder.cpp) resolves the native `UpdateAnimationGraphManager` call (AE ID 32899); the existing local hook around that call already observes the post-update skeleton. Code inspection found its `VisualPoseMailbox::InspectPostGraph` invocation was accidentally restricted to the one diagnostic watched holder. That makes received host pose packets inert for every other remote NPC even when the opt-in application is enabled. The next patch moves the mailbox invocation outside the watched-holder gate, while retaining the watched-only expensive checksum probe and default-off pose writes. This addresses coverage, not correctness or cadence; paired validation is required.
- Paired build `9284AF2F...` confirmed that wider hook coverage: follower mailbox inspections rose from 3,258 to 19,135 between `authority-20260923-141946.json` and `-142000.json` while both clients stayed alive and the game loop stayed near 60 Hz. The opt-in all-actor write trial (`-142024.json`) applied 805,127 bones in 10,258 frames without a reported write failure, but its last callback took 12,475 µs and its last source frame was 938 ms old. Pose writes were explicitly turned off immediately. This is not a usable presenter: it needs source-tick presentation, stale cut-off, interpolation, and a cheaper validated native write path before enabling by default. Reviewer A independently found the same watched-holder restriction and proposed a bounded presenter; its suggested lip-sync path remains untested, so only the source-verified coverage fix was adopted.
- [CommonLibSSE-NG's `BSFaceGenAnimationData`](https://github.com/CharmedBaryon/CommonLibSSE-NG/blob/main/include/RE/B/BSFaceGenAnimationData.h), [`BSFaceGenNiNode`](https://github.com/CharmedBaryon/CommonLibSSE-NG/blob/main/include/RE/B/BSFaceGenNiNode.h), and [`BSFaceGenKeyframeMultiple`](https://github.com/CharmedBaryon/CommonLibSSE-NG/blob/main/include/RE/B/BSFaceGenKeyframeMultiple.h) show a separate phoneme keyframe array on the face node. This supports a read-only paired lip-motion probe before attempting authority writes; skeletal bone packets alone cannot establish mouth parity. The runtime node offsets and line-to-face timing have not yet been validated on the installed executable.
- [CommonLibSSE-NG's `NiAVObject` source](https://github.com/CharmedBaryon/CommonLibSSE-NG/blob/main/src/RE/N/NiAVObject.cpp) exposes native transform propagation, and [Active Ragdoll's native node code](https://github.com/adamhynek/activeragdoll/blob/master/src/utils.cpp) directly writes a node's local transform before native update. The current mailbox uses one `WriteProcessMemory` call per bone in our *own process*; its measured 12.5-ms post-graph callback is unsuitable for multiple NPCs. A guarded direct in-process transform assignment under the existing graph lock is a justified opt-in performance trial, with the same graph/root generation checks, native write exception guard, and paired readback. It does not by itself establish render or physics parity. A receipt-age cutoff is also needed to stop presenting a frozen old frame while the current pose producer remains sparse.
- Paired build `02CC37A1...` exercised that opt-in path. In `authority-20260923-142639.json`, the follower applied 124,679 bones in 1,712 frames with zero write failures; the last callback took 145 µs rather than the previous 12,475 µs. Its game-loop entry gap remained ~17 ms. The 200-ms receipt-age gate skipped 9,208 old-frame applications. Shared-tick capture `-142654.json` still had 0/12 exact evaluated-pose and skeleton matches, but several NPCs' 11-12 sampled render-local bones matched while the moving roots differed 52-1,424 units. The actor stream itself only captures two poses per 100-ms movement packet. [The project's transport send path](../Code/client/Services/Generic/TransportService.cpp) allocates a 64-KiB client packet buffer, while [server movement fan-out](../Code/server/Services/CharacterService.cpp) sends on a 20-ms loop. A bounded 4-pose/50-ms producer trial stays well below the client packet buffer for typical 99-bone actors, but must be paired-tested for bandwidth, frame cost and stale skips before adopting. It is not a claim of five-player capacity.
- Paired producer build `AF56DA0F...` kept the host/follower game loops near 60 Hz and published 2,270 follower pose frames by `authority-20260923-142957.json`, but 7,986 post-graph callbacks still skipped frames outside the 200-ms receipt window. Shared-tick capture `-143011.json` remained 0/12 exact skeleton matches and 116/133 sampled render-local bone matches (compared with 123/133 in the earlier slower-stream capture, at a different scene moment). The next presenter must use the existing network presentation tick (movement is deliberately delayed 300 ms), retain multiple source-ticked host frames per actor, and interpolate bounded render-local transforms; a newest-frame raw copy is not aligned with the movement timeline. This is a source-and-live-data conclusion, not an assumption that more packets alone solve parity.
- [Upstream Skyrim Together's interpolation implementation](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/tiltedphoques/TiltedEvolution/dev/Code/client/Systems/InterpolationSystem.cpp) computes `(aTick - first.Tick)` in unsigned arithmetic, caps only the upper bound, and always loads the *second* sample's discrete animation variables and direction. If the presentation tick precedes the first point, unsigned underflow incorrectly jumps to the second; between samples, next-state animation variables arrive early. This is a universal timeline error that can explain some follower actors animating before the host independently of pose transport. The fix clamps before subtraction and applies the first discrete state until its successor's tick is due. It needs paired testing; it is not by itself a scene or ragdoll synchronization fix.
- Paired build `07F8DE21...` kept a four-sample host pose history and interpolated against the same 300-ms network presentation tick as remote movement. `authority-20260923-143515.json` recorded 7,117 interpolated frames, 530,573 applied bones, zero native write failures, only 44 unbracketed timeline misses, a 357-µs last callback, and a ~16.9-ms follower game-loop gap. Shared-tick actor capture `-143530.json` still had 0/12 exact skeleton checksums, 123/133 sampled render-local matches, and 52-1,021-unit render-root gaps. Interpolation therefore smooths available host locals but does not establish same-time cross-PC parity; the 300-ms presentation delay, root/cart authority, scene scheduling, and ragdoll bodies remain separate deficits. The opt-in pose presenter was disabled afterward.
- Paired build `267CB045...` added the source-backed unsigned-tick clamp and discrete-variable timing fix. Initial `authority-20260923-143833.json` and `-143921.json` showed both clients alive at MQ101 stage 15, matching player animation event names, and ~16-17-ms game-loop gaps; camera roots were still ~54 units apart and the parity gate was red. The snapshots are a stability check, not proof that later dialogue or graph events now align. Do not disable native graph evaluation wholesale: `AnimationSystem::Update` and Skyrim scene/script callbacks still need it. The intended follower mode is host-authored final *render pose* after native evaluation, while gameplay events and eventually ragdoll bodies receive their own host-timed authority.
- `authority-20260923-143530.json` and `-144105.json` reveal that a 12-actor "same tick" pose capture takes 144-379 ms *within each client*, despite the top-level world ticks being only 4-11 ms apart. Two actor root-world values in the first capture looked swapped relative to their root-local transforms but were coherent again in the next, consistent with observing native render updates at different phases during a long scan. This weakens any claim that 0/12 whole-skeleton exact checksums alone prove 0/12 simultaneous render mismatches. The next diagnostic narrows the heavy probe to a selectable single form ID on both PCs, records its own capture duration, and compares at a shared scheduled tick. This is measurement fidelity work, not a gameplay workaround; the real scene/root gaps remain independently visible in the lower-cost authority snapshots.
- Paired one-actor build `E603970B...` captured actor `0x000654EF` in 10.8/21.1 ms (`authority-20260923-144554.json`) and 15.9/27.0 ms with the opt-in visual presenter (`-144632.json`); the shared capture tick skew fell to 1 ms in the latter. The actor still had a 55.9-unit render-root gap while 11/12 sampled render-local bones matched. The same lower-cost reference comparison showed the follower cart `0x000B9DF3` ~55.9 units behind the host, despite the newest received host target being only 7.2 units from the host (125-ms target age). Thus the root error is largely inherited from its driven reference/physics island, not just a skeletal bone update fight. The local `GetDynamicBody` path currently rejects keyframed motion, so a kinematic trial must explicitly admit both original dynamic and keyframed state for the *selected* body while leaving host producer discovery restricted to dynamic bodies.
- [CommonLibSSE-NG's `TESObjectREFR::SetMotionType`](https://github.com/CharmedBaryon/CommonLibSSE-NG/blob/main/src/RE/T/TESObjectREFR.cpp#L820-L830) delegates to the native node method and records a Havok change; its [`NiAVObject::SetMotionType`](https://github.com/CharmedBaryon/CommonLibSSE-NG/blob/main/src/RE/N/NiAVObject.cpp#L160-L165) resolves the native entry point. [SKSE's Papyrus reference API](https://github.com/ianpatt/skse64/blob/master/scripts/vanilla/ObjectReference.psc) exposes the same motion-type operation. This makes a selected-reference, opt-in kinematic experiment a source-backed engine action; it does **not** prove that constrained cart scripts remain functional when the follower stops solving that body. The trial must verify motion-type transition, pre/post native-step target error, cart continuity, and restoration on disabling the probe before using it generally.
- Paired build `85A12C8E...` tested that selected-reference kinematic mode on follower cart `0x000B9DF3`. Before activation, `authority-20260923-145322.json` showed both bodies dynamic (motion type 3) and ~50.4 game units of reference separation. With follower motion type 4, `-145503.json` recorded 561 successful pre-step pose writes and a 1-ms source age, yet reference separation grew to ~1,578.8 units and pre/post body error remained ~1,552 units. This rejects the naive keyframe-plus-bounded-pose-write combination for constrained carts: the selected body stopped following the native cart path faster than the 20-unit/step catch-up could correct it. The probe was disabled; `-145527.json` confirmed follower motion type 3 again. This does not rule out a properly owned full-island proxy or post-scene presentation, but it is not safe as the default follower behavior.
- [CommonLibSSE-NG's `NiAVObject` update implementation](https://github.com/CharmedBaryon/CommonLibSSE-NG/blob/main/src/RE/N/NiAVObject.cpp#L1569-L1612) distinguishes downward/world-data propagation from merely assigning a local transform. The current host pose packet already carries `RootWorld` alongside bone locals, but `VisualPoseMailbox::InspectPostGraph` applies only locals. Before applying host root world or calling the native update, measure the live graph root against both the newest host root and the source-tick-interpolated host root in the existing post-graph hook. This is a read-only diagnostic to locate the missing presentation stage; a root write without child-world propagation could make render bounds, skinning, and collision disagree.
- Read-only root diagnostic build `654E9CF2...` was paired-tested on both PCs. Scheduled capture `authority-20260923-150321.json` for NPC `0x000654EF` had an exact host/follower visual packet checksum and same source tick, but a 68.25-unit rendered-root gap; 10/12 sampled render-local bones matched, while evaluated local pose errors were small (maximum 0.0635 translation units and 4.32 degrees). With that NPC explicitly selected, `-150723.json` measured a 15.56-unit local-to-latest-host root gap and a 5.96-unit gap to the interpolated 300-ms presentation target. `-150738.json` had no bracketing presentation pose (zero target-error reading and 334 cumulative timeline misses), exposing sparse four-frame history coverage. Bone playback alone cannot close the world-root gap; the next owner-authored presenter needs root/bone time alignment, child-world propagation, and a no-bracket fallback, while retaining local graph execution for script events. The selector is default-off, read-only, and pose writes remained disabled for this test.
- [Upstream Skyrim Together's interpolation source](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/tiltedphoques/TiltedEvolution/dev/Code/client/Systems/InterpolationSystem.cpp) consumes timestamped movement points at a caller-supplied presentation tick; it does not intrinsically require a fixed 300-ms delay. The current client subtracts 300 ms from remote actor movement/animation and separately from voice/subtitle presentation. On a LAN, that fixed delay itself creates same-wall-clock visual displacement and can place the pose mailbox outside its four-frame bracket. Introduce a bounded, runtime-selectable delay shared by actor and audio/subtitle presentation so paired trials can compare 300/150/80 ms without altering protocol or making an unverified lower value the default. Reduced delay may trade lag for interpolation gaps; measure both before adoption.
- User reports floating-head NPCs and riders standing on horses specifically **as actors spawn in the opening cinematic**. Reviewer A's read-only review identified a source-verified mount lifecycle gap: `OnNotifyMount` discards a one-shot notification when either remote actor is not yet present, while the server does not retain a rider-to-mount relation or send it in spawn/ownership snapshots. Reviewer B's read-only review independently found an actor-3D/equipment timing risk: `WaitingFor3D` applies inventory at first `GetNiNode`, but this does not prove body armor meshes are attached. [CommonLibSSE-NG's native `Actor::GetMount` binding](https://github.com/CharmedBaryon/CommonLibSSE-NG/blob/main/src/RE/A/Actor.cpp#L3053-L3061) provides a source-backed way to inspect actual rider/horse state; [its `GetWornArmor` binding](https://github.com/CharmedBaryon/CommonLibSSE-NG/blob/main/src/RE/A/Actor.cpp#L3259-L3289) distinguishes equipped armor state from mere scene-node presence. The mount-drop path is confirmed by local code inspection; floating-head cause remains a hypothesis pending paired inventory/mesh evidence. Do not make the visual-pose writer the scapegoat without checking its runtime flag (default off).
- The first source-side mitigation queues a received mount relation until both rider and mount have scene nodes, retries native `InitiateMountPackage` on rejection, and exposes notification/wait/application counts in the game snapshot. It compiles, but is not deployed or paired-verified yet. It addresses **receiver spawn ordering only**; the server still lacks persistent mount state, source mount events can be missed before ownership assignment, and unmounts are not represented. Those are required follow-ups before claiming general mount parity. The presentation-delay selector in the same pending build also remains default 300 ms and untested.
- Extend the server's validated mount request into persistent rider state and include that relation in spawn and assignment snapshots. This follows the existing server `Serialize`/`PopulateAssignmentResponse` paths and preserves the same ownership-epoch validation before updating state. A follower can then enqueue the relation even when it enters range after the original notification. Still missing: native source polling for mount state before ownership assignment, explicit dismount/horse change, and validation that `InitiateMountPackage` acceptance results in a seated actor. Do not claim standing-on-horse fixed from packet presence alone.
- Local `HookInitiateMountPackage` currently emits `MountEvent` **before** the original native method returns. Since both the hook and method have a boolean return, only a successful native initiation should update server-persistent relation; otherwise a failed animation/package start becomes durable false state. Move emission after the original call and gate it on success. This guards the new snapshot state but still does not prove a completed seated animation.
- Paired build `D41DBCE3...` deployed the receiver queue, persistent server mount relation in spawn/assignment snapshots, success-gated mount source hook, and default-300-ms presentation-delay selector. `TPTests` encoding factory passed 55 assertions including nonzero mount IDs. In the automated cinematic, `authority-20260923-153232.json` recorded two follower mount notifications and two successful native package initiations, no pending or rejected attempts, and both MQ101 stages at 15; a follower screenshot at 15:32:59 showed nearby bodies and the cart driver present, but does not demonstrate the user's later floating-head case or prove seated native state. The queue's actual 3D-wait branch did not fire in this run. A live 150-ms follower-only delay trial at stage 25 coincided with a 401.6-unit NPC render-root gap and 41.2-degree local-pose error (`-153448.json`); after restoring 300 ms, the later stage-40 capture had a 39.1-unit root gap (`-153539.json`). The scene stages differ, so this is not a controlled causal comparison, but the lower delay is not proven safe and was restored. Full parity remains false.
- Later follower screenshot at 15:36:08 showed intact visible bodies for nearby Helgen NPCs; it did not catch the reported transient floating-head spawn. `authority-20260923-153809.json` had matching MQ101 stage 70, follower mount diagnostic still at two notifications/two accepted packages, camera-root error 5.23 units and first-cart error 8.10 units—both improved from some earlier captures but still outside the 1-unit parity gate. All 12 protocol/unit test cases passed (130 assertions). A screenshot and native seated-state measurement at the actual failure moment are still needed to distinguish head mesh/equipment readiness from pose or scene divergence.
- [CommonLibSSE-NG's `Actor::GetMount` native binding](https://github.com/CharmedBaryon/CommonLibSSE-NG/blob/main/src/RE/A/Actor.cpp#L3053-L3061) resolves AE ID 38702 and returns the rider's actual mount through a retained `NiPointer`; its [`IsOnMount`](https://github.com/CharmedBaryon/CommonLibSSE-NG/blob/main/src/RE/A/Actor.cpp#L3653-L3658) checks interaction extra data. The current mount diagnostic counts only successful *package initiation*, not actual seated state. Add a bounded read-only snapshot of native mount form ID for network actors (releasing the returned retained pointer), then compare the source and follower during cinematic spawn. This is prerequisite to source polling, dismount, and claiming a mount fix.
- Paired camera trial build `29024932...` added a default-off position-only native post-camera-update switch, following [SmoothCam's root/child camera translation path](https://github.com/mwilsnd/SkyrimSE-SmoothCam/blob/master/SmoothCam/source/camera.cpp#L1364-L1388). With the follower input-gated and both native camera states at ID 0, `authority-20260923-141408.json` measured 43.0 units of root separation before the switch; `-141434.json` and `-141448.json` measured 7.8 and 7.9 units with it enabled. There was no game crash in the trial, but the gap remains above the 1-unit gate and the switch was turned off afterward. `authority-20260923-141537.json` captured 12 overlapping actors: all 12 graph descriptors matched, but none of their evaluated poses or render skeleton checksums did; one ragdoll checksum matched. This confirms the transport/control split remains unresolved.

- On 2026-09-23, Reviewer A independently reviewed the update path and identified a diagnostic-cache regression (transient native body failure evicted candidates; passive clutter lost fast refresh). Both were corrected before paired deployment. Reviewer B's follow-up correctly flagged one-shot tick/ordering risks but also claimed the bridge still dominated every frame; that claim was refuted by paired counters below, so its native recommendations remain hypotheses only. Neither assistant's findings substitute for source and live measurements.
- The one-shot bridge build `3E48FD54...` moved the expensive full `GameTestService` snapshot from every 100 ms to scheduled shared-tick requests. [Upstream Skyrim Together's VM hook](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/tiltedphoques/TiltedEvolution/dev/Code/client/SkyrimVM64.cpp) shows that the app update is driven by GameVM activity, so expensive per-update diagnostics directly perturb game timing. Between paired artifacts `authority-20260923-135729.json` and `-135818.json` (48.75 seconds), host/follower `World::Update` ran 2,909/2,930 times (~59.7/60.1 Hz), the debug bridge spent only 148/202 ms total in `OnGameThread`, and host physics scans and follower packet receipts were both 810. Earlier, over 94 seconds, the continuous bridge consumed ~86.7 host and ~91.1 follower seconds. This demonstrates a test-infrastructure-induced slowdown, not a Skyrim engine cap. The cart was still ~63 units apart in the first new paired capture, so smooth updates did not establish parity.
- At the wagon-exit state (MQ101 stage 75), synchronized one-shot actor capture `authority-20260923-140246.json` landed 7 ms apart. Both PCs reported the same quest stage, but 0/12 overlapping skeleton checksums, 0/12 ragdoll checksums, and 0/12 evaluated-pose checksums matched; worst sampled local rotation differed by ~109 degrees. The follower camera root was 352.1 game units from the leader, active scene phases differed (8 vs 7 and 15 vs 10), and player animation events were `OffsetBoundStandingStart` vs `moveStop`. This directly corroborates the user's observed follower wagon-exit divergence and places the problem across scene execution, evaluated animation, ragdoll, and camera, not just cart physics.
- [SmoothCam's source-backed camera-position path](https://github.com/mwilsnd/SkyrimSE-SmoothCam/blob/master/SmoothCam/source/camera.cpp#L1364-L1388) writes root local/world and child-camera world translation, and its [camera-state update hooks](https://github.com/mwilsnd/SkyrimSE-SmoothCam/blob/master/SmoothCam/source/hooks.cpp#L1599-L1619) operate after native state evaluation. Our camera authority stream already exists but pose writes are currently disabled following an earlier flip. A new read-only diagnostic records the local and last host camera state IDs, input gate, packet count, and native callback thread/cadence before any renewed write trial. No camera fix is claimed.
- [Upstream Skyrim Together's `SkyrimVM64.cpp`](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/tiltedphoques/TiltedEvolution/dev/Code/client/SkyrimVM64.cpp) invokes the multiplayer app update only from the game VM update hook when `GameVM::inactive == 0`; it is not an independent fixed-frequency timer. [Precision's pre-physics callback API](https://github.com/ersh1/Precision/blob/main/src/PrecisionAPI.h) separately identifies a native physics-step boundary. For the low packet cadence, we adopted read-only cumulative timing at the VM hook and across `World::Update` segments, retaining the existing gameplay behavior. These counters distinguish slow game-hook entry, slow multiplayer dispatcher/runner/test harness, and game-native VM time without assuming physics calls equal rendered frames.
- A read-only Reviewer A review of this workspace highlighted two cache correctness regressions: transient `GetDynamicBody` failure could evict a ref for up to a grid sweep, and passive clutter had lost per-snapshot refresh. The next build retains form-ID candidates through transient body failures, includes passive refs after discovery, and prunes only unloaded references every five seconds. This is a code-review finding, not in-game proof; paired validation follows. the implementer also noted that a physics packet mixes a collision-synchronized reference position with a separately sampled native body transform, so packet-internal phase coherence is not yet guaranteed.
- The opt-in pre-step pose-and-velocity trial in build `C41A1A8...` used the [CommonLibSSE-NG `bhkRigidBody` position/rotation setter](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/CharmedBaryon/CommonLibSSE-NG/b93280e832f263dbef44e44cbe2936622a02f91a/include/RE/B/bhkRigidBody.h) through the verified native-step seam, bounded to 8 game units and 10 degrees per step. Paired captures `authority-20260923-132107.json`, `-132130.json`, and `-132142.json` recorded 150, 495, and 660 successful pose writes, but cart separation oscillated about 104-170 game units. Body-to-reference pivot remained coherent (host 11.6375, follower 11.6568 units); one sampled native solver step increased target error from 158.96 to 168.94 units. This is a failed parity trial, not a fix. It was disabled on the follower afterward. The next diagnostic measures host scan/send and follower receive cadence plus synchronized source-tick age before changing control behavior again. [Precision's pre-physics API](https://github.com/ersh1/Precision/blob/main/src/PrecisionAPI.h) independently corroborates that pre-step callbacks are the proper timing surface, but does not address network freshness or native solver divergence.
- Paired telemetry build `466CD5AF...` showed 177 host scans and exactly 177 follower packets over 45.36 seconds, while the host's native physics step ran 531 times. Thus the server relay was not the primary packet-loss point; the 50 ms snapshot producer itself only ran at 3.9 Hz. Follower source-tick age was 103-264 ms in two samples. The [CommonLib attached-grid representation](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/CharmedBaryon/CommonLibSSE-NG/b93280e832f263dbef44e44cbe2936622a02f91a/include/RE/G/GridCellArray.h) supports our earlier adjacent-cell inclusion, but doing a full scan on every update cost 57.5 ms across 12,800 reference slots (`authority-20260923-133207.json`). Reordering the distance rejection ahead of native body reads reduced the sampled scan to 36.5-49.9 ms, but producer cadence remained only ~6.4 Hz (`-133358.json`, `-133422.json`).
- The next build `5E5E8E51...` caches moving-body form IDs (not pointers), refreshes them each snapshot, scans the current cell twice per second, and sweeps one attached grid cell per snapshot. It keeps periodic keyframes and bound checks, and resolves cached IDs anew to tolerate unloads. In paired `authority-20260923-133703.json` and `-133730.json`, scans visited 512-1,024 slots and took 4.9-6.5 ms; over 27 seconds the producer ran 208 times (~7.7 Hz), and host/follower packet counts matched within one. Selected cart packets were still about 150 ms old at receipt (`-133816.json`), and cart separation was 80-192 units across these samples. This is a measurable scan optimization, **not** 1:1 parity or a five-player performance validation. A follow-up adds stale-pose pruning; it still needs a paired run after deployment.
- Final deployed build `67265074...` adds a five-second stale unloaded-pose prune. Paired `authority-20260923-134048.json` and `-134118.json` confirm the scan still visits 512 slots in 2.1-5.6 ms and host/follower packet counters end at 266/265, with both clients alive. The selected cart was still 276.9 game units apart at the later sample, so the parity gate remains red. The producer ran 229 snapshots in about 30 seconds, still far below 20 Hz despite the scan becoming cheap; some other game-thread or global frame limiter now dominates. The next investigation should measure `UpdateEvent` cadence and other per-frame service costs, and separate rendering throughput from packet freshness before further body-control tuning.

## Reproducible multiplayer mod profiles

- [Wabbajack](https://github.com/wabbajack-tools/wabbajack)
  - Its installer reproduces a mod setup without treating a human-readable
    version or archive filename as content identity. Archive size, hash, and a
    provider-specific download state are kept separately.
  - Direction for this project: use cryptographic fingerprints as compatibility
    truth and retain provider metadata for later repair/download adapters. A
    version label is display metadata, not proof that two files match.

- [Mod Organizer 2 profile implementation](https://github.com/ModOrganizer2/modorganizer/blob/master/src/profile.cpp)
  and [plugin model](https://github.com/ModOrganizer2/modorganizer/blob/master/src/pluginlist.h)
  - MO2 separately represents enabled mod priority, plugin enabled state,
    plugin load order, archives, masters, profile INIs, and local saves. This
    confirms that an ESP/ESM/ESL list alone cannot reproduce Skyrim's deployed
    data view.
  - Direction for this project: deliver strict loaded-plugin identity first,
    then add the winning loose-file/archive tree, SKSE DLL/config inventory, and
    generated behavior/output layers. Preserve both mod priority and plugin
    load order in an isolated campaign profile.

- [Nexus Mods collection guidelines](https://help.nexusmods.com/article/115-guidelines-for-collections)
  - A collection is a metadata list; it may not bundle other authors' mod files
    without permission. Generated list-specific output is treated separately.
  - Direction for this project: the server hosts the signed campaign manifest,
    not a universal mod mirror. Downloads resolve through original providers;
    LAN/content-addressed transfer is limited to our files, generated outputs,
    and content whose redistribution permission is known.

Implemented first boundary: plugin manifest schema v1 adds SHA-256, byte size,
normal/light type, and exact load-order slot to authentication. Server-side
comparison now fails closed for missing, unexpected, altered, reordered, or
unverifiable loaded plugins. A dedicated server without plugin files can pin
the first load-order-compatible authenticated client's hashes and persist only
that manifest; it does not mirror mod content. This does not yet validate
BSA/loose assets, SKSE plugins, configuration, FOMOD provenance, or Creation
entitlements. The remaining layer contract is tracked in
`docs/MOD_PROFILE_COMPATIBILITY.md`.

## Runtime display switching and cursor behavior

- [SSE Display Tweaks](https://github.com/SlavicPotato/SSEDisplayTweaks)
  - Relevant files: `SSETweaks/window.cpp`, `SSETweaks/render.cpp`, and `SSEDisplayTweaks.ini`.
  - Its borderless-upscale path enlarges the native window while reporting a stable render client rectangle during D3D setup. This avoids treating every window-size change as a new Skyrim render resolution.
  - Its cursor lock is focus-aware: it applies `ClipCursor` on focus/activation and releases it on `WM_KILLFOCUS`, `WM_DESTROY`, or inactive `WM_ACTIVATE`.
  - Direction for this project: use a stable internal render size when toggling to borderless fullscreen and scale presentation to the monitor. Do not force Skyrim's full render-target rebuild merely to enlarge the window.

- [CommonLibSSE-NG `GViewport`](https://github.com/CharmedBaryon/CommonLibSSE-NG/blob/main/src/RE/G/GViewport.cpp)
  - Confirms Skyrim initializes Scaleform viewport `scale` and `aspectRatio` to `1.0`; changing those fields to the physical monitor aspect is not a supported fix for the stretched 3D menu model.

- [Autodesk Scaleform `Viewport`](https://help.autodesk.com/cloudhelp/ENU/Scaleform-Help/cpp_ref/06771.html)
  - Scaleform's 3D presentation also has view/projection matrices. Updating only the 2D viewport can therefore resize menu text while leaving a stale 3D projection.

- [32:9 Skyrim interface notes](https://www.nexusmods.com/skyrim/mods/98700)
  - Existing 32:9 work treats the main-menu logo separately from the Flash UI and recommends the engine settings `fUIMistMenu_CameraFOV_G` and `fUIAltLogoModel_TranslateX_G` for its camera/model placement.
  - Direction for this project: measure these values and the live aspect ratio across F11 before changing them; do not assume the main-menu model is governed by the Scaleform viewport.

- [Skyrim Together widescreen compatibility report](https://github.com/tiltedphoques/Mod-Compatibility/issues/20)
  - Records that a dedicated widescreen interface replacement is compatible with Skyrim Together Reborn. It supports using established aspect-specific UI assets rather than treating 32:9 as only a swap-chain problem.

### Current conclusion

The observed 5120x1440 overlay and correctly resized text prove that the overlay texture and 2D viewport are updating. The still-stretched main-menu model is a separate Skyrim/Scaleform 3D projection problem. The next display implementation should follow SSE Display Tweaks' stable-render-resolution borderless upscale design instead of adding unverified `GViewport` field changes.

## Installed UI and engine research

- [CommonLibSSE-NG](https://github.com/CharmedBaryon/CommonLibSSE-NG)
  - Local source snapshot: commit `b93280e832f263dbef44e44cbe2936622a02f91a`.
  - `MistMenu.h` documents the native 3D title/load-screen menu, including its
    model, camera path, shader, FOV, and rotation state.
  - `UI3DSceneManager.h/.cpp` documents the UI camera/frustum and the relocated
    `SetCameraFOV` function.
  - `BSScaleformManager.cpp` confirms that Skyrim initializes each loaded movie
    with the collected display size, safe rectangle, scale mode, and viewport.

- [SKSE64](https://github.com/ianpatt/skse64)
  - Local source snapshot: commit `25b72352adb6543fa6d0bd3795780672b2e238e0`.
  - Used as a second source for native UI/Scaleform behavior and plugin/runtime
    integration.

- [SkyUI](https://github.com/schlangster/skyui)
  - Local source snapshot: commit `835428728e2305865e220fdfc99d791434955eb1`.
  - Confirms the established ActionScript/SKSE extension surface and provides a
    source-quality reference for menu input and hot-reload development.

- [SSE Display Tweaks](https://github.com/SlavicPotato/SSEDisplayTweaks)
  - Local source snapshot: commit `41668a7497872366cb663d8c353f07e1001fa1c1`.
  - Its source identifies the exact window activation, cursor clipping,
    swap-chain resize, and borderless-upscale paths relevant to F11 behavior.

- [SKSE official builds](https://skse.silverlock.org/)
  - Confirms SKSE 2.3.1 targets Skyrim runtime 1.7.104.

- [Skyrim MCP](https://github.com/jarvann/SkryimMCM)
  - Local source snapshot: commit `82a0a80d31ae7d2254d40c129650d1e260da38a2`.
  - Demonstrates the same broad live-debug architecture we had proposed: an
    in-process native plugin, newline-delimited JSON over a Windows named pipe,
    and an out-of-process MCP server.
  - Its current UI manager only reports a fixed list of open menus. Our UI
    state recorder is substantially deeper, but its task-queue and named-pipe
    separation are useful references for the future general engine bridge.
  - The README declares MIT, although the inspected snapshot does not contain a
    standalone `LICENSE` file. Treat it as an architectural reference unless
    licensing is clarified before copying implementation code.

See `docs/UI_ARCHITECTURE.md` for the installed-asset inventory and the verified
five-layer main-menu model.

## CEF off-screen popup rendering

- [CEF `CefRenderHandler` documentation](https://cef-builds.spotifycdn.com/docs/147.0/classCefRenderHandler.html)
  - `OnPaint` distinguishes the main view from a popup widget, while
    `OnPopupShow` and `OnPopupSize` supply the popup's visibility and rectangle.
  - The current TiltedUI render handler accepts only `PET_VIEW`, so native HTML
    `select` menus are not composited into the overlay texture.
  - Direction for this project: use the existing in-page dropdown component for
    game settings now. If arbitrary web content is later required, implement
    the complete CEF OSR popup compositing contract instead of partially
    rendering popup widgets.

## Windows Snap and live window resizing

- [Microsoft: Support snap layouts for desktop apps on Windows 11](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/apps/desktop/modernize/ui/apply-snap-layout-menu)
  - Standard Win32 caption/maximize behavior supplies Win+Z Snap Layouts. Apps
    must accept the selected zone size rather than immediately enforcing a
    previously stored size.
  - Microsoft recommends supporting a minimum width no larger than 500
    effective pixels; this project accepts windowed client sizes down to 330.

- [Microsoft: DXGI overview and handling window resizing](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/direct3ddxgi/d3d10-graphics-programming-guide-dxgi)
  - `WM_SIZE` is the authoritative notification for a resized HWND. The
    application should rebuild its swap-chain buffers/render targets for the
    new client area rather than moving the HWND back to an old resolution.

- [SSE Display Tweaks `window.cpp`](https://github.com/SlavicPotato/SSEDisplayTweaks/blob/master/SSETweaks/window.cpp)
  - Uses normal Win32 window messages and only calls `SetWindowPos` for an
    explicit centering/fullscreen operation. It does not continuously enforce
    the configured dimensions after an external window resize.

Direction for this project: distinguish programmatic display-mode transitions
from external `WM_SIZE` events. A Windows Snap operation becomes the new
windowed client size, cancels any stale display preview, and is persisted rather
than reverted.

The same Snap workflow also requires returning ownership of the shared Windows
pointer while the shell UI is open:

- [Microsoft `ShowCursor`](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/api/winuser/nf-winuser-showcursor)
  - Cursor visibility uses a counter, so a single show call does not reliably
    undo a game's earlier hide calls; increment until the result is nonnegative.
- [Microsoft `ClipCursor`](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/api/winuser/nf-winuser-clipcursor)
  - Microsoft explicitly requires an application to release cursor confinement
    before relinquishing control to another application.

Direction for this project: on either Windows-key press, release capture and
clipping, normalize `ShowCursor(TRUE)`, and keep the system arrow active for the
Snap chooser. Reclaim it only when focus or a click returns to Skyrim.

## Main-menu UI3D projection refresh

- [CommonLibSSE-NG `UI3DSceneManager.cpp`](https://github.com/CharmedBaryon/CommonLibSSE-NG/blob/main/src/RE/U/UI3DSceneManager.cpp)
  - Maps the AE singleton to Address Library ID `403560` and Skyrim's supported
    `SetCameraFOV` method to ID `52742`.
  - Reapplying the live Mist Menu FOV through this method is preferable to
    manually writing `NiFrustum` fields because Skyrim recalculates the camera
    projection through its own engine path.

- [Skyrim Ultrawide RaceMenu Fix](https://github.com/theosw/SkyrimUltrawideRaceMenuFix)
  - Independently demonstrates that Skyrim menu-camera corrections must use
    the live `NiCamera` frustum/aspect rather than trusting configured INI
    resolution alone.

Direction for this project: after a renderer target rebuild or accepted Win32
resize, reapply `MistMenu::cameraFOV` using `UI3DSceneManager::SetCameraFOV` so
the title model cannot retain the preceding window's aspect projection.

## Complete live Scaleform reinitialization after resize

- [CommonLibSSE-NG `BSScaleformManager.cpp`](https://github.com/CharmedBaryon/CommonLibSSE-NG/blob/main/src/RE/B/BSScaleformManager.cpp)
  - `LoadMovie` initializes every movie from `BSGraphics::State`, then applies
    its scale mode, safe rectangle, full framebuffer viewport, advances frame
    zero, and calls `IMenu::RefreshPlatform`.
- [CommonLibSSE-NG `GFxMovieView.h`](https://github.com/CharmedBaryon/CommonLibSSE-NG/blob/main/include/RE/G/GFxMovieView.h)
  - Confirms `SetViewport`, `SetViewScaleMode`, and `NotifyMouseState` are
    per-movie state. Updating Main Menu alone cannot resize the independent
    Cursor Menu movie.
Direction for this project: a runtime resolution transition synchronizes
`BSGraphics::State`, reapplies the viewport and existing scale mode to every
live Scaleform movie, calls each changed menu's `RefreshPlatform`, and then
reapplies the Mist Menu camera FOV. This mirrors the relevant
parts of movie startup and prevents Main Menu, Cursor Menu, and UI3D from each
retaining a different prior resolution.

The extracted 1.7.104 `startmenu.swf` ActionScript and Reviewer B's consolidated
`docs/reverse-engineering/UI_SCALEFORM_MIST.md` add an important constraint:
`StartMenu::InitExtensions()` runs once, locks the menu root bottom-right and
`Logo_mc` bottom-left using `Stage.visibleRect`/`Stage.safeRect`, and has no
resize listener. A viewport update therefore cannot reproduce clean-start
widescreen placement. A live experiment that queued native Hide/Show messages
for Mist Menu and Main Menu recreated the shells but did not replay the native
callbacks that populate the menu, leaving it blank; that approach was rejected.
The selected direction is a resize entry point inside the patched SWF that
reflows the existing clips without destroying menu state. The separate
`MenuCursor` singleton (CommonLibSSE-NG ID `403551`) is also resized because
replacing only Cursor Menu's movie viewport leaves its native clamp bounds at
the preceding window size.

Implemented direction: the derived `startmenu.swf` keeps the original
`MovieClip.Lock` behavior but moves the three vanilla anchor calls into an
idempotent `SkyrimSeamlessRefreshLayout` method. A persistent frame watcher
compares the current `Stage.visibleRect` dimensions to the last applied values
and invokes that method only after a real size change. This avoids undocumented
native `GFxMovieView::Invoke` offsets and retains the populated menu instance.
The installed SWF was exported again after compilation to verify that the
watcher and reflow method survived bytecode import.

## Live options behavior

- Skyrim's extracted `quest_journal` `SystemPage.as` sends `OptionChange` on
  each slider/toggle change and reserves `SaveSettings` for persistence when
  leaving the page. This establishes the native UX target: changes are live,
  while saving is a separate operation.
- [SSE Display Tweaks configuration](https://github.com/SlavicPotato/SSEDisplayTweaks/blob/master/SSETweaks/SSEDisplayTweaks.ini)
  documents that its game-setting adjustments are in-memory rather than INI
  writes, supporting the same separation between runtime values and persisted
  launch configuration.

Direction for this project: every control updates the game's live settings
collection immediately. Non-display changes are persisted immediately as well.
Display mode, monitor, and resolution become live immediately but retain a
15-second keep/revert transaction because an unsupported mode can obscure the
confirmation UI. Confirmation persists the already-live state and must not
perform a second renderer rebuild.

Runtime testing showed that INI-collection writes alone do not drive Skyrim's
cached audio mixer. CommonLibSSE-NG documents `BGSSoundCategory`'s
`BSISoundCategory` interface at `+0x30` and virtual slot 3 as
`SetCategoryVolume(float)`, which is used for footsteps, voice, music, and
effects. Executable tracing showed that master volume is not a sound category:
native `OptionChange` case 27 updates separate live audio-manager state. The
implementation therefore invokes that verified native case for master volume
instead of treating a default-object category as equivalent.

The 1.7.104 executable's `OptionChange` callback was independently traced at
RVA `0x9AB260` (Address Library ID `53310`). Switch case 13, the Brightness
entry after the 13 gameplay entries, maps the slider through Skyrim's gamma
minimum/maximum and writes the live gamma value at RVA `0x20D0348` (Address
Library ID `388988`). The implementation writes ID `388988` explicitly after
updating `fGamma:Display`, reproducing the renderer-facing part of the native
callback without fabricating Scaleform callback arguments.

Microsoft's XAudio2 documentation establishes a separate constraint for output
device switching: the mastering voice represents the output endpoint, only one
mastering voice may exist per XAudio2 instance, and it cannot be destroyed while
source or submix voices remain. Therefore an output-device dropdown must use a
verified Skyrim audio-subsystem teardown/rebuild or process-route migration; it
must not merely store a device name and claim a live switch.

## Controller navigation for the CEF options menu

- [Microsoft: Getting Started With XInput](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/xinput/getting-started-with-xinput)
  confirms that `XInputGetState` exposes the digital D-pad, sticks, and face
  buttons directly, independent of a game's higher-level menu bindings.
- [CommonLibSSE-NG `InputMap.cpp`](https://github.com/CharmedBaryon/CommonLibSSE-NG/blob/main/src/SKSE/InputMap.cpp)
  confirms Skyrim's gamepad input is represented by the same XInput button
  masks, including the four D-pad directions.
- [Xinput-Modkey-SE](https://github.com/ike9000e/Xinput-Modkey-SE) is prior
  open-source Skyrim SE work that polls XInput and translates controller state
  into keyboard-style inputs without replacing Skyrim's normal configuration.

Direction for this project: while the modal CEF overlay owns input, poll the
first connected XInput controller and translate D-pad/stick navigation plus
A/B into CEF keyboard events. This avoids depending on Skyrim forwarding input
to a non-Scaleform browser. Dropdowns are made keyboard-focusable and handle
left/right plus confirm explicitly; visible focus outlines make controller
position unambiguous. Skyrim's own device poll is suppressed for the lifetime
of the modal overlay so the translated press cannot also activate the native
main menu underneath it. Win32 focus loss, rather than the Windows-key event,
is the authoritative signal for releasing cursor clipping and restoring the
visible desktop cursor.

Follow-up runtime testing established that device ownership must be dynamic,
not controller-exclusive. D-pad up/down are delivered as arrow keys: the
settings panel uses them for focus movement, while an open dropdown consumes
them to move its highlighted option and A confirms it. Physical raw-mouse
movement immediately restores the browser pointer, and subsequent controller
navigation hides it again. `WM_ACTIVATE/WA_INACTIVE` is included in desktop
cursor release because opening the Windows shell can deactivate Skyrim without
delivering `WM_KILLFOCUS` or `WM_ACTIVATEAPP(FALSE)` first.

The final Win32 correction treats `GetForegroundWindow() != SkyrimHwnd` as
authoritative during `WM_SETCURSOR` and returns `TRUE` through the outer
renderer hook. Returning zero after setting the arrow allowed Windows/Skyrim
to treat the message as unhandled and hide it again. An automated test now
deactivates the real game HWND, moves the pointer over its client area, and
confirms `CURSOR_SHOWING` with the standard arrow handle.

## Unattended in-game regression harness

- [AutoTest](https://github.com/Muriel-Salvan/AutoTest) is prior open-source
  Bethesda-game work that runs configured suites inside Skyrim SE, records
  status in JSON/logs, captures screenshots, and can exit the game when the
  run completes. Its Papyrus/location focus is not sufficient for renderer and
  native UI assertions, but its unattended run/status/artifact model is useful.
- [Papyrus Debug Server](https://github.com/joelday/papyrus-debug-server)
  exposes Skyrim/Fallout Papyrus debugging through WebSocket and the Debug
  Adapter Protocol. It is useful when a failure is inside script execution,
  but it cannot explain native renderer, actor-process, network-authority, or
  world-state divergence by itself. Keep it as a later script-level adapter;
  the client bridge remains the source for native and multiplayer state.
- [skyrim-headless-mods / skytest](https://github.com/myaiexp/skyrim-headless-mods)
  demonstrates a detached, drivable Skyrim session with screenshots, injected
  input, engine-state polling, a runtime probe, and replayable `.steps` files.
  Its current Linux/gamescope launcher is not directly portable to this Windows
  two-PC setup, but its command/trace probe and action-plus-observable-gate
  model are adopted here as native JSON snapshots, transition timelines, and
  semantic leader/follower comparisons.
- [TiltedConnect's own transport test](https://github.com/tiltedphoques/TiltedEvolution/blob/dev/Libraries/TiltedConnect/Code/tests/src/connect.cpp)
  proves that the production client/server transport can run without Skyrim,
  but its upstream test loops forever and exchanges only an arbitrary string.
  Direction for this project: use the same real transport with production STR
  authentication/message codecs, bounded timeouts, structured JSON results,
  and two clients asserting party and authority behavior.
- [Steam Matchmaking](https://partner.steamgames.com/doc/api/ISteamMatchmaking)
  documents that lobby membership and connection to the advertised game server
  are separate actions. Protocol tests therefore cover authentication/party
  convergence independently; Steam lobby invitation/relay coverage remains an
  integration tier requiring Steam clients and distinct accounts.
- [Microsoft Windows UI automation guidance](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-nz/windows/apps/dev-tools/winapp-cli/ui-automation)
  explicitly requires an unlocked interactive desktop for OS input injection
  and warns that successful injection does not prove the target received the
  input; screenshots or inspected state must verify the effect.
- [ViGEmBus](https://github.com/ViGEm/ViGEmBus) documents automated game input
  replay as a virtual-controller use case. The upstream project is retired and
  a kernel driver adds installation risk, so it is deferred until tests need
  to validate Windows' complete XInput discovery path.

Direction for this project: do not call the Skyrim client truly headless.
Run it unattended on an unlocked, GPU-backed interactive desktop. Add an
in-process newline-delimited JSON test endpoint and an external runner that
records every command, structured native/CEF state, screenshot, timing, log,
and crash artifact. Inject controller actions directly at the mod's controller
translation seam first; reserve a virtual XInput device for a smaller
end-to-end hardware-discovery suite. Each action must be followed by an
observable assertion rather than treating successful input injection as a
passing test.

Implemented: `Tools/InGameTests/Run-InGameTests.ps1` combines the native
local-only named pipe with CEF's existing localhost DevTools endpoint. The
first exercised suite records native/DOM state, full-game and CEF screenshots,
and an event trace while testing Options, dropdown navigation, device handoff,
desktop cursor behavior, and a two-way display-mode transition. Its first runs
found a deterministic framed-window growth bug: renderer outer dimensions were
being restored as client dimensions. Windowed `ReadSettings` now uses
`GetClientRect`, and the exercised suite passes with a five-second delayed
renderer/process health check.

The native bridge protocol now also samples game-thread state at 10 Hz rather
than scanning Skyrim's quest collection every rendered frame. It reports the
local actor lifecycle, cell/worldspace, package, controls, camera, open menus,
party identity, watched quest stages, and a bounded quest-event journal.
`Tools/diagnostics-mcp` can capture a correlated screenshot/state/server/error
issue bundle, record a transition timeline, and semantically compare leader and
follower snapshots. These probes are observational; arbitrary console or code
execution is intentionally not exposed through MCP.

## Seamless co-op interaction and synchronization target

- [Elden Ring Seamless Co-op project page](https://www.nexusmods.com/eldenring/mods/510)
  - Establishes the experience target: one persistent session through death and
    bosses, synchronized NPC dialogue/talk events and map state, shared world
    reset when resting, Steam matchmaking, and host-world catch-up for joining
    clients.
  - It also documents an important design split: some state is deliberately
    instanced per player, such as shop stock, while world progression is synced.
  - Direction for this project: copy the low-friction session semantics, not
    undocumented Elden Ring internals. Skyrim requires its own explicit rules
    for leader-owned NPC AI, dialogue leases, door barriers, quest transactions,
    item instance identity, and save projection.

- [TiltedEvolution](https://github.com/tiltedphoques/TiltedEvolution), pinned
  locally at upstream `dev` commit
  `fbf72883015dba9e26bd1539d3af9d33fa794116` (GPLv3, with separately noted
  components in its `LICENSE`).
  - Relevant source includes `CharacterService` actor ownership epochs,
    `QuestService`, dialogue/subtitle messages, player respawn, inventory/object
    synchronization, and animation graph experiments.
  - Direction for this project: retain useful protocol and reverse-engineering
    work, but replace opportunistic per-actor ownership and reflected quest
    events with explicit leader authority and session transactions where the
    product contract requires one shared result.

- [Reborn quest-progression rework](https://github.com/tiltedphoques/TiltedEvolution/pull/848)
  - Documents that Skyrim may deliver quest progression events asynchronously,
    after a scoped suppression guard has expired. Mirrored clients therefore
    echo quest updates and can execute stages repeatedly.
  - Its server-side short-lived deduplication and scene tracking are valuable
    evidence, but timing-window deduplication is not a durable transaction ID.
    The project direction is a leader-issued quest transaction with persistent
    idempotency identity and a snapshot recovery path.

- [SkyMP](https://github.com/skyrim-multiplayer/skymp), pinned locally at
  commit `f926944b18e3aed4bc3864ce668626c05ec2545f`.
  - Its server-side `WorldState` and `MpChangeForm` provide prior art for
    persistent authoritative references, inventories, actor death, timers,
    disabled/deleted state, and respawn.
  - It is not a drop-in campaign solution: the inspected `PapyrusQuest`
    implementation reports current quest-stage lookup as unimplemented, and
    its inventory source notes incomplete ExtraData parsing. Adopt the
    persistent-world pattern while closing those gaps explicitly.

The resulting product and acceptance contract is recorded in
`docs/SEAMLESS_COOP_EXPERIENCE_SPEC.md`.

## Cooperative bleedout and revival

- [Acheron-NG](https://github.com/Acook1e/Acheron-NG), inspected locally at
  commit `59f744634fa5df9d645ce0e337ed94ab89297dbb` (Apache-2.0).
