  - `src/Acheron/Hooks/Hooks.cpp` demonstrates intercepting lethal direct,
    magic-effect, damage-over-time, fall, and physics damage before ordinary
    death is committed.
  - `src/Acheron/Defeat.cpp` demonstrates a guarded defeat/rescue lifecycle:
    set no-auto-recovery, play Skyrim's `BleedoutStart`/`BleedoutStop` idles,
    hold health at a small positive floor, pacify the actor, disable relevant
    player controls, and use atomic state transitions to reject duplicate
    rescue attempts.
  - Direction for this project: reuse the verified Skyrim seams and lifecycle
    lessons, but make eligibility, revive leases, timers, state transitions,
    inventory retention, and recovery server-authoritative. Do not adopt its
    single-player global state directly.

- Current TiltedEvolution `PlayerService::RunRespawnUpdates` watches
  `IsBleedingOut`, starts a five-second deadline, forces health to zero when
  necessary, calls `RespawnPlayer`, and then sends `PlayerRespawnRequest`.
  The server optionally removes gold and broadcasts `NotifyRespawn` because
  remote body state can otherwise remain corrupted.
  - This is useful confirmation that Skyrim's bleedout state is already the
    available interception point, but the local client currently decides and
    executes respawn before the server. Our target reverses that authority:
  lethal damage requests a server transition, and only a committed down,
  revive, death, or respawn message may change the player lifecycle.

## Vanilla quest and Helgen analysis

- [Champollion](https://github.com/Orvid/Champollion), official source checked
  out locally at tag `v1.1.2`, commit
  `fd3798cd9672c2968cbd81660641f7cbbadb13fb` (LGPL-3.0).
  - It is the open-source PEX-to-Papyrus decompiler used to build a private,
    local semantic corpus from the user's installed Skyrim scripts. Its stated
    goal is functionally equivalent PSC reconstruction, but decompiler output is
    still treated as analysis evidence and correlated against plugin records and
    runtime behavior before hooks are selected.
  - The installed `Skyrim - Misc.bsa` contains 14,026 PEX files. All were
    extracted and decompiled outside the repository; 50 MQ101-related scripts
    form the first focused review set. Bethesda source text is not redistributed.

- [xEdit](https://github.com/TES5Edit/TES5Edit) and its
  [QUST record definition](https://github.com/TES5Edit/meta/blob/master/UESPWiki/QUSTDef.wiki).
  - The QUST structure provides the missing binding between stage INDX entries,
    objectives, aliases, and VMAD-attached quest/alias/fragment scripts. This is
    required because a decompiled function named `Fragment_N` does not by itself
    establish which quest stage invokes it.
  - Direction for this project: derive a record-level quest graph from the
    installed masters and join it to static script findings. Do not hard-code a
    wiki stage number or infer one from decompiler function order.

- The installed MQ101 corpus confirms direct manipulation of the sole local
  player, including cart vehicle attachment, chargen/RaceMenu, controls,
  AI-driven state, forced movement, inventory/equipment, saves, scenes, aliases,
  and player-relative triggers.
  - Direction for this project: do not add extra physical players to the authored
    cart/execution scene. Only the host executes MQ101; followers receive its
    cinematic presentation, use isolated local character creation behind a
    ready barrier, and materialize only at a verified host-control boundary.
    The design is recorded in
    `docs/reverse-engineering/INTRO_MQ101_MULTIPLAYER.md`.

- [Mutagen](https://github.com/Mutagen-Modding/Mutagen), NuGet package
  `Mutagen.Bethesda.Skyrim` pinned at `0.54.4` (GPL-3.0).
  - Its strongly typed read-only Skyrim record API is used by
    `Tools/QuestAudit/QuestRecordAudit` to correlate QUST stages, VMAD fragment
    names, objectives, aliases, and linked SCEN records from the installed
    masters. This avoids brittle byte parsing and makes the whole-game audit
    reproducible.
  - Direction for this project: emit only derived metadata and our analysis;
    omit Bethesda dialogue/journal text and never modify the installed masters.

## Headless quest and multiplayer simulation

- [SkyrimReLive](https://github.com/danmeedev/skyrimReLive) includes a
  no-game echo-client mode for networking smoke tests. It supports the same
  architectural boundary used here: exercise transport and replicated state
  without claiming to emulate the Skyrim executable.
- [OpenPapyrus](https://github.com/fireundubh/OpenPapyrus) and the open-source
  [Papyrus compiler/PEX reader](https://github.com/rethesda/papyrus-compiler-v)
  provide useful grammar, bytecode, and static-analysis references.
  - Direction for this project: use PEX and decompiled scripts to derive a
    conservative effect graph. These projects are not a complete Skyrim
    Papyrus/native-function runtime, so unknown conditions and engine callbacks
    remain explicitly uncertain and require in-engine replay.
- No mature open-source replacement for Skyrim's complete Papyrus VM, native
  function environment, AI, Havok, and quest-condition evaluator was identified
  in this review. `docs/MODSIM_ARCHITECTURE.md` therefore separates exact
  protocol/record tests, conservative script exploration, and GPU-backed game
  tests instead of presenting a headless simulator as full engine parity.
- The repository's pinned `TiltedConnect` test client and production
  `SkyrimProtocolBot` were selected for the initial ModSim adapter so scenarios
  exercise the same transport and message factories as the shipped server.
  A separate mock networking stack was rejected because it could pass while
  production serialization, authentication, or disconnect handling was broken.
- [SkyMP's current Papyrus VM](https://github.com/skyrim-multiplayer/skymp/tree/f926944b18e3aed4bc3864ce668626c05ec2545f/papyrus-vm)
  is MIT-licensed and implements PEX reading, bytecode operations, objects,
  events, inheritance, synchronous and promised native calls, and VM stacks.
  It was imported at commit `f926944b18e3aed4bc3864ce668626c05ec2545f`
  with its license and attribution intact. `SkyrimPapyrusProbe` proves it can
  parse the installed 1.7.104 MQ101 PEX files; this moves ModSim beyond a
  decompiled-text effect scanner toward executable quest scripts.
  - The adopted VM now powers `SkyrimPapyrusSim`; all 159 MQ101 `Fragment_*`
    functions execute individually with missing calls and VM error logs treated
    as failures. Native shims derived from SkyMP server behavior remain clearly
    separated into modeled logical effects and conservative engine surrogates.
- [SkyMP libespm](https://github.com/skyrim-multiplayer/skymp/tree/f926944b18e3aed4bc3864ce668626c05ec2545f/libespm)
  is also MIT-licensed and is a candidate for lower-level record and condition
  extraction where Mutagen does not expose enough data.
- SkyMP's server native-function and condition implementations are AGPL-3.0.
  They are valuable behavior references, but were not copied in this initial
  import. Reusing them directly would require preserving the AGPL license and
  source-availability obligations for the combined server component.

## Durable shared-campaign persistence

- [SkyMP server configuration](https://github.com/skyrim-multiplayer/skymp/blob/main/docs/docs_server_configuration_reference.md)
  and the locally pinned MIT `viet` storage interfaces demonstrate a
  server-owned database boundary with replaceable database drivers and
  asynchronous batched upserts. This supports the core decision that shared
  world truth must outlive both clients and the server process. The current
  slice adopts the durable server boundary, but not SkyMP's whole ChangeForm
  schema: Skyrim SE Multiplayer needs ordered cross-domain transactions and a
  common checkpoint watermark for quests, lifecycle, inventory, references,
  and later save projection.
- [CommonLibSSE serialization example by Ryan McKenzie](https://gist.github.com/Ryan-rsm-McKenzie/b1c1e04f95f471c200834b6ac7ebd962)
  demonstrates SKSE co-save records and versioned save/load callbacks. This is
  the correct later location for small client projection metadata such as
  campaign ID, player ID, and last applied revision. It is deliberately not
  used as the canonical campaign database: a follower's missing or stale
  co-save must be recoverable from the server ledger.
- [Elden Ring Seamless Co-op FAQ](https://ersc-docs.github.io/faq/) documents
  host-based progression and warns that session event flags persist into each
  participant's local save. Its separate `.co2` profile informs the product
  behavior, while raw host-save copying is rejected for Skyrim because an ESS
  also contains player identity, Papyrus VM state, aliases, persistent handles,
  and load-order-dependent data.

Implemented direction: `Code/campaign/CampaignLedger.*` is a SQLite WAL-backed
campaign sequencer with a stable campaign UUID, authority epoch, monotonic
revision, unique transaction IDs, durable journal, and checkpoint watermark.
The server opens it before constructing gameplay services. Authentication now
advertises campaign identity/revision/epoch, and leader quest updates commit to
the ledger before modifying the in-memory quest log or broadcasting. Native
ESS writes remain intentionally out of scope until snapshot/reconnect and save
barrier protocols can prove that all clients are materializing the same
committed revision.

## Effective deployed mod view

- [Mod Organizer 2 USVFS](https://github.com/ModOrganizer2/usvfs) is a GPLv3
  process-local virtual filesystem that hooks file discovery/open operations and
  can overlay multiple directories at one destination. This supports scanning
  `Data` inside Skyrim rather than scanning MO2's physical mod directories: the
  scanner observes the same merged loose-file winners available to the game.
  No USVFS code was copied.
- [Wabbajack's pre-compilation documentation](https://github.com/wabbajack-tools/wabbajack/wiki/04.-Pre-Compilation)
  describes tracing installed files to archive sources and hashing files inside
  BSAs, while its changelog documents a non-blocking file-hash cache. This is
  prior art for content identity and cached hashing. The first implementation
  here deliberately hashes whole BSAs: identical archives plus identical loose
  winners prove equivalent inputs without needing to redistribute or unpack
  copyrighted archives. Archive-member provenance remains future work.
- [Wabbajack indexed game files](https://github.com/wabbajack-tools/indexed-game-files)
  publishes per-game file hashes, supporting a later separate base-runtime
  manifest. It is not currently consumed because installed Skyrim builds and
  Creation Club entitlements must be identified locally and policy must remain
  explicit.

Implemented direction: `Code/encoding/Deployment/DeploymentScanner.*` scans the
effective in-process `Data` namespace asynchronously, caches SHA-256 results by
normalized path/backing-file identity/size/write time, and creates deterministic whole-tree and
semantic-layer roots. Authentication carries the roots, and the server freezes
the first compatible complete deployment in the campaign manifest.

## Steam lobby discovery and transport

- [Valve's ISteamMatchmaking reference](https://partner.steamgames.com/doc/api/isteammatchmaking)
  defines private lobbies as invite-only (`k_ELobbyTypePrivate = 0`) and
  friends-only lobbies as visible/joinable to friends (`k_ELobbyTypeFriendsOnly
  = 1`). The client therefore auto-creates an invite-only lobby and changes it
  to friends-only only after the host selects Open. `SetLobbyType` is owner-only;
  the game server independently enforces that only its party leader can change
  the replicated access/password state.
- The same `LobbyCreated_t` reference defines the actionable failure results:
  generic failure, timeout, rate limiting, access denied when the Steam app is
  not configured for lobbies or the account lacks access, and no backend
  connection. The client logs the raw `EResult` and lobby ID so live tests can
  distinguish an ABI/layout bug from an App ID entitlement limitation.
- [Valve's Steam API initialization reference](https://partner.steamgames.com/doc/api/steam_api)
  requires `SteamAPI_Init` to succeed before any Steamworks interface is used.
  Loading `steam_api64.dll`, setting `SteamAppId`, and obtaining interface
  pointers is not sufficient; live testing returned `k_EResultNoConnection`
  until this missing initialization path was identified.
- The same reference documents lobby metadata as owner-written, member-readable
  key/value data. Lobby metadata contains only `has_password=0|1`; the secret is
  never advertised. The actual password is checked by the Skyrim Together
  authentication path and capped at 64 characters. Friend results are filtered
  by Steam App ID and joined lobbies must carry this project's marker and exact
  build before their endpoint is used.
- [Valve's lobby matchmaking guide](https://partner.steamgames.com/doc/features/multiplayer/matchmaking)
  documents friend-lobby discovery, lobby ownership, the overlay invitation UI,
  and `GameLobbyJoinRequested_t`. Friend discovery and the invitation dialog are
  adopted. Automatic runtime invite acceptance remains a follow-up because this
  project dynamically loads the flat Steam ABI and does not yet safely register
  Steam callbacks.
- [Steam Datagram Relay](https://partner.steamgames.com/doc/features/multiplayer/steamdatagramrelay)
  and Valve's [GameNetworkingSockets P2P reference](https://github.com/ValveSoftware/GameNetworkingSockets/blob/master/README_P2P.md)
  confirm that lobby discovery does not itself relay the existing UDP game
  protocol. Current lobby connections still use the host's advertised LAN
  endpoint. A relay adapter must be implemented and exercised separately before
  Internet play can be described as NAT-independent or relay-backed.
- [CEF process result codes](https://cef-builds.spotifycdn.com/docs/148.0/cef__types_8h.html)
  define exit code 38 as `CEF_RESULT_CODE_NORMAL_EXIT_AUTO_DE_ELEVATED`.
  Interactive two-PC test launch tasks must therefore use the logged-in user's
  limited token; an elevated scheduled task can leave the native game alive
  without the CEF lobby/options overlay.

## Windows two-rig test access

- [Microsoft's OpenSSH Server guide](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows-server/administration/openssh/openssh_install_firstuse)
  remains the preferred Windows capability/service configuration reference.
  The second rig's optional capability source was unavailable and its prior MSI
  registration returned error 1603, so the project does not repeatedly invoke
  the hanging capability installation path.
- The official [Win32-OpenSSH ZIP installation guide](https://github.com/PowerShell/Win32-OpenSSH/wiki/Install-Win32-OpenSSH)
  documents extracting the release and running `install-sshd.ps1`. The repair
  script uses this as a fallback, verifies the ZIP against the SHA-256 published
  on the [official release](https://github.com/PowerShell/Win32-OpenSSH/releases/tag/10.0.0.0p2-Preview),
  stops only stale OpenSSH services/processes, and preserves an existing install
  directory before replacement.

## Plugin activation and light-plugin load order

- The upstream [TiltedEvolution source](https://github.com/tiltedphoques/TiltedEvolution)
  treats Skyrim's in-memory compile indices as the authority for whether a
  plugin is loaded. Live testing confirmed that `standardId == 0xFF` correctly
  exposed disabled multiplayer ESPs even though their files existed on disk.
  The deployment helper now enables the two required entries in `plugins.txt`,
  sets `bEnableFileSelection=1`, and backs up both user files before editing.
- Skyrim's regular and small/light plugins occupy separate compile-index pools;
  this is also visible in the reverse-engineered
  [CommonLibSSE-NG project](https://github.com/CharmedBaryon/CommonLibSSE-NG).
  `SkyrimTogetherQuestPatches.esp` has the TES4 `0x200` light flag despite its
  `.esp` extension. The original server loader inferred type only from the last
  filename character, so it assigned the wrong pool and ID. `loadorder.txt`
  now accepts a backward-compatible `light:` prefix, and the deployment helper
  derives that prefix from each enabled plugin's TES4 header.

## CEF message ownership

- CEF's [`CefListValue::SetList`](https://cef-builds.spotifycdn.com/docs/121.3/classCefListValue.html)
  transfers ownership when the supplied list is not already owned and
  invalidates that reference. Its
  [`CefFrame::SendProcessMessage`](https://cef-builds.spotifycdn.com/docs/139.0/classCefFrame.html)
  similarly transfers the process-message contents. A live title-screen join
  reused one argument list for `partyInfo` and `coopLobbyState`; the second send
  dereferenced the invalidated CEF wrapper. `OverlayApp::ExecuteAsync` now
  inserts a writable `Copy()` so callers may safely reuse their payload.

## Lobby focus and cursor ownership

- The WAI-ARIA keyboard-interface guidance in the
  [ARIA Practices source](https://github.com/w3c/aria-practices/blob/main/content/practices/keyboard-interface/keyboard-interface-practice.html)
  requires composite widgets to move focus explicitly when consuming arrow
  keys; browsers do not provide directional button focus automatically. The
  title lobby now cycles through its visible enabled controls with all four
  arrow keys while preserving arrow behavior inside text/password fields.
- Skyrim's Cursor Menu remains the only rendered pointer over title-screen CEF
  panels. Physical mouse movement updates the native and browser hit-test
  coordinates together without re-enabling CEF's software cursor. Absolute raw
  mouse packets (including Remote Desktop input) are normalized from the
  0..65535 desktop coordinate space instead of being ignored.

## Multiplayer state flight recorder

- [SKSE64's plugin messaging API](https://github.com/ianpatt/skse64/blob/master/skse64/PluginAPI.h)
  defines explicit pre-load, post-load, save, new-game, input-loaded, and
  data-loaded lifecycle messages. This confirms that save/load transitions
  should ultimately be recorded as events rather than inferred only from a
  frame sample. The current recorder uses snapshot age and UI loading state;
  wiring the SKSE lifecycle journal remains a follow-up.
- [SKSE64's game-event declarations](https://github.com/ianpatt/skse64/blob/master/skse64/GameEvents.h)
  expose load, combat, death, object-load, cell-load, and related dispatchers.
  They support an eventual bounded event journal for transitions that may fall
  between 100 ms samples. We do not walk event-dispatcher internals or retain
  raw engine pointers in diagnostic output.
- [CommonLibSSE-NG](https://github.com/alandtse/CommonLibSSE-NG/tree/ng)
  documents executable-version-aware reverse-engineered types and limited
  out-of-process unit testing. It supports the project's approach of sampling
  known typed fields on Skyrim's game thread instead of attempting an unsafe
  whole-process memory dump. This repository's pinned reverse-engineered types
  remain the build authority for offsets.
- The project's own `MenuTopicManager`, `SubtitleManager`, `ActorExtension`,
  animation components, and local/remote ownership components are the closest
  primary source for its multiplayer semantics. The recorder therefore emits
  dialogue speaker handles, action-pipeline state, campaign/authority epochs,
  and bounded entity ownership data. Pointer addresses are deliberately not
  treated as comparable state across two processes because ASLR and object
  lifetime make them misleading.

Implemented direction: game-thread snapshots act as a bounded state flight
recorder. They cover session/campaign authority, lifecycle/UI/loading state,
window focus and cursor clipping, player vitals/movement/dialogue/animation,
world-grid and actor-processing summaries, quests, and multiplayer entity
ownership. F10 stores the snapshot beside the screenshot and player note. A
read-only SSH retrieval tool mirrors the follower's newest bundle into the
local diagnostics workspace for semantic comparison.

## Native multiplayer authority seams

- [CommonLibSSE-NG](https://github.com/CharmedBaryon/CommonLibSSE-NG), local
  pin `b93280e832f263dbef44e44cbe2936622a02f91a` (MIT), was inspected for
  `TESCamera`, `PlayerCamera`, `TESCameraState`, `NiAVObject`, `NiCamera`,
  `LoadingMenu`, `MistMenu`, `TESLoadScreen`, `BShkbAnimationGraph`,
  `hkbCharacter`, `hkbRagdollDriver`, and `hkaRagdollInstance`. It supplies the
  candidate native layouts and vtable seams recorded in
  `reverse-engineering/NATIVE_LIVE_VALIDATION.md`; every unexercised offset or
  AE RelocationID remains a 1.7.104 probe requirement.
- [Precision's API](https://github.com/ersh1/Precision/blob/main/src/PrecisionAPI.h)
  exposes a pre-physics-step callback plus collision/contact callbacks. This is
  useful prior art for when read-only Havok diagnostics may run, but it does
  not provide multiplayer authority, snapshot identity, or ragdoll replication
  and therefore is not adopted as the network design.
- [TiltedEvolution's animation documentation](https://github.com/tiltedphoques/TiltedEvolution/blob/dev/README-ANIMATION-MODS.md)
  explicitly notes that animations can still fail to synchronize. Its existing
  behavior-variable/event relay is retained as one domain, but it cannot be
  treated as evidence that evaluated animation bones or Havok ragdoll bodies
  match between peers. Those require separate authority and validation streams.

### Loading-screen presentation probe and authority boundary

- [CommonLibSSE-NG `LoadingMenu.h` at the inspected pin](https://github.com/CharmedBaryon/CommonLibSSE-NG/blob/b93280e832f263dbef44e44cbe2936622a02f91a/include/RE/L/LoadingMenu.h)
  identifies the `Loading Menu` instance and its `BSTArray<TESLoadScreen*>` at
  non-VR object offset `0x58`. This layout is adopted only for guarded,
  read-only sampling on the supported 1.7.104 build; the array is never
  reordered or replaced.
- [CommonLibSSE-NG `TESLoadScreen.h` at the inspected pin](https://github.com/CharmedBaryon/CommonLibSSE-NG/blob/b93280e832f263dbef44e44cbe2936622a02f91a/include/RE/T/TESLoadScreen.h)
  confirms that LSCR is a `TESForm` and that the record owns the NIF, initial
  model transform, camera path, conditions, and loading text. Its plugin/base
  form identity is therefore the protocol key; an eligible-array index is
  rejected because plugin order and condition results can differ between PCs.
- [CommonLibSSE-NG `MistMenu.h` at the inspected pin](https://github.com/CharmedBaryon/CommonLibSSE-NG/blob/b93280e832f263dbef44e44cbe2936622a02f91a/include/RE/M/MistMenu.h)
  identifies the instantiated load-screen model, camera path, FOV, angle, and
  visibility booleans. These fields are adopted as read-only telemetry. The
  model's generic `NiAVObject::userData` is recorded only as present/absent and
  is not interpreted as a `TESLoadScreen*`; CommonLib does not establish that
  ownership relationship.
- [Faster-Loadscreens' `MenuOpenCloseEvent` watcher](https://github.com/1001Bits/Faster-Loadscreens/blob/master/skyrim/src/main.cpp)
  is primary open-source evidence that `Loading Menu` open/close is a useful
  load-lifecycle boundary. Its hard-coded render-loop and show/hide hooks are
  rejected here because that project does not publish verified 1.7.104 RVAs
  and those hooks do not expose the selected LSCR before Mist instantiation.

Implemented direction: the game-thread diagnostics snapshot increments a local
loading epoch on each observed `Loading Menu` open and reports the guarded LSCR
candidate IDs plus Mist state. A serialization-tested `LoadingScreenPresentation`
payload carries the eventual party epoch and plugin-qualified LSCR identity.
No client sends it and no follower applies it yet: neither inspected source
exposes the actual choice point, and post-instantiation inference from the NIF
or `userData` would be unsafe and ambiguous. Live 1.7.104 tracing must identify
the native selection call before the host request/follower constraint hooks are
enabled.
- [SmoothCam's camera implementation](https://github.com/mwilsnd/SkyrimSE-SmoothCam/blob/master/SmoothCam/source/camera.cpp)
  and [camera-state update hook](https://github.com/mwilsnd/SkyrimSE-SmoothCam/blob/master/SmoothCam/source/hooks.cpp)
  demonstrate the engine-facing write boundary: camera state is intercepted in
  `TESCameraState::Update`, while the final position is written to the camera
  root's local/world transforms and the child `NiCamera` world transform.
  Skyrim's normal update can overwrite earlier writes, so the multiplayer
  follower camera must apply its buffered host snapshot at this boundary (or a
  demonstrated later boundary), not merely update `TESCamera` input fields.
- The pinned CommonLibSSE-NG headers for
  [`hkpEntity`](https://github.com/CharmedBaryon/CommonLibSSE-NG/blob/b93280e832f263dbef44e44cbe2936622a02f91a/include/RE/H/hkpEntity.h),
  [`hkpMotion`](https://github.com/CharmedBaryon/CommonLibSSE-NG/blob/b93280e832f263dbef44e44cbe2936622a02f91a/include/RE/H/hkpMotion.h), and
  [`hkMotionState`](https://github.com/CharmedBaryon/CommonLibSSE-NG/blob/b93280e832f263dbef44e44cbe2936622a02f91a/include/RE/H/hkMotionState.h)
  establish the read-only ragdoll checksum path: rigid-body world membership,
  motion type, transform, and linear/angular velocity. The diagnostic uses
  bounded isolated layout views and hashes stable body indices rather than
  process-local pointers. It rejects unreadable pages, impossible array sizes,
  and non-finite samples; no native state is written.
# Continuous authority and seamless correction (2026-09-22)

- [SkyMP movement model and view application](https://github.com/skyrim-multiplayer/skymp/blob/main/skymp5-client/src/view/formView.ts): the source separates incoming authoritative movement from frame updates and tracks movement-change versions. Adopt that separation for our non-actor physics references: receive a target, then reconcile on the game update rather than warping during packet handling. Do not adopt its stale-actor rehosting rule; our current shared campaign keeps party-leader authority where the leader has a loaded cell.
- [SmoothCam native camera update](https://github.com/mwilsnd/SkyrimSE-SmoothCam/blob/master/SmoothCam/source/camera.cpp): the source writes camera node and child camera transforms in the native update path and distinguishes camera state transitions. This confirms the native seam, but the live 2026-09-22 paired test showed our direct post-update host transform copy flipping and separating by 54–77 units. Do not deploy the camera write again without state/timing telemetry and buffered interpolation; camera matrices alone do not synchronize Skyrim's cart simulation.
- Existing Skyrim Together Reborn `InterpolationSystem.cpp` in this tree buffers actor time points 300 ms behind the network clock. Reuse the frame-update pattern for non-actor physics, while keeping the cart correction window shorter and avoiding blocking waits.
- [CommonLibSSE-NG `bhkRigidBody`](https://ng.commonlib.dev/classRE_1_1bhkRigidBody.html) exposes native `GetTransform`, `SetPositionAndRotation`, and linear/angular velocity methods. The failed live cart trial shows why our `TESObjectREFR` render/reference transform is insufficient: the collision body and scripted movement must be observed and corrected consistently. Do not call these virtuals through guessed layout on Skyrim AE 1.7.104; first pin/verify the runtime's `NiCollisionObject`→`bhkRigidBody` chain and body state read-only.
# Separated-cell NPC simulation leases (2026-09-22)

- [SkyMP `FormView` source](https://github.com/skyrim-multiplayer/skymp/blob/main/skymp5-client/src/view/formView.ts) applies remote movement from a shared world model and attempts to rehost an actor when movement is stale. Adopt the *need for an in-range simulator* but not uncontrolled client rehosting: this project will have the server grant an epoch-versioned lease only when the party leader is not in the actor's loaded cell, and let the leader reclaim on entry.
- [SkyMP animation/physics hierarchy notes](https://github.com/BadDogSkyrim/BethesdaLibrary/blob/main/docs/file-formats/animations.md) distinguish graph state, skeletal pose, and ragdoll. Actor ownership and movement replication alone cannot make NPC bones identical; pose/ragdoll authority needs a separate native seam and a measured bandwidth budget.

# Script-driven references and MQ101 cart freeze (2026-09-22)

- [Mutagen's generated Skyrim placed-object keys](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/Mutagen-Modding/Mutagen.Bethesda.FormKeys/release/Mutagen.Bethesda.FormKeys.SkyrimLE/Skyrim/PlacedObject.cs) identify `0x000B9DF3` as `MQ101Cart1Ref` and `0x000BB970` as `MQ101Cart2Ref`. The former was the only shared reference with a large position divergence in our paired intro snapshots, before the follower stopped roughly 10,000 units short of the host. Use these IDs for diagnosis, not as a hardcoded gameplay exclusion.
- [SKSE's vanilla ObjectReference API](https://github.com/ianpatt/skse64/blob/master/scripts/vanilla/ObjectReference.psc) documents `SetMotionType` as a mutation of reference physics. Our follower's generic reference stream currently calls it on every incoming non-actor reference. A quest-driven cart should not be made keyframed solely because it moved. First probe whether it has an animation graph, then separate script/animation-driven references from freely simulated physics bodies. Do not adopt a cart-only teleport/skip patch as the general solution.
- [CommonLibSSE-NG's pinned form-type definitions](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/CharmedBaryon/CommonLibSSE-NG/b93280e832f263dbef44e44cbe2936622a02f91a/include/RE/F/FormTypes.h) confirm that runtime form type 24 is `Activator`. Both MQ101 carts were live-probed as loaded activators without animation graph managers. Therefore graph presence cannot identify all script/quest-driven movement. Adopt a narrow passive-clutter type set for the generic Havok snapshot path; keep activators/scene machinery in Skyrim's native driver until a separate authority stream for script-driven motion is validated. This excludes a class, not individual cart IDs.

# Native dialogue and cinematic timing (2026-09-22)

- Searched upstream Skyrim Together and SkyMP source for a voice-start authority pattern. The directly relevant Skyrim Together hooks are in this tree's `Code/client/Games/Skyrim/Actor.cpp` and `Code/client/Games/Misc/SubtitleManager.cpp`: they broadcast owner speech/subtitles, but still let each local Skyrim call its native speech/subtitle function. The follower's own MQ101 scene can therefore start a remote NPC line independently of the host's broadcast. Adopt a bounded native hook trace to distinguish local calls from network replay before changing the return path. Reject immediate suppression by forging a successful native sound return until the output handle and scene-wait semantics are verified.
- [SkyMP's Skyrim Platform event documentation](https://github.com/skyrim-multiplayer/skymp/blob/main/docs/skyrim_platform/new_events.md) confirms per-frame client event observation but does not solve native voice-start synchronization. Do not use its JS update loop as a substitute for Skyrim's native dialogue/audio boundary.

# Dedicated-server versus host-engine authority (2026-09-22)

- [Tilted Online's technical overview](https://wiki.tiltedphoques.com/tilted-online/technical-documentation/overview) explicitly says its server is only partially authoritative because it has no gameplay code or complete world representation; a client owns and simulates each loaded actor. Merely moving our existing server onto a third PC cannot eliminate duplicate Skyrim scene simulation.
- [SkyMP's server configuration reference](https://github.com/skyrim-multiplayer/skymp/blob/main/docs/docs_server_configuration_reference.md) shows a different path: server-side plugin/load-order and Papyrus data, configurable NPC loading, and its own cell-reset behavior. This is useful prior art for incrementally moving state domains server-side, but not evidence of a drop-in headless Skyrim engine that reproduces every vanilla quest, Havok constraint, and cinematic.
- [SkyrimReLive's project and architecture description](https://github.com/danmeedev/skyrimReLive) reports a 60 Hz authoritative ECS server with 20 Hz snapshots, while its own status says combat authority is still unfinished. It demonstrates a custom simulation server, not that the full Skyrim executable can be run headlessly as-is.
- The paired 2026-09-22 intro after the passive-object filter passed the previous cart-freeze location, but six later paired snapshots still measured 57–92 game-unit differences between matching carts and 102–140 units between camera roots. A follower also invoked native voice for remote NPC 0x2BF9E at 23:02:57.716 local time and replayed the host's same line at 23:03:00.380. These observations favor a single Skyrim-engine authority for scripted scenes plus follower render/proxy playback, not further independent-scene resync by teleport.

# MQ101 cart body-level probe and dialogue presentation (2026-09-22)

- [CommonLibSSE-NG `bhkNiCollisionObject`](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/CharmedBaryon/CommonLibSSE-NG/b93280e832f263dbef44e44cbe2936622a02f91a/include/RE/B/bhkNiCollisionObject.h), [`bhkRefObject`](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/CharmedBaryon/CommonLibSSE-NG/b93280e832f263dbef44e44cbe2936622a02f91a/include/RE/B/bhkRefObject.h), and [`hkpEntity`](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/CharmedBaryon/CommonLibSSE-NG/b93280e832f263dbef44e44cbe2936622a02f91a/include/RE/H/hkpEntity.h) establish the guarded read-only chain `NiAVObject::collisionObject -> bhkNiCollisionObject::body (+0x20) -> bhkRefObject::referencedObject (+0x10) -> hkpEntity::motion (+0x150)`. The live paired probe followed that chain successfully on both MQ101 carts. Both bodies were in a Havok world with motion type 3; [the enum](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/CharmedBaryon/CommonLibSSE-NG/b93280e832f263dbef44e44cbe2936622a02f91a/include/RE/H/hkpMotion.h) identifies 3 as box-inertia/dynamic, not keyframed. Their velocities differed between machines. The reference and rendered-node positions matched exactly on each machine, while cross-machine positions briefly differed by 204 game units in the 23:10 paired run. Thus this is a body/scene timeline issue, not a separate mesh transform offset.
- [CommonLibSSE-NG's `bhkRigidBody` implementation](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/CharmedBaryon/CommonLibSSE-NG/b93280e832f263dbef44e44cbe2936622a02f91a/src/RE/B/bhkRigidBody.cpp) exposes an AE relocation for `SetLinearVelocity` (78089), a candidate for a soft follower-body servo that preserves native constraints. It is not yet validated on runtime 1.7.104 or used for writes. Do not treat the presence of a candidate setter as proof it is safe during the game update or for scene-bound carts.
- The existing `SubtitleManager::HookShowSubtitle` let Skyrim present a remote NPC's locally advanced subtitle even though an owner-originated `SubtitleEvent` is already relayed via the network. It now suppresses that local presentation while connected in a party and retains the owner's replay path. This is presentation-only: it does not forge a scene-completion return or pause local dialogue logic. The paired build has launched and remained connected, but visual subtitle parity has not yet been confirmed by a player or screenshot comparison.
- [CommonLibSSE-NG `BSSoundHandle`](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/CharmedBaryon/CommonLibSSE-NG/b93280e832f263dbef44e44cbe2936622a02f91a/include/RE/B/BSSoundHandle.h) establishes that the native output handle is 12 bytes and has `SetVolume(float)`; [its source](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/CharmedBaryon/CommonLibSSE-NG/b93280e832f263dbef44e44cbe2936622a02f91a/src/RE/B/BSSoundHandle.cpp) uses AE relocation ID 67626. Our existing `Actor::SpeakSound` caller passes a three-`uint32_t` output buffer initialized to invalid ID, matching that handle layout. Adopt post-native volume zero for a remote NPC's locally started line, preserving the real return value, output handle, and sound duration so the local scene can keep its wait semantics. The owner-originated network replay bypasses the native hook and remains audible. This is safer than returning a forged success without starting a handle, but the relocation and audible result still require paired validation on runtime 1.7.104.
- The live 1.7.104 trace validated `SetVolume(0)` returning true for remote-NPC handles. It also exposed two native calls per scene line: the first returned true with invalid sound ID `0xFFFFFFFF`, and the second returned true with a playable ID. Broadcasting before the native call sent two network replays. The owner now emits only after a valid sound handle is returned. The follower no longer calls `StopCurrentDialogue(true)` for a remote NPC on network replay, because this perturbed the independently running local scene; it keeps the silent local handle's completion semantics while presenting owner audio. These are dialogue-domain corrections, not a claim that gestures/lip sync or scene clocks match.

# MQ101 opening title-card timing (2026-09-22)

- [SKSE's copy of the vanilla `Game.psc`](https://github.com/ianpatt/skse64/blob/master/scripts/vanilla/Game.psc) declares three separate native global title-sequence calls: `ShowTitleSequenceMenu`, `StartTitleSequence(string)`, and `HideTitleSequenceMenu`. This makes the title cards an independently timed Papyrus-driven presentation domain, not an ordinary loading screen or a camera overlay.
- [CommonLibSSE-NG's `TitleSequenceMenu` definition](https://github.com/CharmedBaryon/CommonLibSSE-NG/blob/b93280e832f263dbef44e44cbe2936622a02f91a/include/RE/T/TitleSequenceMenu.h) identifies its UI name as `TitleSequence Menu`. The first safe native probe is to timestamp the three calls and menu-open state on both PCs; host-owned cue playback should follow only after the call order, sequence argument ABI, and local quest-script side effects are established. Simply hiding the follower menu would not synchronize the underlying scene or NPC lip animation.
- The paired 23:44 campaign confirmed that the relocated `NativeFunctionBase::Call` hook sees ordinary Papyrus dispatch (`ObjectReference::AddItem`, `ReferenceAlias::AddInventoryEventFilter`), yet it logged no title native calls on either machine before the first MQ101 cart voice line. This could mean the fast campaign did not run the card path, or that the visible overlay uses a different native/UI entry point; it is not evidence that the cards are synchronized. Monitor the `TitleSequence Menu` open/close transition separately before imposing a follower gate.

# Quest-stage native authority boundary (2026-09-22)

- [CommonLibSSE-NG `TESQuest` source](https://github.com/CharmedBaryon/CommonLibSSE-NG/blob/b93280e832f263dbef44e44cbe2936622a02f91a/src/RE/T/TESQuest.cpp) exposes `currentStage` and uses relocated native quest start, but does not expose a source-backed quest-stage setter. This tree's `TESQuest::SetStage` already uses AE relocation 25004; treat a hook on that address as a runtime probe, not proof that all Papyrus/script stage writes funnel through it.
- [CommonLibSSE-NG quest layout](https://github.com/CharmedBaryon/CommonLibSSE-NG/blob/b93280e832f263dbef44e44cbe2936622a02f91a/include/RE/T/TESQuest.h) distinguishes quest flags, stage records, runtime instance data, and `ChangeFlags::kQuestStages`. A follower cannot become read-only by assigning `currentStage` alone: the VM may have latent stacks, stage fragments, aliases, and scene actions still advancing. First measure native stage-write coverage on both peers; only then gate the follower at a boundary with an explicit host-apply override.
- [CommonLibSSE-NG's pinned `BGSScene` layout](https://github.com/CharmedBaryon/CommonLibSSE-NG/blob/b93280e832f263dbef44e44cbe2936622a02f91a/include/RE/B/BGSScene.h) exposes `isPlaying` at +0xB0 and the owning quest at +0x98. [Upstream PR #103](https://github.com/CharmedBaryon/CommonLibSSE-NG/pull/103) proposes interpreting +0xBC as the current phase index, but that change is unmerged and not validated on this game's runtime. Sample +0xBC as a raw word, not a phase, until paired transition observations corroborate it. Scene transition telemetry is a safer first host timeline slice than directly writing the follower's quest/scene fields.
- The local server quest relay stamps `NotifyQuestUpdate` with campaign `Revision` and `AuthorityEpoch` after its leader-only ledger commit, while the client `OnQuestUpdate` did not inspect either field. Adopt an epoch check and per-quest monotonic revision guard before calling any native quest setter. This prevents delayed/duplicate host updates from replaying an old fragment, but does not yet suppress follower-local Papyrus quest execution.
- Paired 2026-09-23 MQ101 snapshots confirmed `BGSScene +0xBC` changing from `0xFFFFFFFF` while idle through small ascending integers during playback on both PCs, matching the open PR's proposed phase interpretation for this runtime. It remains a read-only field. An ordered leader-only scene-observation stream now relays phase transitions through the server and logs follower comparisons; it does not mutate follower scenes. Do not compare raw Windows wall-clock timestamps across the two PCs as synchronized clocks: the 23:54 title-menu open/close timestamp differences are diagnostic clues only, not calibrated drift measurements. Use host-sequenced messages and follower-local sample age for timing claims.
- The observed follower-local native `SetStage` calls while joined explain why suppressing outbound follower quest packets alone is insufficient: its own Papyrus VM can still advance quest fragments before a leader packet arrives. Adopt a guarded native stage-write boundary only for syncable quests during an active party launch/session. The existing `ScopedQuestOverride` marks sequenced host applies and must pass through; local-only exceptions must remain native. This is a stage-authority experiment, not full scene/alias/VM authority, and requires a paired intro before an in-game parity claim. [SKSE's Quest Papyrus API](https://github.com/ianpatt/skse64/blob/master/scripts/vanilla/Quest.psc) shows `SetStage` as a native boolean operation; returning false on a blocked follower write accurately signals that the local VM did not commit it.
- Reviewer B's read-only follow-up suggested a generic main-thread remote-state queue and native scene/graph/Havok setters. Its shell could not inspect this checkout, so the proposal was not adopted as-is: this project's existing packet handlers already buffer some states and the candidate `BGSScene::Update`/rigid-body signatures were not source-verified for AE 1.7.104. [CommonLib's `BGSScene` header](https://github.com/CharmedBaryon/CommonLibSSE-NG/blob/b93280e832f263dbef44e44cbe2936622a02f91a/include/RE/B/BGSScene.h) exposes runtime fields but no callable update method; [Precision's pre-physics callback interface](https://github.com/ersh1/Precision/blob/main/src/PrecisionAPI.h) is relevant to future body-level authority but does not itself establish safe write semantics. Do not ship guessed scene/physics setters to chase cart parity.

# Dynamic rigid-body authority experiment (2026-09-23)

- The paired native-stage-guard run still measured cart position errors swinging from about 6 to 80 game units; both carts remained dynamic Havok bodies, with follower linear velocities differing from the leader. This rejects quest-stage gating as a complete cart fix. [CommonLib's pinned `bhkNiCollisionObject`](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/CharmedBaryon/CommonLibSSE-NG/b93280e832f263dbef44e44cbe2936622a02f91a/include/RE/B/bhkNiCollisionObject.h) locates the body wrapper at +0x20; the pointer chain and `hkpRigidBody` motion/velocity fields were already read successfully on both live carts.
- [CommonLib's pinned `bhkRigidBody::SetLinearVelocity` implementation](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/CharmedBaryon/CommonLibSSE-NG/b93280e832f263dbef44e44cbe2936622a02f91a/src/RE/B/bhkRigidBody.cpp) uses AE relocation 78089 with a `const hkVector4&` argument. Adopt a bounded follower velocity servo for validated dynamic bodies, using host body velocity plus position error, instead of `TESObjectREFR::Update3DPosition` or `SetMotionType` on script-driven objects. This is experimental until a paired run checks crash, cart/camera error, frame step, and visual jumping; body setter safety on 1.7.104 is not proven by source alone.
- That paired velocity trial failed: cart errors remained roughly 50–340 units, and the follower's cart velocity exceeded the host's. The body is evidently also driven by script/scene motion; free-body velocity control alone is not a stable authority surface. It is disabled in the next build.
- The next read-only build exposed native `hkTransform` translation on each cart. Paired samples showed the body translation multiplied by 70 matches the Skyrim reference position (e.g. host x 315.7008 Havok units and x 22096.293 game units), while the body-to-body delta times 70 matched the reference delta. [CommonLib `hkTransform`](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/CharmedBaryon/CommonLibSSE-NG/b93280e832f263dbef44e44cbe2936622a02f91a/include/RE/H/hkTransform.h) places translation at +0x30; [its `bhkRigidBody` declaration](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/CharmedBaryon/CommonLibSSE-NG/b93280e832f263dbef44e44cbe2936622a02f91a/include/RE/B/bhkRigidBody.h) exposes virtual `SetPosition` slot 0x35. A frame-sampled native body-position playback is a plausible next experiment, but it must use a temporal buffer and verify that the engine's own script/physics update does not overwrite it before presentation.

# Timed voice/subtitle presentation (2026-09-23)

- Searched the [upstream Skyrim Together source](https://github.com/tiltedphoques/TiltedEvolution) for a voice-start queue tied to actor interpolation; no directly reusable timed implementation was found. The source in this checkout already buffers remote actor animation at `networkTick - 300 ms` (`CharacterService::RunRemoteUpdates`), while `OnNotifyDialogue` and `OnNotifySubtitle` played incoming cues immediately. This creates an avoidable presentation lead even if the owner sends the correct cue. Adopt source-clock timestamps on owner voice/subtitle packets and release those events at the same 300 ms remote-actor presentation tick. This changes presentation timing only; it does not synchronize the follower's local scene state or exact bone pose.
- The paired pose probe sampled 28 matching actor IDs on each machine during MQ101, with differing skeleton checksums and most ragdoll checksums. Samples are sequential rather than simultaneous, so checksum inequality alone cannot quantify per-frame pose error, but it confirms that mere actor identity/graph descriptors do not establish exact sampled pose equality. A pose stream or correct native graph/physics phase boundary remains necessary for 1:1 visuals.

# Position-only cinematic camera trial (2026-09-23)

- [SmoothCam's `Camera::SetPosition`](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/mwilsnd/SkyrimSE-SmoothCam/master/SmoothCam/source/camera.cpp) writes the root local/world and child `NiCamera` world **translation** after the native camera update, without copying another camera's rotation. Our prior full transform copy flipped the follower cinematic view. Trial translation-only owner playback at the existing post-state-update hook, preserving follower-native orientation, and compare paired camera error and screenshots. This can align viewpoint location but cannot by itself resolve script/scene or cart divergence. Keep the trial disabled if it creates jumps or loses the game camera.
- The paired position-only trial failed visual validation. Some root-position errors fell to roughly 40–55 game units, but a follower screenshot at 01:14 showed mostly sky and cart edge while the leader screenshot at 01:14 showed the intended passengers/road. The native follower camera orientation still diverged, and position-only playback cannot make the cinematic view equivalent. Disabled again; do not infer visual parity from root-position error alone.

# Cross-game authority model and unattended input (2026-09-23)

- [Quake III's server snapshot builder](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/id-Software/Quake-III-Arena/master/code/server/sv_snapshot.c) selects entities for each client's world view and sends server-authored snapshots; [Source SDK's interpolation history](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/ValveSoftware/source-sdk-2013/master/src/game/client/interpolatedvar.h) presents timestamped entity state from a past render clock rather than simulating arbitrary packet arrival time. Adopt their architectural split: one authoritative simulation, ordered state/event log, buffered presentation. Do not import their code or assume Skyrim's engine has their deterministic entity abstractions.
- [SkyMP's client form view](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/skyrim-multiplayer/skymp/main/skymp5-client/src/view/formView.ts) keeps remote world objects as views of received state, and [TES3MP's original changelog](https://github.com/TES3MP/TES3MP/blob/0.8.1/tes3mp-changelog.md) explicitly records late actor-authority packets causing desync. Keep authority epochs and source identity in every domain; do not rely on a global quest stage or save copy to reproduce physics, animation, AI, or scene actions.
- The [independent open-source Elden Ring Seamless rewrite](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/micthiesen/unseamless-coop/main/README.md) explicitly says its Steam lobby, password, and presence layer works but its in-world session layer is not yet playable. Its source is a useful caution: adopting a lobby UX is not equivalent to reproducing the engine session. The original Seamless Co-op's release repository is not a source implementation we can copy.
- [Microsoft's `SendInput` contract](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/api/winuser/nf-winuser-sendinput) requires an active desktop/focus path and reports how many input events were inserted; [the `SetForegroundWindow` contract](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/api/winuser/nf-winuser-setforegroundwindow) may deny focus even to a desktop app. For unattended paired progression after RaceSex menu, add a narrowly allowlisted test-bridge key command running inside the game's interactive process, validate foreground ownership and actual menu transition, and fail closed when Windows refuses focus. It is test instrumentation, not a gameplay input path.
- In the 2026-09-23 paired MQ101 run, comparing bounded samples at the same shared world tick removed the prior ~1 second SSH observation skew. The carts still differed by over 100 game units, and native scene raw phase diverged by seven steps even though both quest stages were 26. This supports the single-authority/view-proxy design above: matching quest stages and relay timestamps are not sufficient to make Skyrim's independently running scene/Papyrus/Havok timelines equivalent. The evidence is in `Tools/InGameTests/artifacts/authority-20260923-075423.json` and later authority artifacts.
- [CommonLibSSE-NG's pinned `BGSScene`](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/CharmedBaryon/CommonLibSSE-NG/b93280e832f263dbef44e44cbe2936622a02f91a/include/RE/B/BGSScene.h) places its action array at +0x80; [its `BGSSceneAction`](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/CharmedBaryon/CommonLibSSE-NG/b93280e832f263dbef44e44cbe2936622a02f91a/include/RE/B/BGSSceneAction.h) exposes actor ID and authored start/end phase. Capture only guarded, read-only phase-eligible action metadata when diagnosing scene drift. The layout does not expose a validated scene-advance or action-apply API, so a raw phase-field write would be unsafe and would not replay dialogue/package/timer side effects.
- [Microsoft documents `keybd_event`](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/api/winuser/nf-winuser-keybd_event) as a superseded keystroke synthesizer with both virtual-key and scan-code parameters. In the local live RaceSex Menu, that exact external call opened the native confirmation dialog; two `SendInput` variants returned success but did not. Trial the same call only behind the bridge's visible-menu and foreground checks, never as a general player-input or networking mechanism. A returned bridge acknowledgement is not proof of UI transition; capture and verify the menu afterward.

# Universal engine-boundary trace (2026-09-23)

- [Precision's pinned hook declarations](https://github.com/ersh1/Precision/blob/9ef45e0d8d9e3cf8b2df444f05142c1e7bf41cd4/src/Hooks.h#L197-L238) and [pre-physics implementation](https://github.com/ersh1/Precision/blob/9ef45e0d8d9e3cf8b2df444f05142c1e7bf41cd4/src/Hooks.cpp#L650-L718) expose generic AE Havok/ragdoll timing seams. The installed Skyrim 1.7.104 executable and Address Library were independently checked against Precision's callsite bytes, but matching bytes alone does not prove a safe hook ABI or thread context. Adopt these for a later read-only bounded trace only after a disposable live hook trial; reject direct body writes or heap/logging in Havok callbacks.
- [CommonLib's pinned animation graph layout](https://github.com/CharmedBaryon/CommonLibSSE-NG/blob/b93280e832f263dbef44e44cbe2936622a02f91a/include/RE/B/BShkbAnimationGraph.h#L109-L139) supplies graph/ragdoll identities but not a universal serialized evaluated pose. Record engine phase and pose checksums before designing playback; do not infer pose parity from graph-variable parity.
- The existing relocated `NativeFunctionBase::Call` hook sees native Papyrus dispatches. Add a cheap generic count, last-function hash, and local timestamp to the game snapshot instead of instrumenting only MQ101 title functions. This is observation, not VM authority: script bytecode, latent tasklets, and non-native effects can diverge without incrementing that counter. Cross-machine absolute counts are not comparable across different launch histories; compare changes over a common campaign window and correlate with the shared world tick.
- [CommonLib's native `NiAVObject::Update` and scenegraph traversal](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/CharmedBaryon/CommonLibSSE-NG/b93280e832f263dbef44e44cbe2936622a02f91a/src/RE/N/NiAVObject.cpp) identify a potential visual-proxy seam distinct from `TESObjectREFR::position` and Havok body state. Do not call it with guessed `NiUpdateData` or overwrite a live node merely because the received host target is correct; first validate update order, child transforms, collision ownership, and render timing. Prior direct body and reference corrections made the cart teleport rather than converge smoothly.
- [CommonLib's pinned `IVirtualMachine`](https://github.com/CharmedBaryon/CommonLibSSE-NG/blob/b93280e832f263dbef44e44cbe2936622a02f91a/include/RE/I/IVirtualMachine.h#L558-L570) identifies `Update(float)` and `UpdateTasklets(float)` at vtable slots 04 and 05. Add observation-only duration/cadence hooks with the original ABI; do not iterate latent queues or suppress follower tasklets, because either could break scene waits or unrelated mod scripts. The local compiled build passed tests, but the new vtable hooks need a one-PC smoke test before a two-PC campaign.

# Open-source engine feasibility (2026-09-23)

- [OpenMW's official 0.49 release](https://openmw.org/2025/openmw-0-49-0-released/) describes Skyrim SE support as experimental and missing essential animation/physics systems; its maturity for Morrowind does not make it a drop-in Skyrim gameplay base.
- [ByroRedux](https://github.com/matiaszanolli/ByroRedux) has a real MQ101 runtime vertical slice, but its [roadmap](https://github.com/matiaszanolli/ByroRedux/blob/main/ROADMAP.md) identifies auto-accepted character creation, partial fragment coverage, narrow HKX cinematic animation, and future multiplayer. Its published `AGENTS.md` and `docs/legacy/` explicitly reference leaked Gamebryo source; do not import its implementation without a provenance review, regardless of the README's MIT claim.
- [Wah Krah Jol](https://github.com/realfakenerd/wah-krah-jol) is a source-visible Skyrim engine effort whose roadmap still places general gameplay/physics/networking ahead. [recreation](https://github.com/Force67/recreation) and [rx](https://github.com/Force67/rx) publish source and claim some authoritative multiplayer features, but the checked top-level `main` trees did not provide a license file; treat as research only until licensing is clarified. [SkyrimReLive](https://github.com/danmeedev/skyrimReLive) is Apache-2.0 multiplayer prior art but reports ghost-player locomotion/weapon sync rather than whole-game parity.
- [Bethesda's Skyrim SE EULA](https://store.steampowered.com/eula/489830_eula_0) reserves original code/assets and contains reverse-engineering restrictions. Avoid shipping game files or leaked code. Jurisdiction-specific exceptions and distribution risk require legal review; this engineering note is not legal advice. The concrete candidate/acceptance decision is in [OPEN_ENGINE_FEASIBILITY.md](OPEN_ENGINE_FEASIBILITY.md).

# Evaluated pose authority seam (2026-09-23)

- [FO4_Wrld's open-source native pose implementation](https://github.com/ThePie88/FO4_Wrld/blob/6e0e1eb55237313a815c8f5e9436fd19524f828b/fw_native/src/native/scene_inject.cpp) is useful cross-game prior art: an owner captures named render-skeleton joints, relays bounded quaternions, and a receiver applies them after its local per-frame actor update. Its [changelog](https://github.com/ThePie88/FO4_Wrld/blob/6e0e1eb55237313a815c8f5e9436fd19524f828b/CHANGELOG.md) documents real failures from stale/detached bone pointers, shared skeleton clones, partial LOD walks, and first-person stub poses. Adopt the architectural lesson—owner pose, graph/skeleton identity, post-native-update presentation, explicit lifetime and LOD gates—but not its Fallout 4 offsets, source code, or AGPL implementation in this differently licensed project. The first Skyrim increment is a bounded, ownership-checked pose transport/probe; applying to a live skeleton requires its own verified Skyrim hook and paired safety test.

- [Active Ragdoll's published `hkbCharacter` layout](https://github.com/adamhynek/activeragdoll/blob/master/include/RE/havok_behavior.h) identifies `poseLocal` at +0x90 as a contiguous `hkQsTransform` array and `numPoseLocal` at +0x98; [CommonLibSSE-NG's graph-manager API](https://github.com/CharmedBaryon/CommonLibSSE-NG/blob/main/src/RE/I/IAnimationGraphManagerHolder.cpp) provides source-backed manager access. We used these as read-only layout candidates and validated the pointer/count/range on the installed Skyrim AE 1.7.104 build before hashing; no published code was copied.
- The paired one-shot sample in `Tools/InGameTests/artifacts/authority-20260923-095225.json` was 63 shared-world-tick milliseconds apart. All 12 overlapping actors had readable evaluated local poses (53 or 99 transforms on both PCs), but zero checksums matched. Graph descriptors matched 12/12; graph transitions matched only 3/12. MQ101 stage was 15 on both PCs, while two carts differed by 273 and 214 game units. This establishes a usable contiguous pose read seam and disproves action/variable relay as a sufficient 1:1 visual solution. A 63 ms sample offset can change animated bone checksums, so checksum mismatch alone does not quantify visual error; the scene/graph/cart divergence is independent corroboration.
- Continuous full bone/ragdoll scanning itself took roughly 123-202 ms on the host and 171-283 ms on the follower per sample, enough to perturb the update loop. `Capture-TwoPcPose.ps1` now schedules a one-shot sample against the shared world tick, waits for both PCs, and preserves the exact sample ticks and scan durations. Any production pose stream should read the contiguous evaluated pose at the verified engine phase, not run the slow safety-heavy diagnostic walker every frame.
- The strengthened capture in `Tools/InGameTests/artifacts/authority-20260923-100252.json` persisted exact one-shot snapshots and computed numeric per-bone errors. At a 74 ms sample offset, the game reported the same MQ101 stage and active-scene phase/action state on both PCs, but cart reference errors were 144/133 units, camera-root error 121 units, and the worst local-bone rotation differed by 90 degrees. That is a state-domain split, not a missing scene-phase packet. The previous attempt at the start of the same run was 444 ms apart and was correctly rejected by the strict timing gate, rather than misreported as pose parity evidence.
- A bounded lossless `EvaluatedPoseSnapshot` is now captured from the owner's active Havok graph, forwarded by the existing `OwnerView`-checked movement path, and held read-only on the receiver. Capture is limited to two actors per 100 ms snapshot while the transport seam is measured; this is a probe rate, not a target 1:1 animation cadence. The 2026-09-23 paired campaign produced 32/32 matching source-tick/bone-count/checksum triples in `authority-20260923-101854.json`. A subsequent sample had 31/31 checksum matches among same-tick actors, with four receiver poses still older than their host copies (`authority-20260923-101910.json`). The camera was still about 143 game units apart in that later sample. Thus serialization, owner validation, and relay can preserve exact pose values, but receiver playback, phase timing, cart/physics authority, and five-player rate/performance are not validated.
- [CommonLibSSE-NG's `UpdateAnimationGraphManager` wrapper](https://github.com/CharmedBaryon/CommonLibSSE-NG/blob/main/src/RE/I/IAnimationGraphManagerHolder.cpp) identifies a relocated native graph update surface. A post-update hook is a candidate for Skyrim pose application, but its AE 1.7.104 callsite, `this` identity, thread, render ordering, and skeleton lifetime must be verified by a read-only paired trace before writes. FO4_Wrld's stale-bone crashes make direct copying into cached NiNode pointers particularly unsafe without generation checks.
- The pinned [CommonLibSSE-NG graph-manager implementation](https://github.com/CharmedBaryon/CommonLibSSE-NG/blob/b93280e832f263dbef44e44cbe2936622a02f91a/src/RE/I/IAnimationGraphManagerHolder.cpp) gives `UpdateAnimationGraphManager(const BSAnimationUpdateData&)` AE relocation ID 32899 and a `bool` result. Trial a transparent detour with the second argument opaque, recording only holder pointer, post-call time, and thread; do not infer pose or render ordering from the name alone. Confirm that the installed 1.7.104 Address Library resolves the ID to executable memory, then correlate callback holders with actor holders in paired snapshots before any state write.
- The 2026-09-23 paired read-only detour resolved and ran on both installed builds: `authority-20260923-102804.json` recorded 28/28 sampled actor holders with a callback within 200 ms on each PC, while host/follower pose packets still matched at identical source ticks. A later sample saw 45/45 recent actor holders and **six different callback thread IDs** per PC. This is not a main-thread-only seam; any future receiver must use a thread-safe immutable pose mailbox, and direct ECS access from the hook is unsafe. `authority-20260923-102910.json` still showed zero matching evaluated-pose checksums across 12 overlapping actors and ~90-degree worst local rotation error, so the read-only detour did not itself improve visible parity.
- [Active Ragdoll's published graph layout](https://github.com/adamhynek/activeragdoll/blob/master/include/RE/havok_behavior.h) says `boneNodes` at +0x160 has one entry per animation bone. Our live read-only diagnostic already validates that array on this runtime. Adopt a bounded post-graph probe comparing `poseLocal` with a few corresponding NiNode local transforms for one watched actor before deciding whether post-update writes belong in the Havok array or the render skeleton. A full per-bone `ReadProcessMemory` walk in the hook would be too costly for five players and is not justified by the current evidence.
- The first three-node post-update probe saw the follower's Havok pose change 14 times but those particular node locals never changed; that was ambiguous, not proof of no render propagation. The next bounded 10 Hz probe read all 99 mapped nodes for one watched actor. In `authority-20260923-103610.json`, both PCs watched the same form ID `0x000654ED`, with 99 pose bones/99 render entries and 96 valid render nodes each. The host saw 31 pose and 31 render-local changes over 62 samples; the follower saw 12 and 12 over 41 samples. World transforms changed more frequently (40/21) as parent movement propagated. Probe cost was 57/68 microseconds on the sampled frames. This supports a post-native visual update seam but does not yet prove that `poseLocal[i]` maps by identical transform values to `boneNodes[i].local`, nor that overwriting local nodes is safe across skeleton rebuilds.
- The paired one-shot `authority-20260923-103821.json` plus `Analyze-PoseRenderMapping.ps1` tested 266 index-matched Havok/render-local bone pairs. All 266 scales matched within 0.001, but only 132 translations and 145 quaternion-to-matrix rotations did. Indices 0, 5, 10, 30 and 98 usually matched; many indices 40–90 were identity render nodes while the corresponding Havok pose was non-identity. This is a concrete rejection of blindly writing `poseLocal[i]` into `boneNodes[i]`. For visual authority, capture the owner's actual `boneNodes` local transform (plus node-presence mask) and relay that; reserve the Havok pose for graph/ragdoll diagnosis until its binding and LOD behavior are mapped. The nonmatching nodes are not solved by an index permutation in the sampled data: their identity local transform often exactly matched `poseLocal[0]`, suggesting inactive/helper nodes or LOD/flattening semantics.

# Visual skeleton authority trial (2026-09-23)

- Owner render-bone locals now travel in a separate bounded `VisualBoneSnapshot`, with graph descriptor, source tick and node-presence mask, through the existing owner-checked server relay. In `authority-20260923-105331.json`, 17/17 overlapping same-source-tick visual checksums matched at the receiver. This proves packet fidelity only. Two actors captured per 100 ms movement update yields roughly one sample per actor per second in the intro, too slow for smooth playback or five-player scaling; a dedicated bounded higher-cadence visual stream is needed.
- A fixed-slot immutable mailbox accepts those packets off the network thread and exposes only the watched actor to the post-native graph-update worker hook. The read-only paired trial published 320 frames on the follower and accepted 108 without a graph-generation rejection; the last source frame was 718 ms old. The slot table still has a collision risk and is not production-ready for arbitrary actor counts.
- A default-off watched-actor write trial changed the receiver's `NiAVObject::local` for 96 mapped nodes per accepted frame, with graph/root generation and parent guards. In `authority-20260923-110008.json`, 33 frames/3,168 bones were written with zero API failures and zero immediate readback error; a single hook took about 3.9 ms. In the subsequent paired one-shot `authority-20260923-110041.json`, all 12 sampled render-local transforms for actor `0x000654EE` matched across peers, but whole-skeleton world checksums still did not match. The follower's evaluated Havok pose still diverged, as expected because only render locals were written. This is not visual or physics parity, and the trial was disabled after measurement to avoid freezing an NPC on stale frames.
- [CommonLibSSE-NG's `NiAVObject` implementation](https://github.com/CharmedBaryon/CommonLibSSE-NG/blob/main/src/RE/N/NiAVObject.cpp) exposes separate native `UpdateDownwardPass`, `UpdateWorldData` and `UpdateTransformAndBounds` virtuals. This supports a distinct world-transform propagation phase after local-pose evaluation. It does **not** prove that calling one of these methods inside the graph worker callback is safe, or that the observed world checksum gap is solely caused by missing propagation. Probe actor-root and sampled child world transforms first; only then trial a source-backed engine update at a verified phase.
- The new paired root/world probe in `authority-20260923-110634.json` sampled peers 43 shared-world-tick milliseconds apart. Passengers on cart `0x000B9DF3` shared the cart's exact reference/node world translation on each PC; that cart and those passengers were 127.14 game units apart across PCs. The second cart `0x000BB970` and its passengers were 159.06 units apart. Thus the world-skeleton mismatch is largely inherited from independently simulated moving cart roots, not just missing child-bone propagation. The cart's Havok body positions differed by the same amounts after the 70:1 unit conversion and its velocities diverged. A bone-local-only patch cannot fix this; source authority must include the parent/body/scene timeline.
- `VisualBoneSnapshot` now carries the owner's `rootNode.world` alongside bone locals, without applying it. The updated packet roundtrip passed 51 assertions across three encoding cases. A paired run `authority-20260923-111358.json` had 32/32 same-source-tick visual checksum matches and zero root-packet translation error, including the cart passenger `0x000654EE`. Its live follower root still differed in the preceding paired one-shot. This separates successful authority transport from failed local world simulation. The packet is still only an observation stream at roughly one sample per actor per second during the crowded intro.
- [CommonLibSSE-NG's pinned `bhkRigidBody` declaration](https://github.com/CharmedBaryon/CommonLibSSE-NG/blob/b93280e832f263dbef44e44cbe2936622a02f91a/include/RE/B/bhkRigidBody.h#L383-L399) names virtual slot 0x35 `SetPosition(hkVector4&)`, distinct from reference/node teleport. [Precision's public API](https://github.com/ersh1/Precision/blob/main/src/PrecisionAPI.h) exposes a callback immediately before `hkpWorld::stepDeltaTime`, confirming that physics-step phase matters for body playback. The project's existing position setter is still unverified on this runtime. Trial it only behind a default-off, single-selected-reference diagnostic switch, measuring pre/post body error and sample age before considering a pre-physics engine hook or broad authority. A position-only trial cannot prove cart rotation, constraint, passenger, or camera parity.
- The default-off single-reference trial selected cart `0x000B9DF3` at runtime only as a diagnostic example. On follower build `00569477...`, virtual slot 0x35 accepted 59/59 calls without an immediate crash; the last measured write reduced target-body error from 66.3 to 15.9 game units. But in paired samples the follower's **reference-to-body** separation grew from the pre-trial 12.2 units to 145.9, then 31â€“58.5 units while the host stayed near 11.3â€“11.6. At the same moments cross-PC cart-reference errors ranged 39â€“208 units and camera error rose as high as 439 units. The body and visible reference are driven through different native phases; correcting the body alone breaks their local relationship and is rejected as the universal fix. The switch was disabled immediately after measurement. The paired evidence is `authority-20260923-111922.json`, `-111954.json`, `-112007.json`, `-112008.json`, and `-112009.json`.
- [SKSE's vanilla `ObjectReference.psc`](https://github.com/ianpatt/skse64/blob/master/scripts/vanilla/ObjectReference.psc) lists `TetherToHorse`, `TranslateTo`, `SplineTranslateTo`, and `SetPosition` as distinct native movement paths, while [CommonLib's `TESObjectREFR::SetPosition` implementation](https://github.com/CharmedBaryon/CommonLibSSE-NG/blob/main/src/RE/T/TESObjectREFR.cpp) calls `MoveTo_Impl`. This supports tracing reference/scene-driver phase separately from Havok body writes. It does not establish which native method drives every cart frame; that still needs observation. The next generic probe should record reference/node/body update order for selected moving references before an authoritative writer is enabled.
- [CommonLibSSE-NG's pinned `TESObjectREFR` virtual layout](https://github.com/CharmedBaryon/CommonLibSSE-NG/blob/b93280e832f263dbef44e44cbe2936622a02f91a/include/RE/T/TESObjectREFR.h#L1660-L1668) identifies `Update3DPosition` at slot 0x3F and `MoveHavok` at slot 0x85 for SE/AE. A transparent, selected-reference-only trace of both calls can reveal whether either function is in the moving-reference path, without assuming that their names cover every direct engine write. An absent callback while the reference moves is meaningful only for those two slots; it is not proof that the reference is not moving natively. [CommonLib's `NiAVObject` update virtuals](https://github.com/CharmedBaryon/CommonLibSSE-NG/blob/main/src/RE/N/NiAVObject.cpp) are a separate render-scene phase that may need its own trace if these reference hooks do not cover the cart.
- The paired default-off hook trial (`authority-20260923-112725.json`) installed both `TESObjectREFR` hooks on each machine and observed **zero** selected-cart calls while cart reference positions moved. A second paired trial (`authority-20260923-113133.json`) hooked its render node: `UpdateWorldData` and `UpdateTransformAndBounds` ran 184/184 host and 44/44 follower, while `UpdateDownwardPass` remained zero. This rules out those two reference methods as the per-frame cart update in the sampled segment and confirms active native node-update callbacks on both PCs; it does not identify the actual writer of `TESObjectREFR::position`.
- The strengthened `authority-20260923-114204.json` trace resolved the same native sites on both machines: node `UpdateWorldData` target RVA `0xEEBF40`, `UpdateTransformAndBounds` target `0xEE2B70`, their callers `0xEE2B9E` and `0x66C1E5`. Both the reference and node positions changed over the measured callback interval. A local `dumpbin /disasm` of the installed `SkyrimSE.exe` at `0xEEBF40` shows copies into `NiAVObject::world` (`+0x7C`) and `previousWorld` (`+0xB0`) but no direct write to a `TESObjectREFR` position. The coincident reference delta is therefore **temporal evidence, not causal proof** that this method wrote it; another thread or nested/native driver may update it during the same interval. The selected trace was disabled after observation. A hardware data watchpoint on the reference position is a better next diagnostic than further guesses about named update methods.
- [Microsoft's debugger exception documentation](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/debug/debugger-exception-handling) and [`WaitForDebugEvent` contract](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/api/debugapi/nf-debugapi-waitfordebugevent) describe first-chance single-step events and the required continue loop. [Thread context APIs](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/api/_processthreadsapi/) expose debug registers on suspended threads. Adopt a bounded, disposable, local-process hardware write-watch utility for the exact `TESObjectREFR::position.x` address, with `DebugActiveProcessStop` on exit. This may stall the host during the observation window, so do not treat the paired scene after attaching as an acceptance run; use it only to identify writer instruction pointers. Reject permanent per-frame debugger attachment or page-wide guards as production authority mechanisms.
- The one-time bounded hardware watchpoint recorded 22 writes to cart `0x000B9DF3` in three seconds, all at Skyrim 1.7.104 RVA `0x2F0F91`: `TESObjectREFR` position x receives a new value from the caller-supplied vector. The debugger detached, but a subsequent dump `SkyrimTogether.exe.53412.dmp` showed a leftover `EXCEPTION_SINGLE_STEP` at the same RVA. **This external watchpoint method is unsafe for live testing and was removed**, including its generated executable. The crash is diagnostic-tool-caused, not evidence that the game authority patch crashed. Do not revive the debugger tool without a formally correct all-thread teardown and isolated test process.
- The existing `Actor::HookSetPosition` already detours the native setter at relocated ID 19790. A default-off, selected-form read-only trace in that hook was paired-tested without debugger attachment: `authority-20260923-115632.json` and its follow-up both reported caller RVA `0x2F4754` on host/follower, with 134/89 calls in the sampled run and form type 61 for the cart. The disassembly at `0x2F4745` passes the vector at caller register `r13+0xA0` to native setter `0x2F0EF0`. The callsite is inside a larger path reading the cart's current Havok/body and performing other updates, so intercepting just one coordinate or directly teleporting the body is not a universal fix. The safe next observation is to identify the source object's owner/lifetime and the post-call scenegraph/physics ordering through transparent in-process hooks, then design one authoritative state application at that boundary. The paired trace was disabled after capture; both game processes remained alive, but cart reference error was still about 146 units, so **1:1 parity is not achieved**.

# Native scenegraph source and recurring crash (2026-09-23)

## Body-state transport preparation

- [Precision's public API](https://github.com/ersh1/Precision/blob/main/src/PrecisionAPI.h) exposes a callback immediately before `hkpWorld::stepDeltaTime`. This shows that a distinct pre-physics phase can be intercepted by a legitimate open-source mod; it does not prove that writing a constrained Skyrim body there is safe. Continue clean-room observation of the installed game and public sources, without importing leaked proprietary code.
- The existing physics packet contained reference position/Euler rotation and linear velocity, but not the Havok body's orientation or exact transform. That discards state before follower playback can be evaluated, especially where body-to-node pivot is nonzero. Add the host's 16-float native body transform to dynamic-body packets only, retain it in the follower's timestamped pose buffer, and roundtrip-test it. This is transport preparation, **not** a parity fix: do not apply the transform until pre-step ownership, constraints, and body-to-node ordering are proven in a paired trial. Measure the 64-byte per-dynamic-body increment against the five-player baseline before broad use.
- Build `32352E31...` and matching server `FAC47F37...` were deployed to both PCs. The first automated campaign-start command timed out while the local game was still initializing; retry succeeded and both clients entered one campaign/epoch. At `authority-20260923-123854.json`, 19/19 overlapping visual-pose packet checksums matched, but the watched carts still had 292.12 and 184.44 game-unit reference error and the parity gate failed. The local game remained responsive. Encoding tests passed 49 assertions, and the server protocol suite passed including a new exact body-transform relay check plus five-player session smoke. This verifies packet transport and launch stability, **not** physics or visual parity.

## Native Havok-step ordering and cart-body delta
