## 2026-09-24 executable corpus correction to ControlMap diagnostics

- The previous `controlMap.enabledFlags=1` readings above were from **the wrong offset** and cannot establish movement state. The copied AE executable's `ControlMap::ToggleControls` (VA `0x140CEFAA0`, Address Library ID `68545`) reads/writes the effective flag mask at `ControlMap+0x120`; `ControlMap::StoreControls` (VA `0x140CEFB30`) and `ControlMap::LoadStoredControls` (VA `0x140CEFB50`) use `+0x124` as saved mask, with `0x80000000` meaning no saved mask. The offset difference from [CommonLibSSE-NG's header](https://ng.commonlib.dev/_control_map_8h_source.html) is independently described in [its AE layout issue](https://github.com/CharmedBaryon/CommonLibSSE-NG/issues/111). The local exe's exact version is the authority here; a similarly built runtime must be confirmed before generalizing.
- The copied exe's `Papyrus_Game_DisablePlayerControls` (VA `0x140A23EE0`) calls `ToggleControls` with a mask constructed from Papyrus arguments; movement contributes `0x401` (movement and jumping). Its enable counterpart is VA `0x140A23FC0`. [CommonLibSSE-NG's `USER_EVENT_FLAG`](https://ng.commonlib.dev/UserEvents_8h_source.html) maps these bits to movement, looking, activate, menu, console, POV switch, fighting, sneaking, main four, wheel zoom, jumping, and VATS. `game_snapshot` source now reads both actual masks and decodes those bits; no controls are changed by this patch.
- `sk.cmd callers ControlMap::ToggleControls` found 13 direct callers, including both Papyrus natives and `Actor::SetLifeState` (VA `0x140693200`). The decompiled `SetLifeState` stores controls then disables mask `0x424` upon player bleedout, and calls `LoadStoredControls` upon leaving bleedout. **Mask `0x424` does not include movement**; therefore this particular bleedout toggle does not directly explain the user's movement lock, although a stale stored mask could still matter. This repo's `PlayerCharacter::RespawnPlayer()` does not visibly call `SetLifeState`, but another engine method may do so indirectly. No respawn cause or recovery is proven without a live before/after capture of `+0x120/+0x124` and life state.
- Independent Reviewer A and Reviewer B reviews of this diagnostic caught the risk of treating an unreadable mask as zero, the misleading old wrapper name, and the unsupported implication that bleedout mask `0x424` disables movement. The source now emits JSON `null` for unreadable raw masks, cross-references the legacy `EnableOtherEvent` wrapper, and the paragraph above explicitly rejects that causal shortcut. Reviewer B also warned that the offsets and flag labels are runtime-specific; the client currently supports only Skyrim `1.7.104.0`, and the next live capture must validate mask behavior on that build before using it in a fix.

The old follower implementation includes an opt-in pre-step one-object kinematic probe; it does not yet provide general owner-driven Havok parity. New mode 4 is source-only and opt-in. ## 2026-09-25 windowed position is remembered

- `Renderer::Init` (VA `0x141007B70`, Address Library ID `77226`) creates the game window with `CreateWindowExA` at `ApplicationWindowProperties::iX/iY` (the `iLocation X/Y:Display` settings), so `Hook_Renderer_Init` sets those from the saved origin before calling it. Only a windowed launch is changed; borderless and fullscreen keep the engine's placement.
- The saved origin is `[SkyrimTogether] iWindowX/iWindowY` in SkyrimPrefs.ini, written when the player finishes moving the framed window (`WM_EXITSIZEMOVE`, or `WM_WINDOWPOSCHANGED` outside a drag for keyboard snaps). Programmatic mode switches, minimised and maximised windows are not recorded. It is reused only while its title bar would still intersect a monitor (`MonitorFromRect`, `MONITOR_DEFAULTTONULL`), otherwise the old centring applies.
- `GameSettingsService::OnMainLoop` runs only from the settings timer, and killed that timer in the same pass that finished a resize, leaving `m_programmaticDisplayChange` set for the rest of the session. Every later player move and resize (`OnWindowSizeChanged`) was then ignored. The timer now runs until that guard clears.
- Measured on the host PC (5120x1440): window moved to 300,120; borderless preview gave 0,0 5120x1440; back to windowed gave 300,120; a relaunch opened at 300,120.

## 2026-09-25 title menu layout at every resolution

- Measured, not assumed: `MainMenuIntegration.cpp` now writes a numeric layout snapshot to `logs/ui-layout.jsonl` 4 s after the Main Menu opens and 3 s after each live resize: every live Scaleform movie's viewport, scale mode, alignment, visible and safe rects, the on-screen position and scale of Main Menu / Loading Menu / LoadWaitSpinner parts (`GFxMovieView::TranslateLocalToScreen`, vtable 0x41; its user matrix is multiplied in unconditionally by `FUN_140d039e0`, so it must be an identity, not null), and the title logo camera. `C:\Tools\skyrim_re\ui-layout-matrix.ps1` launches the game fresh at each size (the reference) and then live-resizes to it from another size; `ui-layout-diff.py` lists every differing field.
- `BSScaleformManager::LoadMovie` (ID 82325, VA `0x14116FDA0`) sets the scale mode, a safe rect from the visible frame (4:3 threshold on the `BSGraphics::State` aspect), the viewport (optionally letterboxed by global 411491), then invokes `_root.InitExtensions` (variadic invoke, ID 82664). Menus pin widgets to the screen edges there with `GlobalFunc.Lock`, once. The shipped `Data/Interface/startmenu.swf` re-locks itself when `Stage.visibleRect` changes, so the Main Menu matched native at 1280x720, 1920x1080, 1680x1050, 1280x960, 2560x1080, 3840x1080, 1664x1056 and 5120x1440 borderless, in both directions.
- The one measured mismatch: the Loading Menu and LoadWaitSpinner stay alive between loads but are not in `menuStack`, so the live refresh left their viewports (and edge-locked widgets) at the launch size. The refresh now walks `menuMap` and re-runs those two menus' own `InitExtensions` while hidden. After the change all ten cases report 0 differences.
- Title logo: `UI3DSceneManager::SetCameraFOV` (ID 52742, VA `0x14098B1D0`, called only by the Mist Menu) fixes the horizontal half-width at `tan(fov/2)*0.75` and derives the vertical from the aspect, so the logo is twice as tall at 32:9 as at 16:9 and changes size with every window shape. A hook keeps the 16:9 vertical extent on wider screens (widening the view instead) through `NiCamera::SetFrustum` (ID 70626); 16:9 and narrower keep the engine's framing. Measured: camera vertical half-extent 0.08392 at 1920x1080, 3840x1080 and 5120x1440, 0.11189 at 1280x960, native and live identical.
- `UI3DSceneManager+0xF8` is a saved pre-menu camera state restored by the Mist Menu destructor (`FUN_14098afe0`), not the frustum the logo is drawn with (`NiCamera+0x150`).

## 2026-09-25 overnight two-PC session: Steam session, server launcher, intro carts, follower launch race

- **Local server never started.** `SkyrimTogetherServer.exe` refuses a `STServer.dll` from another build (`CheckBuildTag`, `Code/server_runner/main.cpp`), and both deploy scripts shipped only the DLL: the launcher on disk dated from 2026-09-22 against a 2026-09-24 DLL, so every session logged `Disconnected from server 0` ~10 s after start and hosting was impossible. The launcher is now built (`SkyrimServerRunner`) and deployed with the DLL; a fresh launcher printed `Server v1.8.2-24-g2d9d5fcd started on port 10578`.
- **Steam invites.** Accepting an invite / Join Game raised `GameLobbyJoinRequested_t` (333), `GameRichPresenceJoinRequested_t` (337) and `LobbyInvite_t` (503) with nobody listening; the lobby is invite-only, so friends never see it as joinable either. `SteamLobbyService` registers a `CCallbackBase`-compatible adapter via `SteamAPI_RegisterCallback`, invites directly (`ISteamMatchmaking::InviteUserToLobby`), sets rich presence (`connect` = `+connect_lobby <id>`) and honours that launch argument. Test bridge: `steam_state`, `steam_invite`, `steam_answer_invite`. Verified host invite -> follower accept in-game -> one party (`C:\Tools\skyrim_re\invite-join-test.ps1`).
- **Intro carts.** Follower cart `0xBB970` stepped 100-117 game units in single 16-ms frames under its own Havok simulation (earlier entries). Reviewer C's keyframed trial wrote Havok body poses into a keyframed body, but a keyframed body follows its scene node, so the pose was overwritten each step (the ~1,578-unit drift). `ObjectService` now makes a host-streamed moving body keyframed and drives the *reference* transform (`Update3DPosition`) plus the Havok body at the host's body offset, from a 12-sample ring rendered 100 ms behind the newest sample; a body the host stops sending holds its last pose (restoring local physics after 1.5 s snapped cart `0xB9DF3` ~14,700 units back to its stale body). Measured over the ride (`cart-samples-fix2.jsonl`): follower peak per-frame step 53 units, 0 large steps; cart `0xBB970` gap median 16.4 / p95 27.6 / max 93.8 units. Open: after arrival cart `0xB9DF3`'s horse drifted ~1,400 units on the follower and the cart followed.
- **Follower stuck in the void after the menu idled.** Reproduced twice with a 6-minute main-menu idle: the follower's new game ran (Papyrus `AddItem`, HUD Menu shown) but the Main Menu stayed open, the player at `(2048,2048,0)` with no cell. The follower log shows the leader's `MQ101` (`0x3372B`) stage 10 update arriving ~2 s *before* the follower's own `StartNewGame` (`FUN_140959160`, ID 52118, which calls `TESQuest::EnsureQuestStarted` on `0003372b`) reached that stage, after which `HookNativeSetStage` suppressed the follower's own stage-10 write. Stage 10 moves the player into the cart, so the world never loaded. `TESQuest.cpp` now allows a follower stage write the leader already reached in the current epoch (`QuestService::HostReachedStage`). Separately, the main-menu launch now invokes StartMenu.as's own `FadeOutAndCall("StartNewGame"|"ContinueLastSavedGame")` natively (GFx variadic invoke, ID 82664) instead of selecting a row, calling `NEW` and waiting for `MAIN_CONFIRM_STATE`; Reviewer C's read-only review (`NEW`, VA `0x140958F70`, only requests `ConfirmNewGame`; `CharacterSelection` only follows a LOAD request) informed this. The `Launch watch:` log line records the menu's ActionScript state each second for 90 s after a launch.
- **Follower cart stranded at a cell border.** With host-driven playback the follower's cart kept its start cell, so when that cell detached the cart and the player riding it unloaded about 10,000 units short of Helgen. `TESObjectREFR::MoveTo` (ID 56626) into the new cell made it worse (7,000+ unit jumps per crossing): for a non-actor it disables and re-enables the reference, reloading its 3D and Havok body. The engine's own path for a Havok-moved reference (ID 19826) looks up the loaded cell under the new position and calls ID 19799 `(ref, nullptr, worldspace)`, which picks the cell with the same `floor(pos/4096)` grid and does not reload anything. Using that: follower cart largeSteps 0, gap p95 26 units, 30 logged crossings along the road (the 9867/9868 back-and-forth appears for both carts at the same place, so the road really crosses that border twice), and both players arrive in Helgen 0-4 units apart.
- **Parity after arrival (MQ101 stage 41, `authority-20260925-040137.json`).** All 30 matched actors within 4 units, one Headsman, no shared network IDs. Still failing and persistent over a repeat capture: scene `0x4679B` playing only on the follower; Tullius (`0x2BFA2`) still seated on horse `0x654E1` on the follower (118 rejected mount applies); worn-armor counts differ for the cart drivers `0x654F5`/`0x654F6` and prisoner `0x654FB` (host 3-4, follower 0) and in the other direction for `0x654F0`; root/nested geometry children differ for `0x527F1` and the Headsman. The pose probe had no overlapping actors, so bone and ragdoll parity were not measured in this run.

## 2026-09-25 reference mods for ragdolls, paired animations, physics objects and inventory

- Cloned read-only to `C:\Tools\ref`: [PLANCK / activeragdoll](https://github.com/adamhynek/activeragdoll) (GPL-3), [Paired Animation Improvements](https://github.com/Exit-9B/PairedAnimationImprovements), [Open Animation Replacer](https://github.com/ersh1/OpenAnimationReplacer), [HIGGS](https://github.com/adamhynek/higgs), [SkyMP](https://github.com/skyrim-multiplayer/skymp) and a newer Skyrim Together fork, [TiltedEvolution-rwf](https://github.com/rfortier/TiltedEvolution-rwf). Only ideas and engine facts are taken from them, not code. Anything reused later is credited in the code as well.
- PLANCK drives a live ragdoll towards the animated pose through `hkbRagdollDriver::driveToPose` and `postPhysics`, using the rigid-body and powered ragdoll controllers. It controls how hard each one pulls with the controllers' on-fractions (`TRACK_RIGID_BODY_RAGDOLL_CONTROLS`, `TRACK_POWERED_RAGDOLL_CONTROLS`, where an on-fraction above 1 blends). It keyframes bones by setting the rigid-body controller header's `m_onFraction` above 1. It adds or removes a ragdoll with `BSAnimationGraphManager::AddRagdollToWorld` and `RemoveRagdollFromWorld`, and resets the driver with `hkbRagdollDriver::reset`. Its offsets are for SE 1.5.97; the matching 1.7.104 functions are being mapped from the corpus (vtable `hkbRagdollDriver` at rva `0x19c07b0`). The idea for co-op is that the follower's corpse copy drives its ragdoll to the host's streamed pose with the engine's own controller, instead of setting body velocities by hand.
- Paired Animation Improvements hooks the synchronized-clip path (AE IDs 63048 `synchronizedClipActivate`, 63050 `synchronizedClipUpdate`, 63942 `CreateSynchronizedClipBindings`). That is where killmoves and the Helgen execution bind two actors to one clip. It is the candidate place to identify a paired kill on the host and replay it on a follower.
- `RaceSexMenu` begin-closing (ID 52388, VA `0x14096B7D0`, found by Reviewer B) switches the menu out of its editing state, which stops the preview idle. `CreatorTogether` now skips it while players are still editing and runs it on release.


## 2026-09-25 party load-door voting (round 5)

Scope: story/dungeon teleport doors only. Free interiors retain the existing per-cell simulator. No form-ID lists. Research below was read before implementing the hook; no live probes or xmake build were run. COMMON.md explicitly delegates this round's independent review/build to the coordinator and excludes local Reviewer A/Reviewer B review; neither is claimed here.

Sources inspected:

- [CommonLibSSE-NG TESObjectDOOR](https://github.com/alandtse/CommonLibSSE-NG/blob/ng/include/RE/T/TESObjectDOOR.h), [ExtraTeleport](https://github.com/alandtse/CommonLibSSE-NG/blob/ng/include/RE/E/ExtraTeleport.h), [BGSLocation](https://github.com/alandtse/CommonLibSSE-NG/blob/ng/include/RE/B/BGSLocation.h), [TESQuest](https://github.com/alandtse/CommonLibSSE-NG/blob/ng/include/RE/T/TESQuest.h), and source `src/RE/T/{TESQuest,TESObjectCELL}.cpp` in the read-only `C:/Tools/ref/CommonLibSSE-NG` checkout. Adopted teleport-extra detection, handle resolution, destination-cell native location lookup, parent-location keyword traversal and native quest target resolution. Rejected hardcoded door IDs and generic interior voting. LocTypeMine alone does not qualify; LocTypeClearable independently does. Keyword editor IDs are resolved once after game data is available; missing keywords are skipped.
- [TiltedEvolution-rwf ObjectService](https://github.com/rfortier/TiltedEvolution-rwf/blob/main/Code/client/Services/Generic/ObjectService.cpp), local checkout and this fork's `ObjectService::OnActivate/OnActivateNotify`, plus `TESObjectREFR.cpp`. The existing event occurs at activation, and remote replay uses a remote actor. Rejected implementing the hold in ObjectService: the native activation would already have run. Added the hold at the beginning of `HookActivate`, before both the event and original call. Accepted votes replay via the existing `TESObjectREFR::Activate` wrapper, which calls RealActivate with ScopedActivateOverride and avoids duplicate network activation.

Offline 1.7.104 corpus inspection (`C:/Tools/skyrim_re/sk.cmd`):

| Native function | Address Library ID | VA | Finding used |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| TESObjectREFR::ActivateRef | 19796 | 0x1402F11B0 | Existing detour; native default/script processing must remain untouched on bypass paths. |
| PlayerCharacter::ActivatePickRef | 40548 | 0x140751580 | Calls ActivateRef at 0x14075168D, returns at 0x140751692 (+0x112). This call site, plus the local-player activator, identifies actual player input. |
| TESObjectDOOR::Activate, vtable slot 0x37 | 17922 | 0x140283210 | Vtable 0x141818268. Checks teleport data and lock state before native travel; key unlock can recursively activate. Locked activations stay entirely native. |
| TESObjectREFR teleport-data accessor | 20216 | 0x140301090 | Delegates to the extra-data accessor. |
| ExtraDataList teleport-data accessor | 11688 | 0x1401644B0 | Gets extra type 0x2B, returns its +0x10 DoorTeleportData pointer; its first word is the destination reference handle. |
| TESObjectCELL::GetLocation | 18905 | 0x1402BB180 | Resolves encounter-zone location, then cell/worldspace location fallback. Use the native accessor instead of assuming loaded-cell layout. |
| BGSLocation::InitItem | 18354 | 0x1402996B0 | Resolves parent location at +0x48. CommonLib provides the keyword-form subobject at +0x30. |
| TESDataHandler::AddFormToDataHandler | 13693 | 0x1401B6A70 | Form arrays start at +0x10 with stride 0x18; type 4 is keywords (+0x70). Iterate this array and compare editor IDs. |
| TESQuest::GetAliasedRef | 25066 | 0x1403D8590 | Resolves an alias through the native locked alias map; no form-ID guessing. |
| TESQuestTarget::GetTargetRef | 25284 | 0x1403E2BE0 | Resolves the objective target's alias against its quest. BGSQuestObjective targets/count are +0x10/+0x18; displayed state is 1. |
| ShowHUDMessage | 52933 | 0x140991A30 | Reused the existing three-argument CreatorTogether HUD notice pattern. |

The Papyrus activation wrapper at VA 0x140A446E0 calls ActivateRef with the same player/zero arguments as direct input. Therefore testing only the activator or default-processing flag would also hold script-driven doors. The audited direct-call return address is intentionally specific to runtime 1.7.104; it needs revalidation when porting to another executable. Non-player and other script callers pass through unchanged.

Authority audit: `Code/server/Services/CharacterService.cpp` EnforceLeaderAuthority transfers only unknown-cell actors or actors in the leader's range. CanClaimOwnership lets a follower claim a known-cell NPC when both leader and previous owner are out of range, with valid ownership epoch. `CellIdComponent::IsInRange` requires identical interior cells. Thus a follower alone in a known shop interior may own/simulate its NPCs, and the leader outside does not pull them back. Unknown-cell actors and mounts retain their existing special rules. This is source confirmation, not a paired test of trading, buying or smithing persistence; those existing inventory/state paths were not changed.

Loading audit: PartyService::ReachWorldReadyBarrier requires session state 1 and m_waitingForWorldReady. Its gameplay barrier requires state 2. They do NOT rearm for normal door loads during state 3. DoorVoteService therefore adds its own arrival barrier only for committed vote doors: after the native destination cell is attached, player 3D exists and Loading Menu is closed, each client holds one UI pause count and acknowledges Loaded. The server validates the destination cell and releases after all original members acknowledge. Loaded retries cover ordering against normal cell replication. Cancel, party change, disconnect, activation failure and a bounded timeout release this service's pause count. Free doors never acquire this gate.

Protocol: server state is per party, with a monotonically increasing vote generation, epoch, original membership, leader, initiator, canonical source door/cell/position, destination, ready set and loaded set. Duplicate ready packets are idempotent. The server validates current party membership, epoch, source space and replicated position within 400 units. Replacements, stale withdrawals/votes, initiator departure, member departure and timeouts cannot retain an old scheduled activation. Any party membership/leader change cancels the generation (including dropping the departed player's vote), requiring a fresh vote. A ready player who leaves loses their vote; the remaining party can vote again. All-ready broadcasts one server-clock tick 1000 ms ahead. Door classification and the initial door anchor are client-supplied engine facts, not independently recomputed from server plugin records; server sender/proximity checks do not make those facts cheat-proof.

Validation and next-run evidence:

- Standalone MSVC syntax checks pass for both new services, message translation units, the minimal activation hook and server registration. Client World.cpp hits an existing concurrent CorpseRagdollService::RemoteRagdoll extended-alignment error in hopscotch_map; it is outside this task's edit scope.
- The isolated test harness in `C:/Users/mwalt/AppData/Local/Temp/door_vote_check_padptwbu` passed 818 checks. It compiles the actual serializers and server DoorVoteService.cpp against simulated World/Player/clock adapters, not a second implementation of the state machine. It covers bounded packet decoding, truncation at every byte, request/notification directions, five/eight players, duplicate votes, stale epochs/generations, outsiders, movement withdrawal, replacement, membership change, shared-tick rescheduling, destination acknowledgments, failure cancellation and expiry. It is not a test of actual transport timing or the engine.
- On both PCs, expect `Door vote: classify ... VOTE (reason)`, server `started by ...`, `vote 1/N` through `N/N`, identical `go at tick ...`, then each client's `activated ... at tick ... (shared ..., result ...)`, `loaded, holding world gate`, and server `all N players loaded`. Compare actual activation ticks and observed load starts, not just the planned tick.
- Test leader and follower initiation; a non-initiator walking over 400 units away; initiator departure; switching doors; disconnect/join/kick/leader change during countdown and loading; a late withdrawal; a locked/key door; a script invoking Activate with the local player; a plain animated door; and a follower alone in a shop while the leader remains outside. Test cave/clearable locations and parent keywords, a mine without Clearable, both ends' running quest aliases, displayed objective targets, and leader control lock. Repeat with five clients before making performance claims.
- A committed native activation cannot be rolled back if another client fails or a cancellation arrives after its scheduled tick. The service cancels/releases the barrier on failure; the observed party may need regrouping. Scheduling is the first client update at or after the shared tick, not a real-time guarantee under network stalls. Custom scripted doors can decline or delay the original activation. No changed build was exercised in game, so no in-game fix or performance target is claimed.

## 2026-09-25: party player down / revive / safe fallback

Research before implementation:

- [CommonLibSSE-NG Actor.cpp](https://github.com/CharmedBaryon/CommonLibSSE-NG/blob/main/src/RE/A/Actor.cpp), also inspected locally under C:/Tools/ref/CommonLibSSE-NG: SetLifeState maps to AE Address Library ID 37612. Adopt the native transition, rather than writing ActorState bits, so the owner's camera and saved controls are restored. Current maximum health is current health minus its damage modifier, including temporary health buffs.
- [Acheron Defeat.cpp](https://github.com/KrisV-777/Acheron/blob/master/src/Acheron/Defeat.cpp), DefeatActor and RescueActor: inspected the actual source, including the BleedoutStart/BleedoutStop graph-event fallback and no-recovery flag. Adopt these graph events for remote presentation and the recovery transition. Do not adopt Acheron's pacification, package overrides, or blanket control enabling: this service must preserve combat authority and existing scripted control locks.
- [CommonLibSSE-NG ControlMap.cpp](https://github.com/CharmedBaryon/CommonLibSSE-NG/blob/main/src/RE/C/ControlMap.cpp), GetMappedKey, plus the local ControlBindings implementation: read gameplay mappings for keyboard, mouse and XInput Activate instead of hardcoding E. R is a separate, edge-triggered give-up action; menu, focus and script-control gates prevent background activation.
- The existing RespawnPlayer selects the centre-grid cell and COC placement outdoors; this is the source of the unwanted placement. Preserve its behavior only for solo play and an interior without a recorded safe position. Party exterior fallback uses the last safe point in the current cell, or the latest recorded cell in the same worldspace. If none exists, recover in place; never invent an exterior destination.

Offline executable inspection (SkyrimSE.exe 1.7.104):

| VA | Address Library ID | Finding and use |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 0x140693200 | 37612 | Actor::SetLifeState: life states 7/8 are bleedout, 0 is alive. Crossing bleedout dispatches the default start/stop action. For the player, entry stores controls and disables 0x424; exit stops the bleedout camera and loads the stored controls. Used on owner and remote copies. |
| 0x1406C7C50 | 38533 | SetNoBleedoutRecovery toggles boolFlags bit 5. Clearing it calls recovery processing only outside combat, so clearing it alone is insufficient for a deterministic revive while the downed actor remains in combat. |
| 0x1406D1F40 | 38680 | Recovery helper excludes life state 7 before calling the health-restoration helper. This reinforces using explicit SetLifeState, not a health-only recovery. |
| 0x140A0D6D0 | 54945 | Papyrus Actor.IsInCombat dispatches Actor vtable +0x718 (slot 0xE3). Existing Actor::IsInCombat uses this native wrapper. |
| 0x140991A30 | 52933 | ShowHUDMessage: use the existing native Skyrim notice path, as in CreatorTogether::ShowNotice. |
| 0x1406747F0 | 37243 | Actor::IsInMidair gets the character controller and reads its context at +0x1E0. It tests only state 2, insufficient to distinguish jumping/swimming from grounded. |
| 0x140722110 | 39856 | AIProcess character-controller getter returns middle-high process +0x250, or null. |
| 0x140B97880 | 62427 | Character-context state getter reads +0x20. CommonLib hkpCharacterStateTypes confirms state 0 is OnGround. Safe tracking explicitly requires that state. |
| 0x1402BEE50 | 19002 | TESObjectCELL::GetWaterHeight(cell, position, outHeight) accounts for cell and local water volumes. Safe tracking requires water height at or below the player's feet. |
| 0x140CF1470 | 68570 | Native control remapping traverses 0x18-stride mappings, with key at +8, consistent with ControlBindings and CommonLib. Read only. |
| 0x140CEFAA0 | 68545 | ControlMap::ToggleControls updates the live mask and optionally the saved mask. Inspected; deliberately not used to blanket-enable controls on recovery. |

Implementation and validation:

- ReviveService replaces only the party branch of PlayerService::RunRespawnUpdates. Down notices have no fade or movement. An alive, non-downed party member in the same space within 5000 units resets the ten-second no-help timer, even while that member is fighting. A facing, loaded target within 200 units can be revived by holding the mapped Activate for three seconds outside combat. Releasing, moving out of range, menus, focus loss, combat, or changing target cancels the hold. R gives up immediately. Safe positions require three continuous seconds grounded, dry, alive, and outside combat; loading/menu gaps, cell changes and large jumps restart the sample timer.
- The server identifies the sender, checks current party/epoch, replicated cell/worldspace and distance, reported combat/down/alive state plus its own character-dead flag, and three seconds of continuous hold heartbeats. Reports expire after 3.5 seconds and holds after one second. A grant is sent only to the downed owner, for its exact down-state revision; it does not declare the actor recovered. The owner's subsequent state report reaches all party members. Periodic state reports also cover late joins and actors whose 3D loads later. Existing player-kill guards were not changed.
- Safe fallback retains crime-gold payment, PlayerRespawnRequest gold loss, spell/shout re-equipping, and spell dispelling. A normal revive restores 50 percent max health in place without the fallback gold charge. Legacy solo respawn code and post-death logic are retained; an old solo fade/knock countdown is cancelled when the party service takes over.
- Standalone MSVC /Zs checks passed for both services, PlayerCharacter.cpp, PlayerService.cpp, both World.cpp registrations and both message factories. The actual new serializers passed 229 assertions: six round trips, every byte truncation, invalid direction/action and uint32 overflow rejection. The actual server service passed 27 assertions against five simulated players: owner-only grant and acknowledgement, missing/early hold, combat/down/dead/alive checks, 200/201 distance boundary, same-worldspace exterior adjacency, interior/worldspace mismatch, party departure, hold cancellation/expiry, movement interruption, duplicate completion, stale down revision and stale party epoch. Harnesses are in C:/Users/mwalt/AppData/Local/Temp/coop-revive-check (protocol.cpp, server-test.cpp, response files and executables). These are isolated protocol tests, not a five-player performance measurement or engine test.
- COMMON.md delegates review to the coordinator for this round and explicitly skips Reviewer A/Reviewer B. No independent assistant review is claimed. No xmake build, commit, deployment, or paired game run was performed. Existing run4 logs do not exercise this new flow.

Next paired run: down host and follower separately; observe both copies kneel at the original position with no fade. Verify `Revive: <name> down`, `Revive: revive started by <name>`, then `Revive: revived by <name>` only after a complete eligible hold. Observe actual get-up, camera and control restoration and health at 50 percent on the owner. Cancel by releasing Activate, moving either player beyond 200, entering combat, opening a menu and losing focus. Test remapped keyboard/mouse/controller Activate. Leave an alive teammate within 5000 for more than ten seconds, then move away, change interior, disconnect, and down everyone. Confirm `Revive: fallback respawn at last safe spot (<reason>)` and actual destination; test R, dry/grounded sampling, water/jump/combat rejection, exterior-cell boundaries and the no-safe interior/exterior alternatives. Check spells and crime/gold steps, then repeat down/revive without reconnecting. Verify a late join and remote 3D reload while down. Repeat with five real clients before profiling or making performance claims.

Remaining risks: behavior graphs (including beast forms), existing remote action/pose replay, native recovery during ragdoll, and activation of a bleedout actor need paired observation. Combat and down reports are authenticated owner reports, not independently simulated by the server; distance uses replicated transforms. Network stalls can interrupt holds and delay state notices. Per-cell safe history is session-local, not persisted to saves. No in-game fix is claimed until the changed coordinator build is exercised.

## 2026-09-25 round 5: false vampirism message and repeated startup quest failures

Scope: investigated `2c7de32c..6fd24b64`, patched only client `QuestService::OnQuestUpdate`, and recorded this research. No build, commit, deployment, or live game mutation. `COMMON.md` explicitly delegates independent review to the coordinator and skips Reviewer A/Reviewer B for this round; neither is claimed as a reviewer.

### Primary references and executable evidence

- [TiltedEvolution-rwf TESQuest.cpp](https://github.com/rfortier/TiltedEvolution-rwf/blob/a7d061564338ab7ff475d6919a19c42664589611/Code/client/Games/Skyrim/Forms/TESQuest.cpp), inspected in `C:/Tools/ref/TiltedEvolution-rwf`: `ScriptSetStage` filters the current stage unless explicitly forced. Our own `2c7de32c` `TESQuest::ScriptSetStage` returns when `currentStage == stageIndex || IsStageDone(stageIndex)`. Adopted that existing idempotence rule, without restoring the asynchronous Papyrus call or changing the protocol. Web searches for Skyrim Together quest sync and vampire script implementations preceded the patch; local primary source and installed game data provided the decisive evidence.
- [CommonLibSSE-NG TESQuest.cpp](https://github.com/alandtse/CommonLibSSE-NG/blob/a898f469851c464d05137bb74b069dd234897643/src/RE/T/TESQuest.cpp), inspected in `C:/Tools/ref/CommonLibSSE-NG`: distinguishes `EnsureQuestStarted` and `ForceRefIntoAlias` from stage execution. Do not assume a running quest has executed its current-stage fragment.
- Executable **ID 25004, VA 0x1403D0DC0**: native SetStage requires Enabled, searches the authored stage list, and invokes the stage item even when `currentStage` already equals the requested stage. Missing stage returns false. It only raises `currentStage` if the new number is greater. **ID 25252, VA 0x1403E19B0**: the stage-item routine tests/sets the done bit and dispatches its items; initialized `currentStage == 0` does not imply stage 0's done bit is set. **ID 25003, VA 0x1403D0CB0** handles quest startup separately. **ID 25052, VA 0x1403D4FA0** implements ForceRefIntoAlias, consistent with CommonLib's ABI.
- Installed `Skyrim.esm` QUST **0005C625 (VC01)**: DNAM type **8 (SideQuest)**, stage **0** maps to `QF_VC01_0005C625.Fragment_10`. Decompiled the installed BSA's PEX using the existing Champollion executable. The fragment unconditionally calls `Debug.MessageBox("You have contracted Vampirism.")`, then `Self.SetStage(50)`. It does not test disease or change race. Thus this exact popup does not establish that the player became a vampire. `PlayerVampireQuest` **000EAFD5** is a different quest (type 0, authored stage 10).
- Verified the decompiled PEX inputs byte-for-byte against `Skyrim - Misc.bsa`. SHA-256: `qf_vc01_0005c625.pex` = `b628486c992f5e74e6af1d41e9977c8bde59b4954a5cb9de27d55c50c3641766`; `playervampirequestscript.pex` = `b40eef2525e0c6a7d25b772d6b4ddcbec7f5a5f4fb8922465b78306deaeacd87`. Bethesda-derived scripts remain outside the repository, in `<local scratch>/round5/`.

### Observed chain and who retries

Read-only follower log copy: `<local scratch>/round5/regression_follower.log`. Session boundaries matter:

- Previous session begins at line **5545**, `22:05:34.510`. The ten reported quests have **1,473-1,490** `Remote quest started` attempts apiece. For example, CWObj appears at lines **6038..38179** and MGRitual03 at **6070..38181**. Consecutive CWObj attempts include `22:08:49.981`, `22:08:50.098`, `22:08:50.213`, matching the follower's 100 ms retry cadence.
- Latest session begins at line **38361**, `22:09:11.494`. Each reported quest appears **once**, followed by the `update did not apply; dropped` safeguard already added in `6fd24b64`. CWObj: lines **38788-38792**; MGRitual03: **38797-38801**. The available latest session therefore does not substantiate continuing ten-quest retry spam after that safeguard.
- Latest session **38671-38673**, `22:09:39.825`: `Native quest stage enter form=5C625 from=0 to=0 ... override=true`, successful return, then `Remote quest started: 5C625, stage: 0`. At **38813-38814**, `22:09:39.864`, its fragment's local `0 -> 50` request is suppressed. This exactly matches Fragment_10. The host's current `tp_client.log` has one VC01 start event at **26327**, and no VC01 stage event in this session. Host/follower wall clocks differ; compare events within their own sessions.
- Host's preceding session has one start event each for CWObj (**24760**) and MGRitual03 (**24766**), not 1,480 events. `OnEvent(TESQuestStartStopEvent)` sends a current-stage snapshot via `SendAliasFills(..., &update)` once per event. Periodic alias sampling has `HasStage == false`. Server `OnAliasFills` checks sequence and calls `ProcessQuestChanges`, which commits a transaction and sends one notification; neither contains a resend timer. The pre-`6fd24b64` follower `ApplyPendingAliasFills` retains a failed revision, and `OnUpdate` retries every 100 ms. **The follower's own pending queue produced the repeated lines**, not host events or the server relay.
- Reading the installed ESM's INDX records confirms **none** of `29940`, `99F27`, `1C48E`, `1DA3C`, `240B8`, `2D512`, `39645`, `6A086`, `A4E82`, `B3233` has an authored stage 0. Native SetStage therefore cannot succeed for these requests; their initialized current stage is nevertheless 0.

### Cause, rejected alternatives, and change

`4b4151ff` replaced both receive-side `ScriptSetStage` calls with `IsStageDone(stage) || SetStage(stage)`. That omitted ScriptSetStage's **same-current-stage guard**. Startup snapshots now execute an unexecuted stage-0 fragment (VC01), or fail forever if no stage 0 exists (the ten quests). `54cb558f`'s None/Misc filtering cannot exclude VC01, which is SideQuest. `6fd24b64` removed forced startup and infinite failed-stage retries but still invokes native stage 0 on already-running VC01.

Restored `currentStage == incomingStage || IsStageDone(incomingStage) || SetStage(incomingStage)` in **both Started and StageUpdate cases** of `QuestService::OnQuestUpdate`. Both are needed because the server converts Started to StageUpdate when the quest is already in its ledger. Equal-stage snapshots are accepted and their revision recorded without calling the native stage writer. Changed, unfinished stages retain the existing alias preparation and native application. Kept the existing failed-update drop safeguard. No quest IDs, quest types, timer tweaks, or protocol changes were added.

Other changed paths were inspected, not patched: remote player spawn allocates a fresh TESNPC with `IFormFactory::Create` in `Games/Forms.cpp`; Initialize borrows default data but does not return the player's base. CreatorTogether excludes local form 14 and requires a remote-player extension. Its SwitchRace (**ID 37925, VA 0x1406AD970**) targets that actor; native player-specific operations test the player singleton. Revive's recovery/respawn paths require a downed player; the latest log has no Revive transition. RespawnPlayerAt's DispelAllSpells is confined to fallback respawn. **SetLifeState ID 37612, VA 0x140693200** handles life/bleedout/camera/control state. The new Decapitate/CreateHead hooks (**IDs 37639/37640, VAs 0x140697C50/0x140697D10**) concern the target's detached head. BSScript.cpp's diff only gates diagnostic logging/stack inspection and still forwards the original native call and arguments. TriggerGate holds host trigger delivery, while door votes and PartyService input changes do not account for this exact VC01 fragment chain. Rejecting these as the cause of the observed popup follows the direct script/log evidence, not a claim that their behavior has been exhaustively validated.

### Validation and next paired run

Offline evidence script `round5/check_quest_regressions.py` checks session counts, ESM stage definitions and BSA identity. `git diff --check` must pass. No compilation or changed-build protocol/in-game tests are claimed: the user prohibited building, and paired testing belongs to the coordinator. Wire encoding and server behavior are unchanged.

On a fresh paired new game, confirm no vampire message on the follower. A single `Remote quest started/updated: 5C625, stage: 0` is harmless and may remain, but there must be no remote native `5C625 from=0 to=0` call or resulting local stage-50 attempt. The ten quests should be accepted without `Failed to update` / `did not apply; dropped` lines or 100 ms repetition. Confirm a real changed MQ101 stage (10, 20, etc.) still runs once with its aliases and later repeated notifications do not rerun it. Check both Started and server-converted StageUpdate, stale revisions, and a new epoch. Also test legitimate vampire acquisition/cure later; this change does not suppress their scripts globally.

Risk: this intentionally restores the pre-regression same-stage suppression, including for repeatable stages; explicitly forced same-stage replay remains outside this protocol's semantics. It does not repair quests or fragments already executed in an affected save, and it does not solve pre-existing deferred/missing-stage delivery limitations. Use a fresh game for confirmation. No five-player performance or in-game fix claim is made.

### Sources inspected and decisions

Its remote-player damage early return and disabled hit-driven target lock also exist here. The group stores per-target detection, stealth points, attacked member and attacker count. Keep native selection and group bookkeeping; do not copy the disabled approximate scoring implementation from our debug view. Use native `IsCombatTarget`, which takes the group read lock, rather than iterating its mutable target array ourselves.
- [Papyrus Extender detection implementation](https://github.com/powerof3/PapyrusExtenderSSE/blob/master/src/Game/Manager.cpp) and [Detection functions](https://github.com/powerof3/PapyrusExtenderSSE/blob/master/src/Papyrus/Functions/Detection.cpp): inspected `C:/Tools/ref/PapyrusExtenderSSE/src/Game/Manager.cpp` and `src/Papyrus/Functions/Detection.cpp`. - Existing local `CharacterService` already replicates factions, inventory and actor values; remote spawn marks a player copy essential and initially adds PlayerFaction. It never calls `SetPlayerTeammate`, and later faction snapshots can replace the initial faction set. Existing `CombatTargetServerId` resolves to a local form and is published through `PresentedCombatTargetFormId`; extend that existing presentation authority rather than inventing another target-selection stream. `ActorValueService` already transports value 92 (MovementNoiseMult, including Muffle) and 54 (Invisibility), but full detection inputs and native player perk entries were absent. `MagicService` routes invisibility through spell-cast sync; the new snapshot transmits its actual owner AV result as well, without recasting spells or running perk abilities on a copy.

| HUD stealth message | 0x140935340 | 51623 | Queues native HUD message 0x18 with level and sneaking flag. Replace only the fresh follower level, retaining native visibility and animation. |
| Actor DoDamage | 0x1406797C0 | 37335 | Difficulty and killmove scaling, combat bookkeeping, native health write and death checks. Owner executes this exactly once, then publishes observed health delta. |
| Controller damage bookkeeping | 0x140560F50 | 33224 | Stamps native recent-damage time via state+28; replay for the host NPC when owner reports its attack. It is not an invented numerical threat score. |
| ActorKnowledge state assignment | 0x1407C3FC0 | 42704 | Existing hook only prevents remote-player-to-local-player detection; it does not cancel NPC-to-copy detection. Left unchanged. |

Native detection still performs LOS, distance, observer skill, alertness, weather/ambient-light adjustment, target race sound level and perk condition evaluation on the host. The snapshot supplies the owner's sneak result, moving/running speed categories, speed sample, illumination, equipped weight, current Sneak/Invisibility/MovementNoiseMult/SneakMod/SneakPowerMod and the ordered active entries for the four detection perk entry points. Perk entries are addressed by shared mod/form identity plus entry index, bounded to 512 entries. The host visits the same entries through native perk evaluation with the actual detecting NPC as context. No perk ability, quest entry or permanent NPC base is applied/modified. This is why modded target-dependent entry-point conditions retain their native evaluator instead of being approximated with an owner-side numeric perk multiplier.

### Changes and authority

- `Code/client/Services/Generic/CombatService.cpp`, `RunTargetUpdates`: every 200 ms, inspect high-process host NPCs and current party-player snapshots. Only an attackable, positively detected opponent is offered to native StartCombat. An NPC already fighting a party member may consider other detected party members; otherwise require native hostility. Existing target membership is checked with the locked native query. No fixed focus time, round-robin assignment, forced host preference, StopCombat or target-score patch.
- `Code/client/Games/Skyrim/Combat/CombatController.cpp`, `HookNativeSetTarget`: all mirrored NPCs respect resolvable presented targets. Preserve existing trial counters and leave host-owned selectors native.
- New `Code/client/Games/Skyrim/Combat/PlayerCombat.h/.cpp`: native detection input hooks, thread-local detection context, locked snapshot cache, native active-perk-entry capture/replay, teammate setter preserving can-do-favor, native awareness and combat-search eye sampling, and HUD message interception. Expired snapshots and eye levels fall back to native behavior after two seconds.
- New `Code/client/Services/Generic/StealthService.h/.cpp`: 5 Hz full owner snapshots; per-player host state and eye return; bind/rebind by native weak handle; restore teammate state and clear caches on expiry/disconnect/session or leader changes; role/ownership/sequence checks; send damage and threat runner events. `World.cpp` registers it and `World.h` declares it.
- `Code/client/Games/Skyrim/Actor.cpp`: added `HookAuthorityDamageActor` around the unchanged old damage hook, and `PlayerCombat::ApplyDamage`. Host-owned NPC hitting remote player sends raw pre-difficulty damage. Follower suppresses its duplicate remote-NPC collision. Player owner calls native DoDamage, samples before/after health and emits the existing HealthChangeEvent. Owner's attack against a mirrored NPC retains the existing NPC health path and sends a separate attribution event; host adds that aggressor through native StartCombat and updates recent-damage bookkeeping. ActorProcess and existing detection-state hooks were not edited by this task.
- New `Code/encoding/Messages/PlayerCombatState.h/.cpp`, `RequestPlayerCombatState.h`, `NotifyPlayerCombatState.h`: four bounded payload kinds (stealth, detection, damage, threat), session epoch, monotonically increasing sender sequence, character IDs and both ownership epochs for impacts. Strict checked reads, finite/range limits, duplicate perk-entry rejection. Opcodes are appended and both factories register the messages without replacing other engineers' additions.
- `Code/server/Services/CombatService.cpp`, `RelayPlayerCombat`: validates active same-party session and current ownership; owner-only player snapshots/attacks; leader-only detection and NPC damage; real player character identity; impact cell interest; per-kind/per-actor epoch-aware replay rejection. Routes privately to the leader or affected owner. Relay sequence state belongs to the relevant ECS entity, not a global two-player slot. Existing projectile relay is unchanged.

A search of that session for targeting-trial, combat-target, sneak and stealth diagnostics returned zero matches. It cannot substantiate either targeting distribution or stealth correctness. No changed C++ protocol tests, compiler checks, paired observations or five-player profiling were run. The user prohibited building. No Reviewer A/Reviewer B review is claimed.

Coordinator protocol tests still required: round-trip all four kinds including negative detection and 512 entries; every truncated payload; NaN/Inf and out-of-range fields; duplicate perk entries; missing/zero session/sequence/ownership epoch; replay and reordering; wrong-owner and wrong-leader requests; unrelated parties; stale victim/source epochs; NPC vs real-player ID spoofing; cross-cell impacts; leader/session changes; late snapshot/spawn order and actor rebind. Check both factories, not only direct state decoding. All PCs and the server need the same new protocol build.

### Next paired run and remaining risks

1. Look for one `Player combat registered copy=... server=... epoch=...` per newly bound player. Every five seconds, `Player stealth server=...` reports that player's own sneak/movement/light/skill/noise/invisibility/entry count, detection level and eye level. Compare two differently skilled/equipped players, then five players. A follower's eye must match that player's host log, not the host player's eye.
2. With debug logging, `Player combat candidate npc=... player=...` should occur when adding a detected candidate, not recur endlessly for already registered opponents. Use equal-distance exposed players, then move one nearer, attack from one, block LOS to one, and crouch one. Observe chosen targets on both PCs. Vanilla scoring does not promise an exactly even target count at every instant.
3. For one NPC swing/projectile/effect, confirm a single `Player combat damage seq=... attacker=... victim=... raw=...`, one actual owner health loss and one health publication. Check multiple difficulties, block/armor, invulnerability, bleedout and revival. Hit the NPC from the follower and confirm `Player combat threat...` on the host and native combat response. Exercise concentration/area/periodic effects separately; coverage of every modded damage entry path is not established by inspecting DoDamage.
4. Crouch before joining; change cells/save/load; reconnect; move out of interest; transfer leadership; clear invisibility/muffle mid-combat; enter/exit sneak while stationary. Snapshot startup/expiry and the existing movement presentation delay can temporarily misalign stealth input and rendered position. This implementation does not add a new spawn-ready barrier. No in-game guarantee is made for that window.
5. Shared perk forms and entry order must match across machines. Script conditions that explicitly compare against the singleton PlayerRef still see a remote NPC copy; the implementation intentionally does not replace the engine player singleton. Existing host-vs-NPC special cases for crime/dialogue/combat remain native. Native threaded hooks and coexistence with other detection mods require an actual changed-build run.
6. Correctness precedes optimization. Profile five-player update cost, snapshot/per-detection copying, perk evaluation and reliable traffic after the above works. The implementation has per-player state with no two-player cap, but no five-player performance claim. No effects or native enemy AI were re-enabled through HookActorProcess.

### Change inventory and behavior

- `Code/client/Games/Skyrim/DialogueListenHooks.{h,cpp}`: `ObserveMenu` installs only the live DialogueMenu's AdvanceMovie vtable slot; `Capture` copies the ordered visible native topics under the topic lock and reads the Scaleform highlight on the UI thread. `HookClick` records chosen text/index and a serial, including choosing the same row again. `HookHide` records subtitle removal. `HookMenuInput` consumes listener cancel input. `Presentation::Begin/Update/End` saves first/third-person state, rotation, FOV and individual input-handler flags, holds movement/look/activation/attacks, frames the head, and conditionally restores owned state. It yields on camera/FOV/control takeover.
- `Code/client/Services/Generic/DialogueListenService.{h,cpp}`: public Begin/End facade; `OnUpdate` publishes only changed content, emits closure, checks party/epoch/distance/combat/death/unload/menu exit and applies presentation. `OnSubtitle` attaches native duration; `OnState` accepts only the selected party speaker/NPC/session, requests a current server snapshot for late entry and times out an unanswered Begin after three seconds. `PushUI` subtracts elapsed network-clock time from subtitle duration for late listeners. An old NPC broadcast is ignored while awaiting a different NPC.
- `Code/client/World.{h,cpp}`: include, registry registration and getter, with minimal edits alongside other engineers' registrations.
- `Code/encoding/Messages/DialogueListenState.{h,cpp}`, `RequestDialogueListen.{h,cpp}`, `NotifyDialogueListen.{h,cpp}`: full-state/change-only protocol with epoch, session/revision, canonical form and server NPC IDs, ordered topic native indices/text/said flags, highlight, chosen text/serial, subtitle text/serial/start tick/duration and FOV. Decode bounds: 64 topics, 512 bytes per topic/chosen text, 4096-byte subtitle, 300-second duration, valid FOV and highlighted index. Truncated/overflow varints and oversized arrays/text invalidate the packet. This bounds the worst valid payload below the existing 64 KiB transport buffer. Very large mod-added lists/text are clipped at capture.
- `Code/encoding/Opcodes.h` and both message factories: appended opcodes and visitor registration; existing entries retained.
- `Code/server/Services/DialogueListenService.{h,cpp}` plus `Code/server/World.cpp`: authenticated speaker identity, same-party/epoch validation, server NPC/cell/position validation, one active speaker per NPC per party, retained current snapshot, nearby broadcast to arbitrary member count, and remembered revisions after closure. At 250 ms intervals it sends snapshots to newly nearby members and closes streams/recipients on party change, disconnect, death, unload or range loss. Speaker range allows 600 units; listener range is 400. A Query cannot acquire a conversation or mutate gameplay.
- `Code/client/Services/Generic/OverlayService.cpp` and its actual existing header `Code/client/Services/OverlayService.h`: `PushDialogueListen` only marshals listener state to CEF; it does not activate the interactive overlay.
- `Code/skyrim_ui/src/app/components/dialogue-listen/dialogue-listen.component.{ts,html,scss}`: standalone, pointer-transparent component with existing Skyrim font, right-side translucent topic panel, grey said rows, highlighted native index, 650 ms chosen-row flash, auto-scroll to highlight, subtitle expiry, and Tab/B hint. Text is 15 px or larger. Angular interpolation keeps remote text out of HTML execution. `app.module.ts`, root HTML and `typings.d.ts` only register/mount/type this component.

### Checks and next paired run

Completed without generating a build: `tsc --noEmit --project tsconfig.app.json` and Angular `ngc -p tsconfig.app.json --noEmit` pass. Scoped `git diff --check` passes. No xmake/client/server/UI build, no commit, no protocol binary execution, and no in-game test was performed. These checks do not establish C++ compilation, native hook correctness or working multiplayer presentation.

Coordinator protocol checks before deployment: round-trip both messages with empty/maximum lists and non-ASCII text; reject truncated packets at every byte, counts >64, text >limits, overflowing integers, invalid FOV, duplicate/out-of-order topic indices and missing highlighted entries. Verify party isolation, forged publishing Speaker ignored, Query cannot publish, old revisions after close ignored, old sessions ignored, late listener snapshot, and two simultaneous conversations in a five-player party. These protocol tests remain required and were not claimed as passed under the no-build instruction.

Paired run: speaker opens an ordinary branching NPC conversation; a second player activates that occupied NPC after BusyLock integration. Compare topic order, said-grey state, mouse/controller highlight, repeated selections and chosen flash, each subtitle including skip, and one audible NPC voice per line. Join halfway through a long line and confirm it expires with the speaker. Test an initially stationary menu: no repeated `state sent` messages until content changes. Repeat with one speaker and four listeners, then two independent speakers and three listeners; no two-player limit is encoded.

Expected log lines: speaker `Dialogue listen: state sent (N topics)` on changes; listener `Dialogue listen: <localId> listening to <speakerId> with <NPC hexadecimal form>` once entry succeeds; `Dialogue listen: ended (<reason>)` after Tab/B, speaker closure, >400-unit displacement/NPC movement, combat/death, cell unload/load, disconnect, party change or camera/control takeover. Inspect existing native voice/replay logs alongside these; absence of duplication must be confirmed audibly, not inferred from agreement or source alone.

For each exit, verify movement, look, activation and first/third-person/FOV recovery. Test while a local script disables controls during listening, and with camera/UI replacement mods. Other code writing the same handler flag to the already-held false value cannot be distinguished by a value comparison; native ControlMap masks remain untouched, but direct handler writers need a paired coexistence check. Custom SWFs without the vanilla TopicList aliases yield no highlight. Camera framing uses the listener's position and head target, so it is not a pixel-identical spectator-camera copy; the fallback for nonstandard skeletons is an approximate head height. No five-player CPU, allocation or bandwidth target is claimed. Profile these only after correctness is observed.
Additional static verification: an in-memory execution harness for the actual listener component passed topic mapping (including gapped native indices and literal markup text), repeated-choice flash reset, subtitle expiry, close/disconnect cleanup and event teardown. No project build or emitted test files were used. A source check verified the 15 new files, exactly one registration per factory/service, and a conservative maximum valid packet estimate of 38,144 bytes versus the existing 65,536-byte send buffer. These are UI/static checks, not protocol round-trip or native validation.

## 2026-09-26: voted-door follower crash, first attempt (superseded by review revision below)

The exception is a null write at `0x140C09A9E`, inside **64677 / 0x140C097C0**, `hkp3AxisSweep::removeObjectBatch`. The faulting context (not the dump-handler context) unwinds through **61307 / 0x140B55500** (`hkpWorld::removeEntityBatch`), **64158 / 0x140BF4CF0** (`hkaRagdollInstance::removeFromWorld`), **63622 / 0x140BD3A00** (graph removal), **39897 / 0x140723FF0** (actor ragdoll cleanup), **37624 / 0x140696060** (process demotion), and **13316 / 0x1401A28A0** (`TES::SetCurrentCell`). The sorted removal index is zero; the selected node's handle is null. This establishes the failing invariant, not which producer invalidated the handle.

Inspected primary source references:

- [CommonLibSSE-NG hkp3AxisSweep.h](https://github.com/alandtse/CommonLibSSE-NG/blob/ng/include/RE/H/hkp3AxisSweep.h), plus local `hkpWorldObject.h`, `hkpBroadPhaseHandle.h`, and `hkaRagdollInstance.h`. Adopted the node stride 0x18, node handle +0x10, body world +0x10, body broadphase handle +0x44, and ragdoll body array +0x10 for interpreting the native code. Rejected treating the fault as a freed NiNode: the faulting instruction writes a Havok handle, not scene-graph memory.
- [PLANCK main.cpp](https://github.com/adamhynek/activeragdoll/blob/master/src/main.cpp), local lines 3438-3600, and [its behavior structures](https://github.com/adamhynek/activeragdoll/blob/master/include/RE/havok_behavior.h). Its add/remove lifecycle uses graph ownership and the cell's physics world. Adopted attachment and world ownership checks, not its physical animation controller or a quest-specific workaround. Exact-runtime **63621 / 0x140BD36A0** can insert a graph's ragdoll into its stored world, so a surviving 3D pointer alone is insufficient permission to call it during detach.
- Exact-runtime **77928 / 0x14104FCE0** and **77929 / 0x14104FD00** lock/unlock the bhkWorld at +0xC598 (conditional on the native locking flag). Revalidate membership after lock acquisition and reject the wrong expected stepping world before acquiring it. Apply UID, world, and motion checks under that lock. These close a source-visible check/use gap; the missing heap pages prevent attributing this particular crash to that gap.
- [Microsoft MINIDUMP_TYPE](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/api/minidumpapiset/ne-minidumpapiset-minidump_type). Adopted FullMemory, FullMemoryInfo, and IgnoreInaccessibleMemory. The supplied 1.1 GB dump has MemoryList but no Memory64List; its large game data section accounts for most of its size, and actor/ragdoll/world heaps are absent. Rejected inferring a full-memory capture from file size. Inspected local CrashLoggerSSE `src/Crash/CrashHandler.cpp` as a separate exception-context/minidump workflow reference.

Changes are generic corpse/detached-head attachment gating, retained-binding invalidation on detach, and post-lock world/identity validation in `CorpseRagdollService.cpp`, plus future heap capture in `CrashHandler.cpp`. No engine broadphase skip/null patch was made: bypassing removal after corruption can leave world arrays/constraints inconsistent. No change to door votes or files owned by other workers was justified by this stack.

Validation: both edited translation units pass MSVC `/Zs`; eight extracted-helper tests cover stable membership, detachment/migration during lock acquisition, partial migration, mixed bodies, empty input, wrong stepping world, and rebinding. No xmake build, commit, deployment, paired gameplay verification, or five-player performance measurement. The root producer and actor form ID remain unproven. Detailed stack/evidence: local job artifact `tmp/crash_door/unwind_door.txt` and `tmp/round6/report_doorcrash.md`. Independent assistant review was not performed, per this task's explicit COMMON.md exception.

## 2026-09-26: doorcrash independent-review revision

This supersedes the first attempt above, including its FullMemory recommendation and eight-test validation. The zero broadphase node's producer is still unknown. This revision fixes concrete lifetime/phase defects; it is not an observed in-game crash fix. The original dump settings are restored. Filename cleanup now accepts only regular files matching `crash_UTC_YYYY-MM-DD_HH-MM-SS.dmp`, instead of any path containing `crash`.

Primary references searched on the web and inspected locally:

- [PLANCK main.cpp](https://github.com/adamhynek/activeragdoll/blob/master/src/main.cpp), local lines 3438-3530 and 3573-3600: retains graph ownership, releases the graph update lock before world insertion, and uses the parent cell's retained world. Adopt the separation of graph discovery from world operations. ## Floating heads, heads round 2: refutation follow-up (2026-09-26)

Root-cause status: UNPROVEN. Detached clothing is reproduced in saved paired captures; neither the originating operation nor an in-game repair is established. This revision uses the user's explicit instrumentation fallback. No reset, equip, attach, root replacement, or gameplay workaround was added. COMMON.md exempts this job from starting the local reviewers; the entire existing `muse_review_heads_r1.log` was read and its concrete points addressed.

Primary-source research: searched the web again for Skyrim BipedAnim/armor attachment implementations, then inspected [CommonLibSSE-NG BipedAnim source](https://ng.commonlib.dev/BipedAnim_8h_source.html), the local `C:/Tools/ref/CommonLibSSE-NG/include/RE/B/BipedAnim.h`, and [HDT-SMP Hooks.cpp](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/aers/hdtSMP64/master/hdtSMP64/Hooks.cpp) (armor hook, lines 136-155). Adopted the documented layout and observation boundary. HDT's signature uses byte arguments in the two stack flag positions; its scene/physics modifications are not appropriate for read-only diagnosis. The installed 1.7.104 executable, rather than an older mod's offsets, decides our ABI.

ABI correction: `AttachArmor` argument SIX is a byte, not an opaque pointer-sized value. At 0x14021D00F and 0x14021D066, the only caller stores a BYTE at caller RSP+0x28, with arg five at +0x20 and arg seven (pointer) at +0x30. The decompiler's `undefined8 param_6` overstates the argument width. `TAttachArmor` and its detour now use uint8_t for both stack flags. There is no evidence the old declaration caused the captured floating heads (that trace was not in the capture build); this is an observer ABI correction.

Every current detour was checked with `sk.py fn id:<ID>` and all its direct call sites were inspected with Capstone against `C:/Tools/skyrim_re/SkyrimSE-1.7.104.exe`. The explicit `id:` prefix is necessary: a bare number can match a function name rather than an Address Library ID. Saved evidence: `heads-r2-native-*.txt`, `heads-r2-abi-check.py`, `heads-r2-abi-check.txt` under the job's `tmp/round6` folder.

| Engine ID | 1.7.104 VA | Signature / call-site evidence |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 15657 | 0x140217D90 | void(biped*, root*). Caller 24721 / 0x1403C26C0 calls at 0x1403C284A with RCX=biped, RDX=root. It writes biped+8, not armor attachments. |
| 15678 | 0x140218E50 | void(biped*, float, byte). Callee saves XMM1 and R8B; callers 24740 / 0x1403C4DC0 and 24741 / 0x1403C5400 call at 0x1403C5332 and 0x1403C552D with the weight in XMM1. Includes buffered-clone reuse. |
| 15701 | 0x14021BFA0 | void(biped*, pointer-sized model, armor*, int32 slot). Caller 13001 / 0x140192880, call 0x1401928B9: RDX loaded as qword; R8 loaded as qword; R9D=slot. Callee saves RDX into R14 and sign-extends R9D. |
| 15712 | 0x14021D170 | NiAVObject*(biped*, armor*, destination*, int32 slot, byte, byte, model*). Caller 15711 / 0x14021CE30 has calls at 0x14021D025 and 0x14021D07B; RAX is consumed as a pointer. Both flag positions are byte stores; seventh is a qword. |
| 15661 | 0x140218000 | void(biped*, BIPOBJECT*, byte clear, int32 replacement). Saves R8B and R9D. All direct callers checked, including RemoveAllParts 15659 / 0x140217E30 calls 0x140217EFD and 0x140217F0E, and LoadParts call 0x1402190BC. Can queue detachment and clear partClone before the queued detach executes. |
| 15660 | 0x140217F70 | void(const uint32 ownerHandle*, NiAVObject* clone). Reads DWORD [RCX], uses RDX as node; three immediate calls in 15661 and delayed call 0x140669CA7 in 36991 / 0x140669920. Calls parent's virtual +0x1C0 when clone+0x30 is non-null. Observing only 15661 would miss the actual time of queued detachment. |

The engine evidence expands the diagnostic boundary, not the causal claim. 15711's BSFade branch intentionally calls 15712 with destination=null. 15701's generic attach fallback is conditional on the part not being skinned. Neither a non-null returned clone, a skinned flag, nor destination=null alone establishes success/failure. The detached clothing captures cannot establish whether a missing destination, stale buffered clone/root, or later removal happened first.

The saved capture comparison was rerun. Both bad follower executions (153352/154107 and 170847/171516) miss slots 1,2,3,7 for FF000DB3 and FF000DB5. The 170847 creator capture already has the same symptom. Healthy control 163948/164620 uses the same proxy-retirement/reconciliation path and has attached clothing. In the bad 170847 follower log, native updates are lines 4278/4282 and handoffs are 4457-4463; the control has initial updates 25780/25789, extra updates 25890/25899, and handoffs 25907-25931. There are no SetActorInventory entries for the affected native copies in either path. See `CharacterService.cpp:605` / `:1133` / `:1161` and `Actor.cpp:701`. An unconditional Reset3D or AttachChild remains unjustified and would change working behavior.

Observer implementation: `ArmorAttachmentTrace.cpp` now uses fixed POD records published with release/acquire atomics. Producers make one reservation attempt and never wait for the consumer. They neither log, format strings, allocate, capture stacks, resolve references, nor mutate native state. All originals are called exactly once with preserved arguments/results even if recording fails. 15701/15712 and 15661 observe all valid slots; whole-biped 15657/15678 snapshots sample current and buffered slots 1,2,3,7. Parent sampling stops after ONE link. 15660 records clone, owner-handle value, before/after parent, thread, caller and QPC interval. Only integer values survive the callback; consumers never dereference them.

The 1024-record buffer recycles after draining rather than silently ending after 4096 startup events. `ArmorTrace gap dropped=...` explicitly marks incomplete history. `GameTestService::OnGameThread` drains once per tick, at most four events with a soft 1 ms check after each complete event; logger blocking cannot be strictly time-bounded. SetRoot/LoadParts use at most 22 ReadProcessMemory calls per complete before/after event; CompletePart uses at most 10, AttachArmor and populated RemovePart at most 11; DetachPart at most three. Reads include one 0x2778-byte biped copy per sample. No live performance claim: fixed storage is approximately 7 MiB and there is a disabled drain branch, so the old 'zero cost' wording is too broad. With the environment unset, no native hooks are installed and no per-frame scene scan is added.

Snapshots explicitly remain non-atomic. `rootUnchanged` is only an endpoint check, not synchronization. `DescribeActorVisuals` preserves existing fields and adds `treeReadable`, `treeComplete`, `rootUnchanged`, `bipedUnchanged`, `nonAtomic`; unreadable child entries/nodes and null roots invalidate completeness. Negative membership is inconclusive if traversal is incomplete, roots change, or concurrent child edits intervene. No engine locks/refcount changes were added just to obtain a coherent diagnostic.

Next paired run: set `SEAMLESS_ARMOR_TRACE=1` on BOTH actual game processes before launch and retain logs from launch. Require `ArmorTrace enabled version=2` with `perSlot=all rootLoadSlots=1,2,3,7`; correlate `biped` to actor_state, `seq` within each event, and captured `beginTicks/endTicks` across threads (log output is deferred and is not event order). Look for `event=SetRoot`, `event=LoadParts`, `event=CompletePart`, nested `event=AttachArmor`, `event=RemovePart`, and `event=DetachPart`. A detached result at completed load differs from a later DetachPart changing a previously populated parent to zero, or a root switch with unchanged buffered clones. A gap, missing native interval, ambiguous concurrent samples, or a scene detach outside these armor routines leaves the cause unproven. Do not compensate with a periodic attach/reset. Confirm any later source repair with complete paired actor_state and screenshots, not log receipt.

Validation: MSVC `/Zs` succeeded for ArmorAttachmentTrace.cpp, GameTestService.cpp, and World.cpp (the latter was touched to consolidate overlapping integration and finishes identical to the turn-start copy). `heads-r2-root-syntax.log` records exit 0; vcvars printed a vswhere PATH warning but cl ran all three units. A `git show 25fa030d` / `git ls-tree` preservation audit established that the trace files and DescribeActorVisuals were absent at the protected baseline; round-2 changes are confined to this task's diagnostics and the single drain call. No existing corpse, execution, intro, cart, trigger, inventory, or vote behavior was removed. No xmake, commit, full link, deployment, runtime hook exercise, or five-player measurement was done. Host and follower process queries returned no SkyrimSE process.

Round-2 whole-observer validation: `heads-r2-trace-test.cpp` includes the actual production observer and substitutes only native originals, startup registration, and logger. With real Windows ReadProcessMemory it passes all 42 per-slot paths, six-hook argument/result forwarding, current/buffered removal, actual-detach parent transition, invalid reads, callback logging exclusion, consumer native-read exclusion, saturation, in-flight record preservation, recycling, and five concurrent producers. `heads-r2-test.log` reports PASS. `heads-r2-syntax.log` reports successful MSVC /Zs for ArmorAttachmentTrace.cpp, GameTestService.cpp and World.cpp. `heads-r2-checked-inputs.json` records both exit codes 0 and matching SHA-256 inputs before/after checks; they still matched at the final source check. This validates observer mechanics, not native detour installation, paired gameplay, protocol behavior, or five-player performance.
