## In-engine harness and server startup diagnosis (2026-09-26)

This work preserves behavior at 25fa030d and does not modify CreatorTogether.cpp,
PlayerStateTrace.*, or BoundPoseKeeper.*. Other engineers' uncommitted changes remain.

### Session startup

A direct launch of the deployed server returned 0xC0000005. ProcDump captured
`round6/capture/server-startup/SkyrimTogetherServer.exe_260926_224512.dmp`.
STServer.dll+0x21504F reads address 4 with RCX=0. The matching build PDB resolves
this to EnTT any_cast/registry_context::emplace in World.cpp's InventoryService
construction. Its constructor recursively inserted NpcInventoryRelay into the
same context. Installed EnTT 3.10 dense_map::insert_or_do_nothing constructs the
mapped object inside packed.emplace_back before updating the bucket/return
iterator. Recursive insertion is invalid even without a capacity change.
Adopt service-owned unique_ptr relay with out-of-line destructor; preserve both
worn/loot subscriptions and all existing relay behavior.

Primary source inspected: [EnTT context](https://github.com/skypjack/entt/blob/v3.10.0/src/entt/entity/registry.hpp),
[dense_map](https://github.com/skypjack/entt/blob/v3.10.0/src/entt/container/dense_map.hpp).
Both local Reviewer A and Reviewer B independently confirmed this mechanism.
Earlier hypotheses of build mismatch, opcode factory initialization, and new
services crashing on first connection were rejected by matching deployed tags,
updated object timestamps and the dump. Missing log entries alone were misleading:
the file logger buffered its startup messages. Server startup succeeded after the
ownership fix; paired authentication and full scenario validation are tracked
separately in each run's report. Steam launch logging now records PID/path/cwd and
exit code; existing correct game-root cwd and build validation remain.

### Native actions, thread and focus

Primary source inspected: [CommonLib Script.cpp](https://github.com/alandtse/CommonLibSSE-NG/blob/ng/src/RE/S/Script.cpp),
[Script.h](https://github.com/alandtse/CommonLibSSE-NG/blob/ng/include/RE/S/Script.h),
[ConsoleLog.cpp](https://github.com/alandtse/CommonLibSSE-NG/blob/ng/src/RE/C/ConsoleLog.cpp).
Adopt native Script construction, SetCommand, CompileAndRun and destruction;
ConsoleLog is presentation, not command execution. Reject key emulation and
window-thread engine calls. Exact 1.7.104 corpus inspected:

- Script constructor ID441580 / VA140342A40 (0x80 bytes).
- Script::SetCommand ID21883 / VA1403435E0 (engine heap text ownership).
- Script::CompileAndRun ID441582 / VA140343D00 (script, compiler, enum1, selected ref).
- Script destructor ID441581 / VA140342AC0 (frees native allocations).
- Console::ExecuteCommand VA140909E00 queues a native script task; direct compile
  is used only at the proven main-loop tail. CommonLib's compiler is one byte.
- Main loop ID36564 / VA140658870; existing hook tail runs after native worker joins.
- Main::SetActive ID36606 / VA14065C310 coerces active=true when the byte at
  ID380768 / VA1420B58C0 (bAlwaysActive:General) is true. Existing TiltedOnlineApp
  already sets it during VM updates; harness also sets it from startup/main-loop
  and the launcher prepares the INI before starting an unfocused game.
- Existing driver reuses native PathToReference composition: IDs30875,37820,
  37822/37823,37801,91842, and native creator ChangeName ID52415. The existing
  corpus-backed driver is retained. Objective filtering now accepts the requested
  quest while retaining the existing default MQ101/MQ101DragonAttack behavior.

### Party protocol and captures

Primary sources inspected: [Valve reliable message contracts](https://github.com/ValveSoftware/GameNetworkingSockets/blob/master/include/steam/steamnetworkingtypes.h),
[upstream Skyrim Together party service](https://github.com/tiltedphoques/TiltedEvolution/blob/master/Code/server/Services/PartyService.cpp),
local DoorVoteService, WorldState decoder, and CommonLib event-source layouts.
Adopt the existing reliable transport plus application completion acknowledgments;
reject transport acceptance as proof an engine action ran. Harness packets have
bounded payloads, campaign epoch, run ID and ordered sequence. The server derives
sender identity, authorizes the leader, retains the original participant set, and
requires every member's completion. Default-off test compilation and runtime gates
exist on client and server. Membership/leader changes and timeouts cancel a run.
No two-player-only acknowledgment structure is used; five-member policy tests are
included, without claiming five-player runtime performance.

Reviewer A/B independently found synchronous path result handling, stale terminal
packets, start deadlines, final disk flush, numeric validation and door-vote identity
issues in the first draft. Those findings informed corrections before deployment.
Their agreement is design review, not paired runtime validation.

Engine waits receive quest-stage, cell attach/fully-loaded, scene, menu, trigger and
package events. Clock waits and movement progress are serviced on the main loop.
Captures copy four cart/horse positions and one armor actor at a time; no heavy
snapshots or screenshots. The main thread produces bounded JSONL records into a
bounded queue; a writer thread owns disk append/flush. Queue overflow invalidates
success. Body slot observations use the existing ArmorAttachmentTrace layout:
actor biped getter VA14073A760, slot stride0x78 and 42 slots VA140217E30,
CompletePart ID15701 / VA14021BFA0. Parent-chain walks are capped16 and explicitly
non-atomic. Trace hooks retain their existing behavior and gain a test-build-only
file opt-in. Absence within a bounded parent chain is a candidate anomaly, not a
complete visual-tree proof.

Scenario-specific markers belong to helgen-intro.json, not production sync logic.
`tcl`/same-worldspace `player.moveto` is only a recorded fallback for the tower jump;
a path failure alone is not proof the wall caused it. Door traversal must use the
actual automatic load-door path: TESObjectREFR::Activate replay wrapper bypasses
DoorVote, so explicit harness activation rejects doors. Success requires matching
DoorVote State/Go/Release and both destination cells, not a direct load/teleport.

### Harness startup correction (2026-09-26 23:08)
The first paired harness deployment exited before the bridge started. A ProcDump capture at `capture/harness-20260926-230631/SkyrimTogether.exe_260926_230806.dmp` resolves to libcef's initialization trap, then `CefParseJSON` (launcher RVA 0x4128fbb1), `HarnessService.cpp` Parse (0x40fdd7e5), and its constructor (0x40fd9dbf). This is a new harness initialization defect, not evidence of the Helgen door crash. CEF initialization happens later in OverlayService::Create. The service now defers JSON/settings/event setup until an atomic successful-CefInitialize publication; constructor and pre-initialization status use no CEF APIs. Reviewer A independently inspected the ordering and rejected an unsynchronized browser IsReady gate; the atomic publication adopts that correction. The capture queue also uses a bounded SPSC ring so writer mutex contention cannot falsely invalidate a run. Scenario stage 103 was corrected to MQ101DragonAttack after vanilla fragment inspection.

The corrected clients booted on both PCs in attempt `harness-20260926-231443`, and native harness status became enabled/idle. Session formation then exposed a separate unattended-only gate: OverlayService::Render invokes PollMainMenuOptions only when `titleScreen && focused`; that function drains Steam title tasks and pumps transport, while World::Update may be dormant at the title screen. HarnessService now invokes those existing pumps from its native-main-thread Tick while the Main Menu is open, only when the test setting is enabled. The native Main::SetActive code (VA 0x14065C310, Address Library 36606) was reread; it coerces focus state true with bAlwaysActive and resumes BSTimer. Both Skyrim.ini and the in-engine byte at ID380768 enable that behavior. No focus/key emulation is used.

Further unattended validation: `harness-20260926-231703` (50.3s) authenticated both PCs but rejected start because the harness UpdateEvent snapshot had not refreshed on the title screen. The title pump now refreshes that snapshot and drains acknowledgments; start waits up to90s for campaign readiness. Reviewer B required a single pump owner in test mode, matching the correction in PollMainMenuOptions. Native normal rendering is MainLoop36564/0x140658870 -> 0x1406565B0 -> Renderer::End77246/0x141009F70; utility/loading callers prevent claiming universal thread confinement from this chain alone.

`harness-20260926-232009` (60.1s) exchanged the first ordered step and both ACKs, but its retained CEF list was invalidated after the scenario parent dictionary was destroyed. CEF's primary [cef_values.h](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/chromiumembedded/cef/master/include/cef_values.h) specifies invalidation of owned child values when the parent is destroyed; retain an independent Copy. This was adopted for the step list, with explicit copies at parser/serializer boundaries. Factory tests now exercise all operations and every truncated prefix:368 assertions pass.

`harness-20260926-232246` passed the first two barriers and waited at creator, but LaunchSharedCampaignFromMainMenu silently returned: its cached s_pMainMenuMovie was set only by the focused poll. The launch function now resolves the live Main Menu movie before its unchanged native FadeOutAndCall path (native StartNewGame52118/0x140959160, existing source research). This attempt was explicitly stopped, not counted as a completed intro.

## 2026-09-26: Scale pass 1, behavior-preserving costs

Scope: the seven requested costs only. Read round6/COMMON.md (including SCALE), LOOP_AUDIT.md and muse_verify_loopaudit.log. The comparison floor is `25fa030d`. No xmake build, commit, live probe, counter, timing gate, action batching, aggro scheduling, corpse publication or scene-poll change. COMMON explicitly waives Reviewer A/B for this job; this work has no independent-assistant review. Other engineers' existing edits, including those in shared translation units, are retained.

### Inspected implementation references and decisions

- [Skyrim Together TransportService::Send](https://github.com/tiltedphoques/TiltedEvolution/blob/dev/Code/client/Services/Generic/TransportService.cpp), also the local client implementation: adopt scoped ScratchAllocator and reset after temporary objects die. The server arena is 2 MiB to fit the existing 1 MiB Buffer plus allocation metadata. One arena is shared by the send helpers on each thread. The 1 MiB logical packet capacity is unchanged. Reject merely resetting the old uninstalled 256 KiB allocator.
- [TiltedConnect Server::Send](https://github.com/tiltedphoques/TiltedConnect/blob/master/Code/connect/src/Server.cpp) and [PacketView](https://github.com/tiltedphoques/TiltedConnect/blob/master/Code/connect/src/Packet.cpp), inspected in Libraries/TiltedConnect: Send compresses bytes in place and changes the packet length; PacketView's constructor resets its envelope to kPayload. Preserve compressed bytes, length AND envelope in each broadcast cache. Cache lifetime is one common-helper broadcast, keyed by Player::GetStringCacheId. Preserve the original recipient traversal, exclusions, party/cell/range checks, and individual Send calls. No cache survives a message or crosses baseline groups. No change to StringCacheService's per-connection suffix generation. Recipient-specific movement construction in server/Services/CharacterService.cpp remains separate.
- [EnTT v3.10.0 signal storage](https://github.com/skypjack/entt/blob/v3.10.0/src/entt/entity/sigh_storage_mixin.hpp) and registry/container headers, inspected in the installed package: construct/update signals run after the change, destroy before removal; direct field assignments do not publish update signals. Adopt component lifecycle indexes and change the two direct RemoteComponent epoch assignments to patch(). Maintain the exact multi-component views, local/remote/object precedence, and original first-match traversal for ambiguous duplicate IDs. Reject an arbitrary winner for duplicate identities. Initialize at connection/use, outside service context emplacement (nested context insertion during a constructor would invalidate EnTT's dense-map insertion).
- Local `ServerReferencesMoveRequest`, `ReferenceUpdate`, `Utils`, and client ownership/spawn/despawn paths: movement currently has **no ownership epoch on the wire**. Index `(serverId, current ownershipEpoch)` and resolve the same live movement-view entity selected previously; do not invent an epoch-based packet rejection. Tests cover component replacement, ownership epoch patch, removal, entity reuse, duplicates, missing components and registry clear.
- Local TiltedCore StlAllocator and hopscotch erase implementation: keep discovery's existing Set and its mutation order. Mark observed IDs in persistent storage; collect only removals in the previous set's order before inserting additions, which might rehash. Native census and reconciliation retention remain unchanged. Reject swapping to an unordered container with different removal callback order.
- Native corpus `sk.py fn id:37758`: **VA 0x1406A2300, Address Library ID 37758**, follows the process/detection-state lookup and can return null. `fn id:41408`: **VA 0x140786350, ID 41408**, visits high-process handles, retains exclusions, takes maximum detection starting at -1000 and counts LOS. Keep GetAwareness's query/exclusion/order logic; accept a prebuilt observer vector. Stealth builds it lazily at the first eligible track and reuses it within that host tick. CombatService's separate 200 ms combat pass already builds once and stays separate to preserve its phase and StartCombat order. No native offsets or probes added.

### Before/after cost and unit-test table

P = players (or total connected players for PlayerManager); A = indexed actors; R = indexed objects; M = received movement records; B = follow probes/targets; H = high-process handles; C = eligible observers; T <= P-1 = stealth tracks; S = serialized message bytes; E(S) = encode cost; r <= P = recipients; G <= r = distinct string-cache baselines; Q = pending loot sends. Hash costs are expected, not adversarial worst-case.

| Item | File:function | Old cost formula | New cost formula | Test added |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | `server/GameServer.cpp:Send`, `SendToLoaded/Players/PlayersInRange/Party/PartyInRange` | r*E(S), r allocations of 1 MiB per message | Direct send reuses a 2 MiB thread arena; common broadcasts G*E(S) + O(r) dispatch, O(G*S) cache bytes/copy. Still r deliveries; uncompressible packets still enter the existing compressor per send. | Actual TiltedCore scratch and PacketView; reuse, empty recipients, 5/10 recipients, three interleaved baselines, fresh broadcast, compressed/uncompressed envelopes and send order (transport compression stand-in). |
| 2 | `server/Game/PlayerManager.cpp:Create/Remove/GetById` (+ header) | O(P) per lookup | Expected O(1) lookup/add/remove; O(P) index storage | Both const/nonconst lookup, duplicate connection, erase/reconnect, missing ID, ten players. |
| 3 | `client/Utils.cpp:EntityViewIndex/FindEntityByServerId/FindMovementEntityByServerId/Get*OwnershipToken`; `CharacterService.cpp:OnConnected/OnReferencesMoveRequest/OnOwnershipTransfer` (+ Utils.h) | O(M*A) movement lookup; Utils lookup O(A+R) | Expected O(M) movement lookup; O(1) utility lookup/lifecycle maintenance per affected index; O(A+R) persistent storage. Rare duplicate IDs intentionally retain O(A+R) first-match fallback. One bootstrap O(A+R). | Real EnTT 3.10, 10,000 differential lifecycle operations versus original linear views; duplicate precedence, partial views, epochs, bootstrap and clear. |
| 4 | `client/Services/Generic/DiscoveryService.cpp:VisitForms` (+ DiscoveryService.h) | O(H+A) census/diff plus full O(A) set copy each update | O(H+A) census/mark/diff retained; no full set copy; O(dA) candidate storage work and allocation only on growth | 1,000 randomized censuses against actual `25fa030d` VisitForms, comparing exact callback order and membership; duplicate handles, suppression, missing ProcessLists, reconciliation retention and rehashes. |
| 5 | `client/Services/Generic/ObjectService.cpp:OnMainFrame/ApplyRemotePhysics/OnUpdate`, `PruneFollowProbes` | O(B^2) prune every frame, plus O(B) unchanged telemetry | O(1) prune gate unchanged; expected O(B) prune only when body/target membership changes. Compare membership during existing target construction. O(B) telemetry remains. | Dirty false/true, retained/missing/new bodies, empty targets. |
| 6 | `client/Services/Generic/NpcLootService.cpp:OnUpdate` | Existing target work + Q*(copy+encode+send) under m_lock | Target work + O(1) queue handoff under lock; copies/encode/send outside lock; O(1) retry lock per failure. Preserve newly queued records; recycle drained capacity when idle. | Another thread acquires m_lock during each send; ordered sends, query failure, exact-snapshot retry, concurrent newer sequence and queued work. |
| 7 | `client/Services/Generic/CombatService.cpp:OnProjectileLaunchedEvent`; `StealthService.cpp:OnUpdate`; `Games/Skyrim/Combat/PlayerCombat.cpp:GetAwareness` (+ PlayerCombat.h) | O(A) per shot; T*O(H) observer collection + O(T*C) detection queries/tick | Expected O(1) per shot (same duplicate fallback as item 3); O(H) collection + O(T*C) unchanged detection queries/tick | Indexed form lookup differential test; awareness defaults, exclusions, null states, max/LOS and exact native-query order across ten uses of a prebuilt list. |

Tests live in `Code/tests/scale_cost_cases.inc`; `Code/tests/run_scale_checks.py` extracts the production helpers/functions so the tests exercise the edited logic rather than a second implementation. Command: `C:/Users/mwalt/AppData/Local/Programs/Python/Python312/python.exe Code/tests/run_scale_checks.py --syntax`. Compiler/test products go to a unique temporary directory. No harness files or shared build outputs are modified. Stand-ins supply engine/transport boundaries; actual EnTT, TiltedCore, hopscotch containers and PacketView are used. Production syntax checks cover all ten edited C++ translation units. Existing GameServer statistics conversion warnings are outside these edits.

### Validation limits and next paired run

The isolated tests and syntax checks pass. They do not validate an in-game fix, wire bandwidth or a five-player performance target. Existing unbounded native census/telemetry/detection loops keep their cadence in this pass; changing budgets would change behavior and is outside the user's authorization here. Common broadcasts still allocate proportional to distinct baseline payload bytes, and indexes add persistent O(A+R+P) memory.

Coordinator acceptance: exercise the changed build on both PCs and then five players. Compare join/reconnect, cell/range transitions, ownership transfer and stream diagnostics, repeated/same-tick animations, projectile delivery, stealth/aggro sequence, discovery during disable/enable reconciliation, worn publication/failure retry, and follow-body/cart logs. Keep the `25fa030d` corpse/head/body, intro/cart, trigger-gate and door-vote cases. No new log is expected from these optimizations. Measure CPU/allocation and network costs only on that validated build; ten-player tests remain a later scale target.

## 2026-09-27: non-actor graph events and native animation checkpoints (tower wall job)

Status: implementation and MSVC syntax validation, NOT a demonstrated in-game fix. Read COMMON.md, EVIDENCE.md (ATLAS CONCLUSIONS: TOWER WALL), ATLAS_B.md sections 4.4-4.6, and EXE_CORPUS.md before implementation. No xmake build, object/link build, deployment or commit performed by this job. COMMON.md explicitly waives the independent-review requirement for this worker; no Reviewer A/B review is claimed. Protected ObjectService.*, CreatorTogether and BoundPoseKeeper files were not edited. Other workers' changes and diagnostics remain intact; no behavior from 25fa030d was removed or disabled. Syntax validation is not runtime regression proof.

### Conclusion and rejected substitutes

REF 6CF54/base FURN 6CF46 has no enable parent or destruction stages. The installed wall graph has two branches: Behavior00 selects State02/playanim02; Behavior01 goes Closed -> Open -> Opened, whose OpenedFrame uses Open.hkx at fraction 1, speed 0. Open.hkx drives the skeleton containing Chunk02 through Chunk12. Begin occurs at clip start and can set stage 103 before completion. Neither a matching reference root, stage 103, enable state, nor a finished Ni sequence name proves the eleven moving chunks agree. The input-event recipient hook is below both Papyrus and native graph-manager callers; it does not assume the untraced Alduin-to-furniture edge uses one particular Papyrus wrapper.

Adopt generic graph input replication plus the engine's native saved graph data. Reject forcing MQ101 fragments/stages, hardcoding Open/playanim02/OpenedFrame or 6CF54 in production, directly writing the chunks, scanning scene nodes, and replaying all past input events for late join. A checkpoint restores state-machine identities, clip positions and changed variables through the same native loader used for object animations. It is not an engine memory dump and contains no pointers/string-table IDs.

### Prior open-source research inspected

- [CommonLibSSE-NG IAnimationGraphManagerHolder](https://github.com/CharmedBaryon/CommonLibSSE-NG/blob/main/include/RE/I/IAnimationGraphManagerHolder.h) and [BSAnimationGraphManager](https://github.com/CharmedBaryon/CommonLibSSE-NG/blob/main/include/RE/B/BSAnimationGraphManager.h), plus the local CommonLib headers under C:/Tools/ref/CommonLibSSE-NG: adopt NotifyAnimationGraph and retained graph-manager ownership/recursive locking. The current executable's bodies/vtables determine offsets and stream slots; old headers alone cannot establish this runtime's ABI or a complete save-state format.
- [OpenAnimationReplacer Hooks.h](https://github.com/ersh1/OpenAnimationReplacer/blob/main/src/Hooks.h) and [Hooks.cpp](https://github.com/ersh1/OpenAnimationReplacer/blob/main/src/Hooks.cpp), inspected in C:/Tools/ref/OpenAnimationReplacer/src: graph-holder Notify wrapper preserves the original result; clip hooks name Activate/Update/Deactivate slots. Adopt original-call preservation and completion observation in actual native updates. Reject animation replacement as the transport/restoration solution. The new hooks retain reference IDs only, not graph/node pointers across calls.

### Native evidence: SkyrimSE.exe 1.7.104

Queried through index/skx.py --typed and sk.py --typed; saved working extracts are under C:/Tools/skyrim_re/agent/world-anim-work. All addresses below are function VAs, not reflection metadata. Address Library IDs are from id_to_rva.tsv.

| Address Library ID | VA | Read conclusion / use |
|---|---|---|
| 56205 | 0x140A47370 | Papyrus PlayAnimation rejects actors and dispatches through REFR+0x38 graph holder. Actor-only capture of this entry cannot cover the wall. |
| 32901 | 0x140554220 | Holder NotifyAnimationGraph obtains a graph manager and calls manager input dispatch. |
| 63362 | 0x140BC0C90 | Manager input event dispatch serializes its graph iteration with the manager lock. Hook establishes duplicate-suppression scope for multiple graphs. |
| 63591 | 0x140BCF690 | Per-graph input event name lookup/dispatch and acceptance result. Hook copies the non-actor owner's ID before the original, then records accepted inputs in native dispatch order. |
| 63555 | 0x140BC9AA0 | Graph constructor writes its TESObjectREFR owner at +0x210; the SDK Actor* annotation does not restrict native furniture ownership. Construction records known graph references even before a party starts. |
| 19528 | 0x1402E0B10 | TESObjectREFR save uses animation change bit 28 and ExtraAnimation 0x41 / graph save path. |
| 20098 | 0x1402FC3E0 | Object animation save constructs AnimationStreamSaveGame and routes through holder/manager save; writes marker 0x2A after graph data. |
| 20099 | 0x1402FC5E0 | Object animation load constructs AnimationStreamLoadGame and routes to holder/manager load. |
| 63368 | 0x140BC1100 | Manager save writes graph count, graph type strings and per-graph save data under its recursive lock. |
| 63369 | 0x140BC1230 | Manager load reads count/type and calls the matching graph loader. Adapter rejects graph-count mismatch before entry. |
| 63601 | 0x140BD0690 | Graph serializer writes state machines, clips, variables, transition/generator state and queued graph events through typed virtual stream methods. |
| 63602 | 0x140BD1690 | Loader resets/restores native graph state, marks active-node work and evaluates through 63587 with zero delta. Use this path to restore saved progress/final pose; do not guess a universal terminal event. |
| 65148 | 0x140C3D8C0 | Save collector's true argument includes hkb clip time (+0xF0) and Gamebryo generator time (+0x68). Capture passes true. |
| 65149 | 0x140C3F3D0 | Saved-state restore traversal used by graph load. |
| 65164 | 0x140C40020 | Restore functor resolves state names/IDs, defaults missing state, clears existing transitions and bounds restored transition ranges by saved array size. |
| 63587 | 0x140BCEDB0 | Graph update runs node updates/internal graph events. TLS scope attributes completion/state changes to the current non-actor reference. |
| 59335 | 0x140AF2690 | State-machine update clears change byte +0x100; inspect it before original and compare state +0x80 / transition count +0xB8 afterward. |
| 59253 | 0x140AE8210 | Clip update changes atEnd byte +0x104. A change dirties a checkpoint, not each advancing frame. |
| 62932 | 0x140BAF650 | Gamebryo update advances time +0x68 before sequence callback; loop flag +0x6D determines one-shot completion crossing. |
| 32773 | 0x14054CA40 | Gamebryo callback updates retained Ni sequence time and returns sequence duration. Observe crossing only; preserve callback exactly once. |
| 63604 | 0x140BD2FF0 | Supplies graph type string used for save/load identity matching. |
| 8849 / 8850 | 0x1400DDD70 / 0x1400DDDA0 | Initialize BShkbAnimationGraph type and $-NoMoreVariables-$ sentinel, respectively. Validator requires both exact strings. |
| 69165 | 0x140EB1120 | BSFixedString::Set interns by value and releases prior content, used by network load-stream string callbacks. |

AnimationStreamSaveGame vtable 0x141829988: destructor, transform(48), quaternion(16), vector4(16), vector3(12), float(4), bool(1), int16, uint16, int32, uint32, string. AnimationStreamLoadGame vtable 0x141829910 adds a version slot after destructor and two string forms at the end. The transport mirrors these typed values, not the BGS save buffer's physical layout. LoadStream version 42 selects the modern >31 branch actually read in 63602; it is an adapter selection, not a claim that an entire save file has that version.

### Changes and invariants

| File(s) | Function/area | What and why |
|---|---|---|
| Code/client/Games/Skyrim/WorldAnimation.h/.cpp | Eligible, Capture, Apply and original-preserving hooks | Generic placed non-actor input capture, completion/change notification, and native graph save/load adapter. Uses retained manager + lock. No actor graph/quest fragment/chunk physics writes. Enable-parent children may animate their own graph without weakening scalar enable-parent authority. |
| Code/client/Services/WorldStateService.h and Services/Generic/WorldStateService.cpp | AnimationEvent/Dirty/Known, PumpAnimations, Publish, Pump, OnUpdate | Separate FIFO observations, dirty/known graph ID sets, budgeted checkpoints and session seed, native main-phase apply. Never coalesce input events. Failed network sends remain ahead of concurrently queued newer revisions. Snapshot cannot publish ahead of an unprocessed input for the same ref. |
| Code/encoding/Messages/WorldState.h/.cpp | Kind, Valid, Serialize/Deserialize, equality | Append AnimationEvent/AnimationSnapshot kinds; preserve old kind numbers and old message layouts. Snapshot schema version 1, maximum 32768 bytes; only snapshot adds payload. Same changed protocol required on all peers. |
| Code/encoding/Messages/WorldAnimationData.h | Reader, Valid | Typed value wire grammar with bounded strings/counts/nested arrays, exact graph/sentinel identity, complete consumption and truncation rejection before native loading. Never hand unchecked lengths/counts to native allocation loops. |
| Code/encoding/Messages/WorldAnimationReplay.h | Receive, Attach, Take, Applied, Failed | Per-ref input FIFO; latest durable native checkpoint; timed retry of failed head; explicit reload applies checkpoint then only newer inputs. Normal live checkpoint participates in sequence order, including when the initial cell reply was empty. Cursor avoids rescanning already applied input history. |
| Code/encoding/Messages/WorldStateReplay.h | Existing replay integration | Route animation kinds to separate queue with at most eight native animation applications per main phase. Existing scalar coalescing, ordering and unsupported adapters retained. |
| Code/encoding/Messages/WorldStateTable.h; Code/server/Services/WorldStateService.cpp | Snapshot and OnCell | Reuse epoch/leader authority and reliable delivery. Cell replay includes durable animation checkpoint with restore marker, excludes historical input command. No wall-specific server behavior. |
| Code/tests/world_animation_fixture.h; world_animation_protocol.cpp | Synthetic fixture, compile-time grammar checks, seven Catch cases | Ordered Open/Reset/Open, failed-head retry, duplicate/stale rejection, bounded/truncated wire data, five/ten-member fanout, leader/epoch transitions, late-join empty reply and reload checkpoint order. Fixture is synthetic, not a captured wall pose. |
| Code/tests/world_state_encoding.cpp | Existing every-kind fixture helper | Supply valid animation fixtures so the existing all-kind protocol coverage remains applicable. |
| Code/tests/check_world_animation_syntax.py | MSVC /Zs runner | Checks nine affected translation units without xmake, object compilation, linking or deployment. Compile-time parser assertions run under /Zs; runtime Catch cases do not. |
| Tools/InGameTests/Compare-WorldAnimation.py; Code/tests/world_animation_comparison_test.py | Existing capture checker, four unit tests | Require all eleven distinct moving chunks, finite XYZ/scale, default <=0.05 position units and <=0.0001 scale error. Missing/duplicate data fail. Root/stage agreement cannot substitute for chunks. Reads existing harness output; adds no live probe. |

### Cost and boundaries

Let P be party members, R registered/changed non-actor refs, Q pending inputs, S saved graph size, and N saved native node count. Actor count A and body count B are not traversed. Hook work is O(1) on unchanged native updates; a change/input costs O(log R) plus copying the bounded event name. Main phase seeds at most eight known IDs on authority change, drains at most 32 input observations and handles eight dirty graph IDs; each save/load is O(N+S), admitted at <=1024 active nodes, <=16 graphs and <=32768 wire bytes. Followers apply at most eight animation operations per phase, with map/ready-queue O(log R), cursor lookup O(log Q), and bounded 64-entry history cleanup per operation. Host fanout is O(P) per transmitted change, not O(P squared); there is no per-player actor/body scan. Event-triggered serialization/queue allocations remain; unchanged animations do not allocate checkpoints every frame. Existing rubble baseline/cell-header reconciliation was not replaced or expanded by this job.

Material limits requiring follow-up/qualification: the existing WorldState GameId path addresses placed plugin references, not arbitrary FF runtime-created refs; this patch preserves that boundary. Direct Ni-only PlayGamebryoAnimation without a BShkb graph is outside this graph adapter. Graphs over the admitted size/count limits are refused. A reference never loaded/registered on the authority cannot seed graph bytes from an old save while it remains unloaded; checkpoints captured earlier in the current session remain on the server for late joins. A failed host capture waits for the next attachment/state notification. Native save/load ABI, zero-delta child/collision propagation, unusual asset graphs, session/load races and hook compatibility still need changed-build observation. No five-player performance claim follows from protocol test shape or these theoretical budgets. Queues retain ordered events until checkpoint/application permits pruning; sustained input overload is not runtime profiled.

### Towerwall round 1: independent refutation disposition, 2026-09-27

Read `C:/Tools/skyrim_re/agent/conductor/towerwall-verify0.astra.md` completely. **Conclusion: the refutation of universal task completion stands.** The native graph-event/native-checkpoint mechanism is supported, and the older deployed implementation achieved sampled wall-position parity. Neither that capture nor source inspection establishes final-source collision restoration, successful passage, late join, reload, or universal animated-reference coverage. This revision repairs specific processing, history, semantic-validation and session-lifetime defects; it does not turn those missing observations into a pass. Confidence is HIGH for the native mechanism and historical position result, MEDIUM for the unexercised revisions, and UNVERIFIED for final-build gameplay acceptance. No new probe, xmake build, object/link build, deployment, commit or game session was performed. COMMON.md waives new independent reviewers for this worker; no fresh A/B review is claimed.

Primary-source recheck: searched the web for CommonLib variable metadata and inspected the local sources corresponding to [hkbBehaviorGraphData.h](https://github.com/CharmedBaryon/CommonLibSSE-NG/blob/main/include/RE/H/hkbBehaviorGraphData.h), [hkbVariableInfo.h](https://github.com/CharmedBaryon/CommonLibSSE-NG/blob/main/include/RE/H/hkbVariableInfo.h), and [hkbVariableValueSet.h](https://github.com/CharmedBaryon/CommonLibSSE-NG/blob/main/include/RE/H/hkbVariableValueSet.h). Adopt the distinction between word variables (types 0..4), pointer variables (5), and quad variables (6..8), with word slots holding quad indices. Reject blindly trusting a wire bool to select a native write. The native executable, rather than a header alone, supplies the project DB lookup and loader offsets.

Fresh executable reads and their implications:

| Address Library ID / function VA | Evidence and decision |
|---|---|
| 63635 / `0x140BD5300` | Serializer iterates project DB +0x78 name/index hash table (capacity +0x7C, entries +0x98, stride 24). It reads variable type at behavior data +0x20, index*6+4. Adopt that exact name/index mapping rather than assuming asset string-array order. |
| 63602 / `0x140BD1690` | Loader first resets default values, then trusts the incoming word/quad tag. Its quad path resolves a project DB index, reads that word as a quad index, and writes four words without a type check. Confirms the reviewer's concrete corruption risk; the new preflight runs BEFORE this function and checks both default/current word arrays and quad indices, then every incoming variable name and category. Graph-count matching alone is insufficient. |
| 65148 / `0x140C3D8C0`, called by 63601 / `0x140BD0690` | Collector traverses graph node records and state-machine transition arrays and allocates saved structures before stream-size rejection. **The previous 32-KiB wording was not a bound on native capture work.** The 1024-node admission limit remains a partial guard, not a proof of bounded transition arrays, helper traversal, native allocation or execution time. No complete preflight or five-player measurement is claimed. |
| 56207 / `0x140A47580` | Direct PlayGamebryoAnimation finds a NiControllerManager, activates a sequence, and marks animation changed. It need not dispatch a BShkb graph input. Therefore the graph hook does not universally cover Ni-only objects. Do not fake coverage by treating a sequence label as saved graph state. |
| 20099 / `0x1402FC5E0` | Object load tries the graph-holder stream and has a distinct Ni-controller fallback. This supports native restoration as a domain-specific approach; the current network adapter implements only the graph stream. It does not read arbitrary old-save changeforms for references never registered on the host. |

Disposition of EVERY review point:

1. **Unbounded processing loops: fixed at the cited sites, not a claim of a globally bounded engine path.** `WorldStateReplay::Drain` moves at most 64 messages and 128 KiB (accounted as object size plus string/payload size) per invocation. Client OnUpdate/Pump use it for outgoing/incoming mailboxes and retain FIFO tails; at most four cell requests leave per update. Existing failed-send requeue ordering remains. Server OnCell now queues a cursor; OnUpdate services one four-reference page globally in round-robin order, preserving per-reference sequence order and excluding historical animation commands. At most eight party entries are reconciled per update. Every page rechecks player, party, leader and epoch; a replacement request from a different authority replaces stale pending work. Scope replay is spread over frames rather than materialized in full. Producer queues and transport callback rate still lack a total storage/admission bound under sustained overload. Session-reset destruction and native save/load are not constant-time operations. Existing scalar baseline/cell-header reconciliation was preserved.
2. **Wire cap versus native capture cap: reviewer is correct.** Corrected the cost claim as above. A complete collector preflight or a streaming native collector redesign remains required for a hard capture-work/allocation bound. Raising/removing the wire cap would only hide this limitation; no such workaround was applied.
3. **Unbounded per-reference event history: fixed with explicit failure semantics.** `WorldAnimationReplay` retains at most 256 events per reference. Overflow clears that bounded history, records the highest uncovered revision, stops application, and emits `World anim: <ref> history overflow; synchronization incomplete until a covering checkpoint`. Further events advance the gap; only a checkpoint covering it resumes via a restore followed by newer inputs. Thus overload is a visible loss of synchronization, NOT a claim that all dropped events were applied. Normal, non-overflow FIFO ordering is unchanged. Eight animation references are examined per follower phase for outside-scope eviction, including unavailable references; re-entry relies on existing cell replay. Storage in this policy is O(R*(256*event size + 2*S)); this does not bound upstream mailboxes. Refusing checkpoints forever leaves that reference explicitly incomplete rather than retaining an unlimited history.
4. **Final source versus successful capture: accepted, no attribution laundering.** The capture logs say `end state`; current source says `graph snapshot`. Existing TPTests.exe is timestamped 2026-09-27 03:11:51, SHA-256 `597a67c9bd9157bdaa94ecd96687653e06ce86d680fa8bb3edd4358b268040b4`, and contains five animation cases. Current source has eleven. The review records a pre-revision WorldAnimation.cpp edit at 03:25. Neither old executable validates this revision.
5. **Universal coverage: accepted and still incomplete.** FF references need stable cross-peer creation/identity mapping, not removal of the form-ID check; literal runtime IDs are not a shared identity. Ni-only playback uses a distinct engine path, as read above. Oversized graphs remain rejected; native collection itself lacks a complete cost guard. Never-loaded host-save references have no graph to capture and no previously retained checkpoint in this session. These are unsupported cases, not successful silent fallbacks. No quest/form-specific repair or host quest-fragment execution was added.
6. **Late join, real reload, collision and passage: accepted as unproved for current source.** Older final chunk positions are evidence only for sampled XYZ. Raw probes are non-atomic and omit scale, rotation and rigid-body transforms; `collision:true` denotes a collision object, not world membership or effective collision shape. Both PCs failed the actual jump in the qualifying run. No source argument can convert that result into a passage pass or attribute the obstruction to this patch.
7. **Thread/crash/semantic safety: the specific variable-write hole is addressed; general safety remains unproved.** `CompatibleVariables` holds the retained manager's recursive lock, admits at most 1024 variable slots per graph and 4096 project-table slots total, rejects missing/unknown/pointer/category-mismatched incoming variables, and checks current/default quad indices before native load. The pure wire validator now accepts a destination predicate. Compile-time fixtures exercise compatible, wrong-category and unknown-name rejection; these do not execute native pointer reads or establish whole-asset compatibility. State/generator compatibility, engine lock scheduling and collision propagation still require final-build validation.
8. **Disconnect/same-epoch/authority cleanup: implemented explicitly.** OnDisconnected closes capture and clears static animation event/pending/dirty sets plus attach/scalar observations and both mailboxes. A local connection generation forces main-phase replay reset even if reconnection reuses the same epoch and leader. Authority changes clear old incoming/outgoing work and pending animation observations; a capture serial rejects hook enqueues that began before that boundary. Known graph IDs remain a registry for seeding, not retained pointers/events. Added same-epoch replay-reset coverage in test source, but neither the static-queue/thread race nor that new runtime test has been exercised in a newly linked executable.
9. **Five/ten-player cost and regression claims: reviewer is correct.** For one S-byte checkpoint, payload fanout remains approximately (P-1)*S: 4S at P=5, 9S at P=10, excluding framing. Hooks execute on evaluated native graphs/nodes, including actor fast rejection; that overhead is not independent of engine animation activity. No blanket regression certificate follows from older tests. Existing lock/door/rubble and pre-25fa030d actor/corpse/cart behaviors were not intentionally removed, but only a final paired run can validate their interaction with this revision.

Eleven animation cases now exist. |

Corrected costs: let P be players, R animation references, Q queued commands, S <=32768 saved bytes, H=256 history entries, V<=4096 inspected destination hash slots, and N<=1024 admitted graph-node records. Mailbox work is <=64 messages/128 KiB per phase; replay applies <=8 operations; scope eviction visits <=8 references with <=H events each; server cell replay visits <=4 references, <=7 durable kinds each, per update globally. Map operations add logarithmic R/Q factors; schema assembly is O(V log V) plus O(S log V), with at most 32768 additional default/current variable-index checks (16 graphs * 1024 variables * two arrays), allocated per snapshot, not per actor/frame. Captures still perform native O(N + transitions + saved generator/track state + strings) work with nested quantities not all preflight-bounded. Host/follower event mailboxes may grow with overload. No actor/body survey was added, but hooks incur fast-path work as native actors/graphs/nodes update. There is no measured five/ten-player CPU/memory/bandwidth result.

## Harness2 review round 1: acceptance and jump-state correction (2026-09-27)

invoked here: COMMON.md explicitly exempts this worker. Earlier reviews are historical.

Primary reference searched on the web and inspected locally: [CommonLibSSE-NG
bhkCharacterController](https://github.com/alandtse/CommonLibSSE-NG/blob/a898f469851c464d05137bb74b069dd234897643/include/RE/B/bhkCharacterController.h),
[hkpCharacterContext](https://github.com/alandtse/CommonLibSSE-NG/blob/a898f469851c464d05137bb74b069dd234897643/include/RE/H/hkpCharacterContext.h),
and [hkpCharacterState](https://github.com/alandtse/CommonLibSSE-NG/blob/a898f469851c464d05137bb74b069dd234897643/include/RE/H/hkpCharacterState.h).
Adopt their distinction between eligibility flags, requested state and current
state, after checking the actual executable. Reject inferring airborne/ground
contact by negating a compound CanJump predicate. Reject using a header comment
alone: this snapshot's wantState comment repeats 218, whereas native code proves 21C.

Native source inspected before changing the driver:

- AL42423 / VA1407B3150: native JumpHandler calls AL37257 / VA140675110 with the player.
- AL37257 / VA140675110: resolves actor/controller, checks actor flag +204 bit3,
  submits player action through AL42350 / VA1407AE440, and only conditionally calls
  AL78262 / VA1410645E0. Calling the void wrapper does not establish action acceptance.
- AL78262 / VA1410645E0: writes requested state 1 at controller+21C and jump height
  at +23C. Immediate readback now names an absent request without assigning a cause.
- AL62427 / VA140B97880 reads context+20; the controller embeds context at +1E0.
- AL78280 / VA141065F00 transfers requested state+21C to current state+200 and resets
  request to 11. AL80102 / VA1410C8030 (Jumping) sets flag 0x2000, computes vertical
  velocity, requests state2 and clears support bit0x100. It does NOT clear CanJump
  bit0x400. AL80106 / VA1410C82D0 (OnGround) separately checks support and requests
  transitions. Adopt current states0/1/2 for the bounded jump driver's observations;
  retain target-distance qualification. This is controller-state evidence, not a
  physical contact manifold or collision-shape sweep. The timeout now starts at
  actual jump submission, not at the preceding runup. Runtime effectiveness remains
  untested. Neither old captures nor this source isolate rejection vs obstruction
  for the historical run, because requested/current states were not captured there.
- AL25066 / VA1403D8590: locked quest alias handle lookup. Adopt a bounded native
  alias watcher rather than an all-actor discovery scan. esm.sqlite MQ101/3372B

**Disagreement resolved: verify1's minor snapshot edge is impossible under Receive's invariant.** Let S be the retained snapshot sequence. The event branch rejects E <= S *before* assigning Received or Gap. Every transition from Gap=0 to Gap=E therefore has E>S; further gap events have the same guard. A snapshot below Gap is rejected without changing S or Gap; an accepted snapshot covering Gap clears Gap. Hence whenever Gap exists, Gap>S. Any covering checkpoint C>=Gap necessarily has C>S and cannot be rejected by `C<S`. No weakened monotonic check is needed. Added a source comment and a protocol case with S=500, gap=757, older/equal events rejected, non-covering checkpoints rejected, exact-covering checkpoint accepted. The proof comes from the production branches; the new runtime case is only type-checked this session.

**Producer admission fixed in source:** `WorldStateReplay.h:11` defines 256 incoming/outgoing mailbox entries, 4096 coalesced scalar observations, and 4096 pending animation inputs. All four producer insertion sites use the cap under their existing locks. Existing observation keys may still replace/coalesce at capacity. FIFO animation events never coalesce. `CanAdmit` uses subtraction after checking reserved<maximum, so SIZE_MAX inputs cannot wrap the capacity arithmetic.

`WorldStateService::OnUpdate` reserves every drained outgoing entry in `m_outgoingInFlight` while Send runs. Publish checks queued+reserved, so failed-send reinsertion cannot overrun the 256-entry mailbox. Requeue occurs only for the same Authority (including connection generation). Publish rechecks that generation under the mailbox lock; observation producers now also check the capture serial. Authority changes clear scalar observations/attach work as well as animation queues. These additional guards prevent work begun at an old boundary from contaminating the new connection.

Overflow latches the first queue name. Producers stop admitting data; Pump stops normal replay at its phase guard. OnUpdate logs `World state queue overflow: <queue>; synchronization incomplete, disconnecting` and calls the existing TransportService Close outside both locks. Source checked: `Libraries/TiltedConnect/Code/connect/src/Client.cpp:150` invalidates the connection then synchronously calls OnDisconnected; `Code/client/Services/Generic/TransportService.cpp:227` triggers DisconnectedEvent. WorldStateService's existing cleanup clears queues, reservations and the latch. Already executing bounded prefixes can finish before the close; no dropped suffix is treated as a healthy reliable stream. This is explicit overload failure, NOT successful synchronization or overload recovery. A large legitimate burst can now disconnect and needs sizing validation.

Changed files this round: `Code/encoding/Messages/WorldStateReplay.h` (admission policy); `Code/client/Services/WorldStateService.h` (in-flight reservation); `Code/client/Services/Generic/WorldStateService.cpp` (caps, overload close, serial/generation cleanup); `Code/encoding/Messages/WorldAnimationReplay.h` (proof comment only); `Code/tests/world_animation_protocol.cpp` (two cases and compile-time bounds); this research appendix and `round6/report_towerwall.md`. No engine-offset/hook/stream changes this round.

Costs and risks: the four queued populations cannot grow with sustained overload. With S<=32768 and event text<=128, each mailbox holds at most 256*(sizeof(WorldState)+S+128) logical bytes, plus container/allocator overhead; outgoing reservation is INCLUDED in 256, not an additional 64. A drained incoming batch adds <=128 KiB outside the queue. Send/retry scratch can duplicate the bounded outgoing batch and per-message payloads; 256 counts admitted messages, not every transient copy. Observations/animation inputs hold <=4096*(sizeof(WorldState)+128) logical bytes each; bounded drained batches are additional. Heap capacities and native/transport allocation are not byte-exact under this accounting. Admission adds O(1) queue checks and O(log Q) existing map lookup, no scans, no loop over players P, actors A or bodies B. Four producer caps are independent of P; server fanout still costs O(P*S), 4S/9S at P=5/10. Retained reference registries/latest-state maps remain O(R), not a fixed global service-memory bound. Native collection retains O(N+T+other nested saved state), with T and helpers unbounded by present preflight. The previously added hooks still execute on native graph/node evaluation, including actor fast rejection. Closing an overloaded connection affects the party and is an intentional visible failure, requiring runtime validation, not a claimed performance solution.

Next qualifying build/run must execute all thirteen animation cases and the world-state suite, bind hashes, and show `World anim: 6CF54 Open -> follower (<sequence>)` plus ordered `graph snapshot` entries. Logs alone do not certify transforms. Compare eleven moving chunks' positions, rotations and bodies; require collision-enabled passage, join after completion, real-door exit/re-entry, same-epoch reconnect and authority transfer. An overflow line is a failed session, never a pass. FF/Ni-only/oversized/never-loaded-save support and complete native capture bounds require implementation, not simply another test. This report records the negative result rather than claiming those accepted objections were fixed.

## 2026-09-27 scale1 review revision: narrowed indexes, changed-only loot targets, due-only telemetry

Supersedes the earlier scale1 completion/performance wording. Full point-by-point disposition and cost table: `C:/Users/mwalt/.claude/jobs/77da3be6/tmp/round6/report_scale1.md`. No commit, xmake, deployment, or changed-build in-game test. COMMON explicitly waives additional Reviewer A/B work; no additional review is claimed.

Primary research: web search for Skyrim Together transport and EnTT lifecycle/thread safety; inspected installed **EnTT 3.10.0** `entity/view.hpp`, `sparse_set.hpp`, `sigh_storage_mixin.hpp`, repository TiltedConnect Client/Server/Packet sources, and local CommonLibSSE-NG `src/RE/A/Actor.cpp:157,967` and `src/RE/P/ProcessLists.cpp:45`. Online sources: [CommonLib Actor.cpp](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/alandtse/CommonLibVR/ng/src/RE/A/Actor.cpp), [EnTT entity-component documentation](https://github.com/skypjack/entt/wiki/Entity-Component-System), [EnTT signal storage 3.10.0](https://github.com/skypjack/entt/blob/v3.10.0/src/entt/entity/sigh_storage_mixin.hpp). The installed 3.10 view's handle() returns a reference, unlike later versions; code/tests use the installed dependency.

Adopted/rejected approaches:

- Adopt candidate-only duplicate resolution: the leading EnTT pool iterates backwards, so the matching entity with greatest pool index is the old view's first result. O(D), not a full O(A) fallback, and unchanged when the leading pool changes or swaps an entity. 10,000 actual-EnTT differential mutations pass. Movement has no wire epoch; retain its old selected entity, use the cached composite ownership key, and never read RemoteComponent between the two lookups. No new packet rejection rule.
- Reject using lifecycle indexes from native token readers without a registry-wide threading design. This task's changes to FindEntityByServerId/GetLocalOwnershipToken/GetRemoteOwnershipToken are withdrawn to their exact 25fa030d bodies. Only movement/projectile runner handlers use the four remaining indexes. Protect their new containers with mutexes and initialize before service construction. Two existing RemoteComponent epoch writes use patch(), no other direct component epoch writer found. The original wider O(1) utility claim is withdrawn. Native-token registry access remains a baseline limitation.
- Transport is synchronously pumped: World Update -> Transport HandleUpdate -> Client Update -> HandleMessage -> OnConsume -> dispatcher. ID53926 / VA **0x1409D7450** is the native VM entry and has main-loop, worker and freeze/update callers; it does not establish an OS-main-thread assumption. No separate transport receive thread was found for this path. Native projectile events are queued through Runner. Reject a global lock around arbitrary engine/native callbacks as an unproven deadlock-prone redesign.
- Adopt NPC target lifecycle invalidation, including the excluded Player and WaitingForAssignment components. Rebuild only when dirty on the original 250ms boundary; keep observation/query cadence, iteration order, send order, and snapshot retries. Disconnect, load, ownership transfer also invalidate. No native root/process readiness is cached beyond the original behavior.
- Adopt numeric earliest-due telemetry, with updates under the existing target lock. Never use diagnostics to alter target/body membership or steering. Prune flags cover every publish/clear and pointer/UID change; the solver checks retained StepTarget world and UID. FollowProbe contains telemetry, not the target pose. Test against the reviewed ungated loop preserves report timing/counters over 1,000 frames. O(1) clean-frame gate, O(B) due/dirty collection; steering remains at its original cadence.
- Adopt shared nesting-depth protection for server scratch buffers: nested sends temporarily use the default allocator. Test real production Server::Send + actual Snappy against uncached sends, including varying baseline payload content. Reject the old fake compression oracle. Broadcast copies still cost O(G*S); no cross-baseline or cross-message cache, no sharing of recipient-specific movement payloads.
- Keep discovery census and add/remove order; replace its flag-reset scan with generation stamps. Tests compare exact 25fa030d results for 1,000 randomized censuses including stamp wrap. Native root/process/reconciliation state can change without ECS membership changes; no proven complete event replacement was established. Do not defer discovery work under a cursor and claim identical timing.
- Keep combat/aggro order and awareness pairs. ID38571 / **0x1406C9F60** delegates to **0x140818C50**, which read-locks and searches live combat-group target membership. Native StartCombat **0x1406C93B0** mutates controller/group state, with Papyrus **0x140A03090** routing through **0x140668EB0**. A cached party-opponent result across a StartCombat call is not proven equivalent. ID41408 / **0x140786350** and its get-state callee ID37758 / **0x1406A2300** establish per-target max/LOS semantics. Reject removing pairs, changing cadence, or a bounded combat cursor, also rejected by the preceding loop-audit review. O(P^2*C) combat checks and O(T*C) awareness remain. The exact combat body compares equal to 25fa030d after stripping read-only timing blocks.

### scale1 round 3: reusable observer storage and actionable lane evidence (2026-09-27)

Read COMMON, the original seven-item task, LOOP_AUDIT and its review, the complete `muse_review_scale1_r2.log`, EVIDENCE and the named paired capture artifacts. No independent review is claimed: COMMON explicitly waives the local Reviewer A/B requirement. The review's residual registry-safety and whole-scan cost objections are not disproved. Primary research: searched for open-source Skyrim Together discovery and EnTT threading implementations; inspected [CommonLib ProcessLists.cpp](https://github.com/CharmedBaryon/CommonLibSSE-NG/blob/main/src/RE/P/ProcessLists.cpp) online and the local `C:/Tools/ref/CommonLibSSE-NG/src/RE/P/ProcessLists.cpp:45` implementation. Its high-actor walk and AL400315 singleton corroborate the snapshot population, not a complete lifecycle journal. Inspected [EnTT storage source](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/skypjack/entt/v3.10.3/src/entt/entity/storage.hpp) and the installed 3.10.0 signal/view implementation used by our tests; [the maintainer's view-threading explanation](https://github.com/skypjack/entt/discussions/1253) explicitly rules out assuming registry/view thread safety. Adopt capacity reuse with lexical ownership; reject storing a mutable shared live observer vector, truncating native observers, or interpreting an index mutex as registry synchronization.

`PlayerCombat::ObserverSnapshot` borrows empty thread-local vector capacity by swap, collects the same filtered handles in the same order, and clears the vector before returning its capacity. Every invocation owns its live vector, including nested invocations. Combat and stealth still collect at their existing call sites; stealth remains lazy at the first eligible track and reuses that snapshot for the remaining tracks. They do not share a stale actor list across services or ticks. The public allocating `Observers()` remains available. With stable population and nonnested calls, the snapshot vector no longer allocates on each tick after warming; growth/new threads/nesting can still allocate. Retained memory is O(H_max) per executing thread, not O(P). Collection remains O(H) and awareness O(T*C), with C<=H and T normally <=P-1; B is not traversed. There is deliberately no fixed actor cap that would drop baseline observers. This removes the identified steady allocation cause, not the remaining full traversal.

Exact-runtime evidence, freshly queried with Python `sk.py fn id:<id>` and `callers id:<id>`, is saved in `round6/scale1_r3_corpus/`:

Caching these results across StartCombat or deferring pairs is not established equivalent. Combat body still compares with 25fa030d after normalizing only timing blocks and the observer-storage declaration. The task explicitly prohibits timing, batching and combat-order changes; worst-case O(P^2*C) remains an unmet blanket scale limit.

None emits our ECS lifecycle signals. The production NPC OnMainFrame test changes root/cell with the target cache clean and observes the same 250ms observation and 1s query-retry boundaries. Dirty-only native polling is not equivalent. Native O(T) at 4Hz and discovery O(H+A) per eligible PreUpdate remain; no speculative lifecycle hooks or cadence changes were added.

AL53930 / **0x1409D8600** and AL54006 / **0x1409E0CA0** call it from draining paths; internal locks do not enclose our pre-original World update. Outside reads and signal callbacks now have independent eight-log budgets; a burst of reads can no longer consume all writer evidence. `Scale lane: outside ... mutation=... index=...` identifies the concrete template/component set. `Scale lane: writer-stack operation=... entity=... thread=... frames=... pcs=[...]` records up to 12 PCs on the first eight outside signal observations. `Scale lane: summary ... outside-reads=... outside-writes=...` preserves exposure counts.
